# HEP → CLU Primers
### Particle-physics concepts for AI experts, rebuilt inside the Causal Learning Unit

> **Who this is for.** You are an ML/AI expert. You know linear algebra, optimization, backprop, RNNs, energy-based models, and the NTK. Your physics is "basic particle physics": particles exist, $E=mc^2$, forces come from fields. This document rebuilds every physics concept the CLU program uses — mass, charge, symmetry, temperature, boosts, horizons — **as statements about a learning system**, walks through all the math we actually rely on, and tags each claim's epistemic status.
>
> **How to read.** Each concept gets four blocks: **Philosophy** (the idea in plain terms), **Look-up** (the standard physics name, so you can go deeper later — we deliberately do *not* re-teach standard HEP material), **Math** (the full derivation as used in our results), and **→ CLU** (what it means for our primitive, which program idea it feeds). Parts build on each other; read in order the first time.
>
> **Provenance.** Notation and claims are imported from the CLU formalism note (F5, `.claude/outputs/formalism-note.md`); every number quoted comes from the verification suite `.claude/scratch/formalism-note/checks.py` (numpy float64, machine-precision Jacobians, fixed seeds). Status tags: **[proven]** derived, **[verified]** additionally confirmed numerically, **[evidenced]** standard-argument-supported, **[conjectured]** falsifiable guess, **[design hypothesis]** engineering bet.
>
> **How the program validates a claim (three procedural layers, all born from its own failures — new wave-14).** A tag above says *what kind* of evidence backs a claim; these three rules govern *how* a quantitative result earns the right to be quoted. **(1) Flag-provenance** — every quantitative result carries a table of commit hash, seeds, and all non-default config flags (governs *tables*). **(2) Pre-registration** (mandatory since 2026-07-10 for any acceptance criterion that is a *measured ratio, exponent, slope, or law*) — write the predicted value *and its derivation* **before** running the harness that measures it (governs *predictions and asides*, which flag-provenance does not — this is exactly the hole a wrong "≈13.9× vault" fell through, later rejected `8.11×`, and it caught a mislabelled R5 residual coefficient before that harness ran). **(3) Reconciliation-list owner** — a report whose findings force downstream changes names that list in its first ten lines, and the Hub converts it into an explicit owner-task (governs *cross-document consistency*, the gap through which a retracted "2.6" stayed live for two waves). *Corollary the program learned the hard way (§5.3, §10.3, the ledger's w13/w14 addenda): **instrument validity precedes inference** — a negative is only as valid as the sampler/estimator that measured it, and that validity is a **separate** empirical question to settle **before** the inference, not after.*
>
> **The one-line program context.** The CLU (Causal Learning Unit, introduced as CHLU) is a recurrent primitive whose update rule is *physics run forward*: the latent state is a physical system, learning shapes its energy landscape, and the physical levers — mass, friction, temperature, causal speed — become the interface of the primitive (program principles P1 "physics as a design library", P2 "primitive = lever interface", P3 "fix geometry, learn content").

## §0 The sixty-second map

### 0.1 What the CLU actually computes

The latent state is a pair $z = (q, p)$: **position** $q \in \mathbb{R}^d$ (the content — "where the thought is") and **momentum** $p \in \mathbb{R}^d$ (the change — "where it is heading"). A single scalar function, the **Hamiltonian** ("total energy"),

$$H(q,p) = T(p) + V_\theta(q)$$

drives everything: $T$ is kinetic energy (cost of motion; contains the learned masses and the speed limit $c$), $V_\theta$ is a **learned potential** (an energy-based model: low energy = plausible/known content, high energy = implausible/garbage). The forward pass is a numerical integration of the equations of motion, one step per tick, with an optional friction knob $\gamma$ and an optional noise knob $T$ (temperature). That's the whole unit. Everything in this document is about what that buys and what it costs.

### 0.2 Cast of characters, one breath each

| character | symbol | intuition inside the CLU |
|---|---|---|
| position | $q$ | the content of the latent state; where memories are read |
| momentum | $p$ | the state's velocity budget; carries "writes" to memory |
| Hamiltonian / energy | $H$ | the master scalar: plausibility + activity; also the confidence/anomaly signal |
| potential | $V_\theta(q)$ | the learned landscape: valleys = memories/attractors, hills = implausible states |
| **inertial mass** | $M_i$ | how much coordinate $i$ resists being changed — a learned per-coordinate stiffness of *response* (≈ inverse learning rate of the state) |
| **spectral mass** | $\mu_k$ | how strongly mode $k$ is pulled back to its attractor — curvature ÷ inertia; sets its forgetting rate. **Not the same thing as $M$; they run in opposite directions** |
| rest mass | $m_0$ | the idle-energy floor of the unit; sets how big the "Newtonian" (gentle) regime is |
| causal speed | $c$ | hard cap on how fast latent content can move; the "light cone" of the unit |
| friction | $\gamma$ | fraction of momentum removed per step; the only forgetting knob |
| temperature | $T$ | how much random exploration the noise injects (paired to friction by a theorem) |
| **charge** | $Q_X$ | the bookkeeping number a symmetry forces to be constant; in the CLU: the *write current* of a memory channel |
| rapidity | $\zeta$ | the "angle" of a boost/squeeze — how hard we re-frame the state at inference |
| decay constant | $f$ | radius/stiffness of a memory manifold (how big the storage dial is) |

### 0.3 The dictionary at a glance (details in the named sections)

| physics | look-up name | CLU meaning | where |
|---|---|---|---|
| conservation of phase-space volume | Liouville's theorem | no silent information destruction in the forward pass | §1.4 |
| symplectic structure | symplectic form, $Sp(2d)$ | the exact invariant our integrator preserves; source of gradient-pairing guarantees | §1.5 |
| leapfrog integration | Störmer–Verlet, geometric integration | the forward pass itself | §1.6 |
| modified/shadow energy | backward error analysis | "the discrete network exactly conserves a slightly different energy" | §1.7 |
| damped mechanics | conformal symplectic systems | friction = uniform, exactly-known volume leak | §1.8 |
| inertia | Newton's second law | per-coordinate learned learning-rate of the state | §2.1 |
| speed of light, rest energy | special relativity, light cone | anisotropic slew-rate cap $c/\sqrt{M_i}$; built-in "gradient clipping" | §2.2 |
| particle mass (HEP sense) | mass term, Klein–Gordon equation | curvature of the landscape in canonical units = forgetting rate | §2.3 |
| harmonic oscillator | normal modes | the exactly-solvable cell all memory analysis reduces to | §3 |
| Noether's theorem | symmetry ↔ conservation | equivariance of $V_\theta$ ⇒ a protected memory channel | §4.2 |
| spontaneous symmetry breaking | SSB, Goldstone boson, vacuum manifold | flat directions = dissipation-proof memory registers | §4.3–4.5 |
| pion mass from quark mass | pseudo-Goldstone, GMOR relation | imperfect symmetry ⇒ finite-but-long memory, lifetime $\propto 1/\mu^2$ | §4.5 |
| coset construction | nonlinear sigma model, HEFT vs SMEFT | two ways to parameterize a memory manifold | §4.7 |
| effective field theory | EFT, integrating out, Wilson coefficients | principled coarse-graining of fast modes; "EFT of memory" | §4.8 |

| black hole horizon | event horizon, Hawking radiation, Lindblad equation | localized forgetting region $\gamma(q)$ with exact volume accounting | §7 |

# Part I — Mechanics as a computational substrate

## 1.1 Why momentum is part of the state

**Philosophy.** An RNN carries one vector $h_t$ and learns a black-box map $h_{t+1} = f_\theta(h_t, x_t)$. Mechanics splits the state into *content* $q$ and *rate of change* $p$, and derives the update from a single scalar $H(q,p)$ instead of learning an arbitrary map. You already believe in this split: **momentum-SGD is exactly this structure applied to weights.** The heavy-ball optimizer

$$v_{t+1} = \beta v_t - \eta \nabla L(x_t), \qquad x_{t+1} = x_t + v_{t+1}$$

*is* damped mechanics: $L$ is the potential, $v$ the momentum, $\beta$ the friction ($\beta = 1-\gamma$), and the effective mass is $m = \varepsilon^2/\eta$ (derivation in §2.1). The CLU move is: take the machinery your optimizer already uses on *weights*, and make it the *model itself*, acting on latents — with the conservative limit ($\gamma = 0$: no forgetting, infinite-horizon stability) available as a design point that no optimizer wants but a *memory* does.

**Look-up.** Hamiltonian mechanics; phase space; (ML side) Polyak heavy ball, Nesterov momentum.

**→ CLU.** This is the origin story of the primitive (handover §1.1) and why "the latent is a physical system" is not decoration: every guarantee below is inherited from this structural choice.

## 1.2 The Hamiltonian and its equations

**Philosophy.** $H(q,p)$ is a *budget function*: total energy = activity ($T$) + implausibility ($V$). The dynamics is the unique flow that moves the state as fast as possible *while exactly conserving the budget*:

$$\dot q = \frac{\partial H}{\partial p}, \qquad \dot p = -\frac{\partial H}{\partial q}.$$

Read them as: "content moves in the direction momentum points" and "momentum feels a force downhill in the landscape."

**Math — the one-line conservation proof.** Along a trajectory,

$$\frac{dH}{dt} = \nabla_q H \cdot \dot q + \nabla_p H\cdot \dot p = \nabla_qH\cdot\nabla_pH - \nabla_pH\cdot\nabla_qH = 0.$$

Energy conservation is an algebraic identity of the flow, not a property you train for. This is what the CHLU paper means by "energy conservation as a structural prior."

**Separability (a load-bearing assumption).** Our $H = T(p) + V_\theta(q)$ has no mixed $q,p$ terms. Every exactness claim in this document (symplecticity of the *explicit* integrator, exact Noether conservation, …) uses this. If a future variant couples $q$ and $p$ inside $H$ — position-dependent mass $M(q)$ (§4.7!), "magnetic" couplings, momentum-dependent attention — the integrator must be upgraded (implicit midpoint / constrained methods), or the guarantees silently die. **[proven; flagged as a standing scope guard]**

**→ CLU.** $V_\theta$ is trained contrastively (wake–sleep): push energy down on data, up on hallucinations — a classical energy-based model. The Hamiltonian wrapper is what turns that static EBM into a *dynamical memory* with conservation guarantees.

## 1.3 The learned potential: valleys are memories

**Philosophy.** $V_\theta(q)$ is an EBM over latent content. Attractors (local minima) = stored patterns; basins = generalization neighborhoods; barriers = discrimination. Three code instantiations (2-layer tanh MLP with a $0.05\lVert q\rVert^2$ confinement bowl; a deep swish MLP; a conv net for images — the latter two *omit* the confinement bowl, which costs them a boundedness assumption we flag in §3.4).

**→ CLU.** Everything the unit "knows" is geometry of $V_\theta$. The rest of this document is about how the *other* levers ($M, \gamma, c, T$) price, protect, and destroy that geometry. Per P3: geometry of the update rule is fixed; $V_\theta$ (and $M$, and later $\gamma_\phi(q)$) is where all content lives.

## 1.4 Phase-space volume: what "no information destruction" means

**Philosophy.** Take a small cloud of nearby states (an uncertainty blob) and push every point through the dynamics. If the map shrinks the cloud's volume, distinct pasts merge into indistinguishable futures — irreversible information loss (this is what dissipative systems, and NODEs pushed toward stability, do). If volume is exactly preserved, the map is invertible-in-bulk: the cloud can stretch and shear (that's computation) but never collapses. Hamiltonian flows preserve phase-space volume *exactly*.

**Look-up.** Liouville's theorem.

**→ CLU.** This is the resolution of the "discrete-and-unstable vs continuous-and-dissipative" dichotomy in the paper's thesis: LSTMs explode/vanish; NODEs buy stability by contraction (forgetting); a symplectic map is stable *without* contraction. Forgetting then becomes a *deliberate, metered* act (the $\gamma$ knob, §1.8, §3) instead of a side effect.

## 1.5 Symplecticity: the exact invariant (stronger than volume)

**Philosophy.** Volume preservation is the headline, but Hamiltonian flows preserve something finer: the **symplectic form** — the sum of signed areas of the cloud's shadows on each $(q_i, p_i)$ plane. In 2D (one coordinate), symplectic = area-preserving, full stop. In higher dimensions it is strictly stronger than volume preservation, and it is *this* structure that produces the singular-value pairing law for gradients (§1.8).

**Math.** Define the antisymmetric matrix

$$\Omega = \begin{pmatrix} 0 & I_d \\ -I_d & 0\end{pmatrix}.$$

A differentiable map $\Phi$ is **symplectic** iff its Jacobian $J = D\Phi(z)$ satisfies, at every $z$,

$$J^{\top} \Omega\, J = \Omega .$$

Immediate consequences: $\det J = +1$ (volume), $J$ invertible with $J^{-1} = \Omega^{-1} J^\top \Omega$ (explicit inverse — "reversibility for free").

**→ CLU.** $J$ here is literally the *backprop Jacobian of one forward step*. Symplecticity is therefore simultaneously a statement about the stability of the forward dynamics and about the conditioning of the backward pass. That double duty is why we keep insisting on it.

> **Update (wave-5) — what symplecticity *functionally* buys, measured (the best "minus-the-physics" pedagogy we have).** The abstract invariant was turned into a controlled ablation (`minus-the-physics.md` Part A / critique G2: 3 seeds, dim 4, S¹ testbed, 150 ep, param-matched to ±0.05%). Two twins strip one property each: **`UnconstrainedTwin`** = a free residual recurrence (no $H$, no volume law), **`BrokenVolumeCHLU`** = the same leapfrog + potential but with a learned per-coordinate scaling $z\mapsto e^{\log s}\cdot\mathrm{Verlet}(z)$, so $\det J = e^{\sum\log s}\ne 1$ (bit-identical to CLU at init; only the scaling breaks symplecticity). "Which component buys what":
>
> | property (↓ better where noted) | CLU (symplectic) | broken-volume ($\det J\ne1$) | free twin (no physics) | attributed to |
> |---|---|---|---|---|
> | eval MSE (fit) | 0.190±0.018 | 0.079±0.004 | **0.013±0.008** | *nothing* — physics is a constraint that **costs raw fit**; the free twin fits ~15× better |
> | BIBO (frac settled bounded) | **1.00** | 0.33±0.47 | 1.00 | **volume conservation** (broken-vol diverges, $r^*\to10^{14}$ on 2/3 seeds) |
> | flat spectral $\mu^2$ (↓ = protected) | **0.008±0.015** | 0.122 | N/A | **volume conservation** — a protected near-flat memory direction |
> | latch coset drift (↓ = latches) | **0.19±0.19** | 0.245±0.05 | 1.15±1.2 | **leapfrog structure** — CLU latches ~6× tighter than the twin |
> | vacuum $r^*$ after wake–sleep CD | **0.72 (survives)** | **0.0 (collapsed)** | N/A | **volume conservation** — CD-vacuum survival (cross-links the erosion story, §1.8/§7.2) |
>
> **One-line:** symplectic *volume conservation* buys **bounded, protected, CD-robust memory**; the *leapfrog structure* buys the **latch**; neither buys raw fit — that is the price of the physics prior (an honest **Pareto-not-podium**, now measured rather than asserted; feeds all three shorts as claim **CM-1**). The measured cost is specifically **contraction-forbidden** (volume) — *not* the causal cap, which was inactive in this arm; see the "two forbidden cheats" frame (§8.4).

## 1.6 Discretization: why Euler kills you and Verlet doesn't

**Philosophy.** You can't run continuous flows on a GPU; you take steps of size $\varepsilon$. Naive discretization destroys the conservation laws you chose the framework for. The fix is not smaller steps — it's *structure-preserving* steps.

**Math — the cautionary worked example.** Explicit Euler on the harmonic oscillator ($H = \tfrac12 p^2 + \tfrac12 q^2$):

$$q' = q + \varepsilon p,\quad p' = p - \varepsilon q
\qquad\Rightarrow\qquad
J = \begin{pmatrix} 1 & \varepsilon\\ -\varepsilon & 1\end{pmatrix},\quad \det J = 1 + \varepsilon^2 .$$

Every step multiplies phase-space area by $1+\varepsilon^2$; eigenvalue moduli are $\sqrt{1+\varepsilon^2}$, so energy grows by $\approx \varepsilon^2$ per step *systematically*. At our default $\varepsilon = 0.05$ the state doubles its energy every $\ln 2/\varepsilon^2 \approx 277$ steps — the model explodes on a *perfect, quadratic, convex* landscape. This is the discrete-time version of "recurrent nets are unstable," and it is an artifact of the integrator, not the physics.

**The CLU's integrator (exact code semantics, `velocity_verlet_step`).** Kick–drift–kick (KDK) with an appended damping:

$$
\begin{aligned}
p_{1/2} &= p_n - \tfrac{\varepsilon}{2}\nabla_q V_\theta(q_n) &&\text{(half kick)}\\
q_{n+1} &= q_n + \varepsilon\, \nabla_p T(p_{1/2}) &&\text{(drift)}\\
p' &= p_{1/2} - \tfrac{\varepsilon}{2}\nabla_q V_\theta(q_{n+1}) &&\text{(half kick)}\\
p_{n+1} &= (1-\gamma)\, p' &&\text{(friction, } \gamma\in[0,1)\text{)}
\end{aligned}
$$

**Math — why the $\gamma=0$ map is exactly symplectic.** Each substep is a *shear*. The kick's Jacobian is lower block-triangular, the drift's upper block-triangular:

$$J_{\rm kick} = \begin{pmatrix} I & 0\\ -\tfrac{\varepsilon}{2}\nabla^2V & I\end{pmatrix},
\qquad
J_{\rm drift} = \begin{pmatrix} I & \varepsilon\nabla^2T\\ 0 & I\end{pmatrix}.$$

Check the symplectic condition for a lower shear $L = \begin{pmatrix} I&0\\ A&I\end{pmatrix}$:

$$L^\top \Omega L
= \begin{pmatrix} I & A^\top\\ 0 & I\end{pmatrix}
\begin{pmatrix} 0 & I\\ -I & 0\end{pmatrix}
\begin{pmatrix} I & 0\\ A & I\end{pmatrix}
= \begin{pmatrix} I & A^\top\\ 0 & I\end{pmatrix}
\begin{pmatrix} A & I\\ -I & 0\end{pmatrix}
= \begin{pmatrix} A - A^\top & I\\ -I & 0\end{pmatrix} \stackrel{!}{=} \Omega
\iff A = A^\top .$$

$A = -\tfrac{\varepsilon}{2}\nabla^2V$ is a Hessian, hence symmetric — symplectic. Same argument for the drift with $\nabla^2 T$. A composition of symplectic maps is symplectic (the condition chains through the product). So the conservative step is **exactly** symplectic — for *any* learned $V_\theta$, any step size, in exact arithmetic. Note where separability entered: because $T$ has no $q$-dependence and $V$ no $p$-dependence, the shears are exactly the pieces of $H$, and the explicit scheme works. **[proven; verified: check (d), $\lVert J^\top\Omega J - \Omega\rVert_{\max} \le 3.3\times 10^{-16}$ on random anharmonic potentials]**

**Look-up.** Störmer–Verlet / leapfrog; geometric numerical integration; (ML-adjacent) Hamiltonian Monte Carlo uses exactly this integrator.

**→ CLU.** The forward pass of the unit *is* this map (`lax.scan` over it). "Structural prior, not training target" cashes out here: stability comes from the shear structure, which no value of $\theta$ can break.

## 1.7 The shadow Hamiltonian: the discrete system is honest, just about a different energy

**Philosophy.** A discrete integrator can't conserve $H$ exactly — but a *symplectic* one does something better than "approximately conserve": it **exactly follows the physics of a slightly modified energy** $\tilde H = H + O(\varepsilon^2)$. All discretization error is repackaged as a small, *principled* deformation of the landscape. ML analogue you already know: SGD does not minimize the loss you wrote down; it (approximately) minimizes an implicitly regularized one. Backward error analysis is the physics version of that statement, made exact.

**Look-up.** Backward error analysis; modified equations; Baker–Campbell–Hausdorff; Benettin–Giorgilli theorem (exponentially long conservation times); textbook: Hairer–Lubich–Wanner, *Geometric Numerical Integration*.

**Math.** For our KDK map with separable $H$:

$$\tilde H = H + \frac{\varepsilon^2}{12}\Big[(\nabla_pT)^\top\, \nabla^2_qV\, (\nabla_pT) \;-\; \tfrac12 (\nabla_qV)^\top\, \nabla^2_pT\, (\nabla_qV)\Big] + O(\varepsilon^4).$$

*Validation on the solvable case.* For the harmonic oscillator ($T = p^2/2m$, $V = kq^2/2$, $h := \varepsilon\omega_0$, $\omega_0 = \sqrt{k/m}$) one can find the exactly conserved quadratic of the discrete map by solving $A^\top G A = G$ for the step matrix $A$ (§3.1). The unique answer (up to scale) is

$$\tilde H_{\rm SHO} \propto \frac{p^2}{2m} + \frac{k}{2}\Big(1 - \frac{h^2}{4}\Big) q^2,$$

and expanding the general formula above for this case reproduces exactly the same $q^2\!:\!p^2$ coefficient ratio $1 - h^2/4$ — the formula checks out analytically. Numerically, on a quartic well: $\mathrm{std}(H)$ along a trajectory scales as $\varepsilon^2$ (measured ×4.00 per step-doubling) while $\mathrm{std}(\tilde H)$ scales as $\varepsilon^4$ (measured ×16.0 per doubling): the map really does conserve $\tilde H$ one whole order better. **[proven (quadratic) + verified (f): std(H) = 1.16e−5 → 4.63e−5 → 1.85e−4; std($\tilde H$) = 1.94e−9 → 3.11e−8 → 4.98e−7 for $\varepsilon = .02,.04,.08$]**

Two corollaries with teeth:

1. **Frequencies get renormalized at $O(\varepsilon^2)$:** the discrete oscillation frequency is $\omega_{\rm map} = \arccos(1-\tfrac{h^2}{2})/\varepsilon = \omega_0(1 + \tfrac{h^2}{24} + \dots)$. So curvature-based timescales (the "forgetting rates" of §3) are step-size dependent at second order — quote them as map eigenvalues, not continuum formulas, in experiments.
2. **Symmetry protection [proven]:** every correction term in $\tilde H$ is built from derivatives/brackets of $T$ and $V$. If a linear symmetry preserves both, every term is invariant, so $\tilde H$ has the *same* flat directions as $H$: **discretization cannot generate a mass for an exactly-protected Goldstone mode** (§4). Protected memory is step-size-robust; unprotected (curvature) memory is not. This asymmetry is a selling point of the symmetry-based memory design in V2.

*Fine print:* the exponentially-long-time conservation guarantee needs $H$ real-analytic. tanh and swish are analytic; **ReLU is not** — a ReLU potential voids this class of guarantee. Keep activations smooth in CLU potentials. **[evidenced, standard]**

**→ CLU.** "Energy drift" plots in our experiments should oscillate, not trend, and the oscillation amplitude should scale as $\varepsilon^2$ — this is a free, sharp diagnostic that the implementation is healthy. And Prop-8 (symmetry protection) is a differentiator vs. any memory scheme whose lifetimes are curvature-tuned.

## 1.8 Friction: dissipation with an exact accounting — and the regularizer it exposed

**Philosophy.** The paper's stated limitation §D.1 is *hyper-stability*: a perfectly conservative memory can't forget at all. The code's answer is the $(1-\gamma)p$ step — shave a fixed fraction of momentum each tick. The physics question: what *exactly* does that do to the structure of §1.5? The answer is unusually clean: friction doesn't smear the symplectic structure, it scales it by an exactly known factor. The technical term is **conformal symplecticity**.

**Look-up.** Conformal symplectic / conformal Hamiltonian systems.

**Math — the master theorem of the whole program [proven, verified (d)].** The damping map $(q,p)\mapsto (q,(1-\gamma)p)$ has Jacobian $G = \mathrm{diag}(I, (1-\gamma)I)$, and

$$G^\top \Omega G = \begin{pmatrix} 0 & (1-\gamma)I\\ -(1-\gamma)I & 0\end{pmatrix} = (1-\gamma)\,\Omega .$$

Chain it with the (symplectic) Verlet part, $J = G\, J_{\rm kick_2} J_{\rm drift} J_{\rm kick_1}$:

$$J^\top \Omega J
= J_{\rm kick_1}^\top J_{\rm drift}^\top J_{\rm kick_2}^\top \big(G^\top\Omega G\big) J_{\rm kick_2} J_{\rm drift} J_{\rm kick_1}
= (1-\gamma)\,\Omega .$$

And since shears have unit determinant while $\det G = (1-\gamma)^d$:

$$\boxed{\,J^\top\Omega J = (1-\gamma)\Omega \quad\text{and}\quad \det J = (1-\gamma)^d\,}\qquad\text{exactly, for every } \theta, z.$$

Observed: $\lVert J^\top\Omega J - (1-\gamma)\Omega\rVert_{\max} \le 3.3\times10^{-16}$ across random potentials and states, $\gamma \in \{0, 0.15\}$.

**Corollary 1 — singular-value pairing (the honest gradient story) [proven].** Write $J = \sqrt{1-\gamma}\, S$; then $S^\top\Omega S = \Omega$, i.e. $S$ is symplectic. For symplectic $S$: $S^{-1} = \Omega^{-1} S^\top \Omega$, and since $\Omega$ is orthogonal, $S^{-1}$ has the same singular values as $S^\top$, hence as $S$. But the singular values of $S^{-1}$ are the reciprocals $\{1/\sigma_i\}$. Therefore $\{\sigma_i\} = \{1/\sigma_i\}$ as a multiset: **singular values come in $(\sigma, 1/\sigma)$ pairs.** For the damped map, pairs are $(\sqrt{1-\gamma}\,\sigma,\ \sqrt{1-\gamma}/\sigma)$ — each pair's product is exactly $1-\gamma$; over $n$ steps, $(1-\gamma)^n$.

Translation for deep-learning claims: through a conservative CLU unroll, backprop Jacobian spectra are symmetric in log — **gradients cannot all vanish** (that would need all $\sigma < 1$, impossible in pairs) **but can explode**, always with a matched contracting partner. Friction adds a uniform, *known* contraction overlay. "Symplectic ⇒ stable gradients" is a half-truth; the true statement is "symplectic ⇒ paired gradients," and controlling the spread of the pairs is a real job (§3.4). This honesty is baked into the ICLR thesis on purpose.

**Corollary 2 — our Lyapunov regularizer never did anything [proven, verified (d)].** The code's chaos penalty is $\mathrm{mean}_i \log\sigma_i(J)$. But

$$\mathrm{mean}_i \log \sigma_i = \frac{1}{2d}\log\prod_i\sigma_i = \frac{1}{2d}\log\lvert\det J\rvert = \tfrac12\ln(1-\gamma)$$

— a constant. Independent of $\theta$, of the state, of the learned landscape. And the training code calls the step with $\gamma = 0$, so the "loss" is identically zero with zero gradient (up to a $10^{-8}$ epsilon inside the log and float32 dust). Observed: deviation from $\tfrac12\ln(1-\gamma)$ under $2.1\times10^{-16}$ across random $\theta$ — while $\max_i \log\sigma_i$ varied by $O(0.1)$ across the same draws. So a *working* regularizer exists one line away: penalize $\max_i\log\sigma_i$, or $\sum_i(\log\sigma_i)^2$ (total squeeze/hyperbolicity), or $\sum_i \max(0,\log\sigma_i)$. The mean measures the *sum* of local Lyapunov exponents, which the structure theorem pins; chaos lives in the *max*.

**Look-up.** Lyapunov exponents, Lyapunov spectrum, Oseledets theorem.

**Corollary 3 — time-reversibility and wake–sleep.** With $R(q,p) := (q,-p)$: $R\circ\Phi_{\varepsilon,0}\circ R\circ\Phi_{\varepsilon,0} = \mathrm{id}$ — flip momenta, run forward, and you retrace your path. This is the precise mechanism behind "recognition is time-reversed generation" (one operator, no separate encoder/decoder). $\gamma > 0$ breaks it — *that is what forgetting is*, definitionally, in this formalism.

**What survives at a glance:**

| property | $\gamma = 0$ | $\gamma > 0$ (constant) | $\gamma(q)$ field (§7) |
|---|---|---|---|
| symplectic form | preserved exactly | scaled by $(1-\gamma)$ exactly | neither (locally distorted) |
| phase-space volume | preserved, $\det J = 1$ | contracts, $\det J = (1-\gamma)^d$ | $\det J = (1-\gamma(q'))^d$ **exactly** |
| singular values | pairs $(\sigma, 1/\sigma)$ | pairs $(\sigma, (1-\gamma)/\sigma)$ | pairing lost where $\nabla\gamma \ne 0$ |
| energy | shadow-$\tilde H$ conserved (osc. $\propto\varepsilon^2$) | drains from $T$ only | drains only inside the horizon |
| reversibility | exact | broken (by design) | broken inside the horizon only |

**→ CLU.** This table is the contract each vertical writes against: V2's memory claims live in column 1–2; V3's depth-stability claims must say *which* column they assume; Thread 1's trash regions are column 3. And the dead regularizer is a concrete engineering fix that gates any experiment claiming "Lyapunov regularization helped."

---

# Part II — Mass (all three of them) and the speed of causality

## 2.1 Inertial mass $M$: the learned learning-rate of the state

**Philosophy.** Inertial mass answers: *how much does this coordinate resist being pushed?* Big $M_i$: coordinate $i$ accelerates reluctantly, coasts ponderously, is hard to write and hard to disturb. Small $M_i$: twitchy, fast, cheap to rewrite. In HEP this is just "mass" in $F = ma$; in the CLU it is a **learned, per-coordinate responsiveness dial** on the latent state.

The exact ML anchor: match the heavy-ball optimizer (§1.1) to the damped Verlet step. With $T = p^\top M^{-1}p/2$, one step moves $q$ by $\varepsilon M^{-1}p_{1/2}$ where $p$ accumulates $-\varepsilon\nabla V$; eliminating $p$ gives position updates $\Delta q \approx -\frac{\varepsilon^2}{M}\nabla V + (1-\gamma)\Delta q_{\rm prev}$, i.e. heavy ball with

$$\text{momentum } \beta = 1-\gamma, \qquad \text{learning rate } \eta_i = \frac{\varepsilon^2}{M_i}.$$

**Inertial mass is an inverse per-coordinate learning rate** — and since $M$ is learned, the CLU learns its own preconditioner for the *state* dynamics, the way Adam estimates one for weights. (Code: $M = \mathrm{softplus}(\texttt{log\_mass})$, guaranteeing positivity.)

**Math — the three kinetic energies in the code.**

| mode | $T(p)$ | velocity $\dot q = \nabla_pT$ | rest inertia $M_{\rm eff}$ |
|---|---|---|---|
| `newtonian_identity` | $\tfrac12\lVert p\rVert^2$ | $p$ | $I$ |
| `newtonian_learned` | $\tfrac12 p^\top M^{-1}p$ | $M^{-1}p$ | $M$ |
| `relativistic` | $c\sqrt{p^\top M^{-1}p + m_0^2c^2}$ | $\dfrac{c\,M^{-1}p}{\sqrt{p^\top M^{-1}p + m_0^2c^2}}$ | $m_0M$ |

(The relativistic small-$p$ expansion: $\nabla_pT \to M^{-1}p/m_0$, so the *effective* Newtonian inertia is $m_0M$ — the rest mass multiplies every learned mass. Worth remembering when comparing modes at fixed `log_mass`.)

**→ CLU.** Thread 5's "mass = budget allocator" starts here — but see §2.3 for the honest scoping: at linear order a *constant* $M$ can be absorbed into coordinates; its irreducible content is the nonlinear/relative effects (speed caps, regime assignment, shared-landscape pricing, boost response, learnability).

## 2.2 The causal speed $c$, rest mass $m_0$, and the relativistic governor

**Philosophy.** Special relativity's core dogma: there is a maximum speed, period; as you pump momentum into a particle, its *velocity* saturates while its energy keeps growing. Two pointers for later reading: **light cone** (the region of spacetime you can influence at speed ≤ c — the paper's "causal diamond" framing lives here) and **rest energy** $E_0 = m_0c^2$ (energy of a particle that isn't moving at all). We import the *math* of this, per P1, with zero commitment to spacetime: in the CLU, $c$ is a **hard, built-in slew-rate limit on latent content**. The ML anchor: it behaves like gradient clipping — but it is not a bolted-on projection; it is a smooth reparameterization of kinetic energy whose *conservation properties survive* (clipping inside a Hamiltonian would break energy accounting; the relativistic $T$ doesn't).

**Math — deriving the speed limit.** Take coordinate $i$, diagonal $M$, and let its momentum grow, $p_i \to \infty$ (others fixed):

$$\dot q_i = \frac{c\, (p_i/M_i)}{\sqrt{p_i^2/M_i + m_0^2c^2}} \;\xrightarrow{p_i\to\infty}\; \frac{c\,(p_i/M_i)}{\lvert p_i\rvert/\sqrt{M_i}} = \frac{c}{\sqrt{M_i}}\,\mathrm{sign}(p_i).$$

$$\boxed{\ v^{\max}_i = \frac{c}{\sqrt{M_i}}\ }$$

**The cap is anisotropic**: heavy coordinates have *lower* speed limits. The paper's "velocity saturates at $c$" is exact only for $M = I$. This is a feature — each particle carries its own causal budget, which is precisely Thread 5's "per-particle governor" — but experiments quoting the bound must quote the right one. **[proven; verified (i): $v(p=10^8) = c/\sqrt{M}$ to 9 digits for $M \in \{0.25, 1, 4\}$]**

**The crossover momentum.** Where does "gentle Newtonian" become "governed relativistic"? Set $p^*_i = m_0c\sqrt{M_i}$ (the momentum at which the two terms under the root are equal):

$$v(p^*_i) = \frac{c(p^*_i/M_i)}{\sqrt{2}\, m_0 c} = \frac{1}{\sqrt2}\,\frac{c}{\sqrt{M_i}} = \frac{v^{\max}_i}{\sqrt2}.$$

So $p^* = m_0c\sqrt{M}$ marks the knee **[verified (i): $v(p^*)/v^{\max} = 0.707107$]**. Consequences: *light particles go relativistic first* — at a common momentum scale, small-$M$ coordinates are governor-dominated (saturated, safe, insensitive to further momentum) while large-$M$ coordinates remain in the linear regime. And $m_0$ globally scales the knee: it is the **size of the Newtonian window**, as well as the idle-energy floor $T(0) = m_0c^2$.

**Two more exact relativistic facts we use:**

1. **Kinetic mode-coupling [proven].** The velocity of mode $i$ depends on *everyone's* momentum through the shared square root:
$$\nabla^2_p T = \frac{c}{\sqrt{\cdot}}\Big[M^{-1} - \frac{(M^{-1}p)(M^{-1}p)^\top}{p^\top M^{-1}p + m_0^2c^2}\Big],$$
diagonal at $p=0$ but off-diagonal at finite momentum: a "hot" coordinate makes the whole unit effectively heavier and cross-coupled. Newtonian modes never do this. (Practical: spectra computed at attractors are exact at rest only; hot/boosted states shift them.)
2. **What one friction step removes [proven].** For the relativistic $T$: using $p\cdot\nabla T = (T^2 - m_0^2c^4)/T$,
$$\delta T \approx -\gamma\,\Big(T - \frac{m_0^2c^4}{T}\Big),$$
zero at rest, $\approx -\gamma T$ ultra-relativistically. The governor (code: $\gamma_n = s\cdot\tanh(\max(0, H - E^*))$, a one-sided brake above a target energy $E^*$, coasting below — a Van der Pol-style limit-cycle controller; look-up: *Van der Pol oscillator, limit cycle*) drains energy at this rate whenever the state runs hot.

**And one negative fact that matters enormously (preview of §3.4):** the governor triggers on $H - E^*$. Rolling off a *saddle* of $V$ converts potential to kinetic energy **at constant $H$** — the governor is structurally blind to it. The only thing that bounds the resulting velocity is the relativistic cap. In Newtonian modes, saddle escape reaches arbitrary speed at fixed energy. **The relativistic mode is not garnish; it is the only lever that bounds isoenergetic instability.** **[proven]**

**→ CLU.** This section is the "C" in CLU. Exp II's kinetic-safety claim, the anomaly-detection story (energy + saturation as physically-bounded responses to perturbation), and V1's "retry that can't hurt you" all lean on these bounds — with the anisotropy $c/\sqrt{M_i}$ now part of the official statement.

## 2.3 Spectral mass $\mu$: the HEP meaning of "mass," and why it is a forgetting rate

**Philosophy.** Here is the conceptual pivot the whole program turns on. In particle physics, the "mass of a particle" is **not** inertia-in-your-hand; it is the **curvature of the field's potential at its resting point** — how strongly the field is pulled back when displaced. A field $\phi$ with potential term $\tfrac12\mu^2\phi^2$ oscillates at frequency $\mu$: that $\mu$ *is* the particle's mass. (Look-up: *mass term*, *Klein–Gordon equation*, *dispersion relation*.) Massless particles (photons) = zero curvature = no restoring force = they propagate forever without decaying. That last sentence, transplanted into a learning system, is the entire V2 thesis: **zero curvature = no restoring force = a value that, once written, is not pulled back = memory.**

**Math — from the CLU's matrices to mode masses.** Linearize at an attractor $q^\ast$ ($\nabla V(q^\ast) = 0$), stiffness $K := \nabla^2V(q^\ast)$, inertia $M_{\rm eff}$:

$$M_{\rm eff}\,\delta\ddot q = -K\,\delta q .$$

Change to **canonically normalized coordinates** $\tilde q = M_{\rm eff}^{1/2}q$, $\tilde p = M_{\rm eff}^{-1/2}p$ — note this rescaling is itself symplectic ($d\tilde q\wedge d\tilde p = dq\wedge dp$), so it costs nothing structurally. The dynamics becomes $\delta\ddot{\tilde q} = -W\,\delta\tilde q$ with

$$W := M_{\rm eff}^{-1/2}\, K\, M_{\rm eff}^{-1/2}, \qquad \mu_k^2 := \lambda_k(W).$$

The $\mu_k$ are the **spectral masses** = normal-mode frequencies. All linear dynamics — oscillation, decay, stability — depends on $M$ and $K$ *only through* $W$'s spectrum.

**The two masses run in opposite directions.** At fixed curvature, $\mu = \sqrt{k/M}$: heavier inertia ⇒ *lighter* spectral mass ⇒ slower mode. This collision has already bitten our own notes once ("integrate out heavy modes"): in EFT vocabulary "heavy" means large $\mu$ (fast, stiff, UV — the stuff you *eliminate* when coarse-graining), while in the allocator vocabulary "heavy" means large $M$ (slow, ponderous, IR — the stuff you *keep*). Same axis, opposite adjectives. Program rule (F5 Def-2): the bare word "mass" is banned; say **inertial mass $M$** or **spectral mass $\mu$**.

| | inertial mass $M$ | spectral mass $\mu$ |
|---|---|---|
| what it is | kinetic-term coefficient | $\sqrt{\text{curvature}/\text{inertia}}$, mode frequency |
| big value means | slow, high-capacitance, low speed cap | stiff, fast, quickly-forgetting |
| HEP name | (nonrelativistic) inertia | particle mass, mass term |
| CLU role | response dial / allocator | **forgetting rate** (§3) |
| EFT reading | large $M$ = IR = kept | large $\mu$ = UV = integrated out |

**The honest deflation of "mass runs everything" [proven].** Since the canonical rescaling is symplectic and free, a *constant* $M$ is a coordinate convention at linear order — absorbable. What cannot be absorbed, and is therefore the *real* content of the learned $M$:

1. the anisotropic causal cap $v^{\max}_i = c/\sqrt{M_i}$ (§2.2) — lives in physical coordinates where data lives;
2. relativistic regime assignment $p^*_i = m_0c\sqrt{M_i}$ (§2.2);
3. **relative pricing of a shared landscape**: one $V_\theta$, many coordinates — $M$ redistributes the *same* curvature into different timescales $\mu_i^2 = K_{ii}/M_i$ (heavy+flat = deep memory; light+curved = fast scratch);
4. boost response under *mass-weighted* squeezes (§6.4);
5. learnability: `log_mass` is a $d$-parameter knob gradient descent can turn without deforming $V_\theta$ — a cheap reallocation channel. **[design hypothesis → sharpened at wave-5, see below]**

> **Update (wave-5) — the "mass hierarchy must be designed-in, not awaited" doctrine (Hyp-3), sharpened by its missing control (critique P5/G4; claim CM-5).** Three earlier reads agreed that learned $M$ stays near-uniform and *no hierarchy emerges* — `mass-spectrum-peek.md` ($\sigma_{\rm struct}$ 2–23% of init), `v1-l0-gate.md` (log-std ≈ 0.08 across 23 PCD models), `seed-sweeps.md` ("the optimizer never finds the 16× ratio"). `mass-lr-doctrine-test.md` (50 cells, 5 seeds, 2-unit lattice, 16× data-mass ratio) supplied the control they lacked — a mass-specific Adam lr (new flag `training.mass_lr_mult`) + a longer horizon — and **re-scoped the reading, not retracted it**:
> - the "~0.08 narrowness ceiling / optimizer never differentiates" was a **300-epoch training-budget artifact**: at 1500 ep the uniform lattice orders its masses in the **correct direction on 5/5 seeds** (Spearman +0.89±0.00) *even at base lr*, and a **10× mass-lr amplifies the spread to 0.52±0.29 — 6.5× the ceiling — while cutting eval-MSE 1.85→0.64**;
> - **but the designed magnitude is never reached**: best induced $M$ = (1.75, 0.98) vs designed (3.11, 0.19); the designed prior fits **7–14× better** (eval-MSE 0.046 vs 0.635). The optimizer induces the *qualitative* ordering, never the *quantitative* band;
> - **naive over-lr is actively harmful**: 100× lr overshoots into a global-mass runaway that **inverts the ordering** (Spearman −0.80; the fast unit ends up heaviest). Its low MSE is a trap — a global rescale that happens to fit. **Read alignment/spread as the hierarchy signal, never MSE** (MSE rewards the runaway). Slow-first curriculum did not help (arguably hurt).
>
> **Net:** Hyp-3 holds with the control in hand, sharpened to *"the mass **ordering** is inducible (mass-lr ≈10× + longer training); the designed **magnitude** must be built in."* The V3 banding story therefore reframes: banding is not "a prior the optimizer cannot reach at all" — it reaches the ordering — it is "the magnitude the optimizer only approaches, plus a still-worthwhile selection recipe." (This is the *pivot variable* the ledger flags — narrow $M$ is why boost-retries and the FDT violation are both currently inert; all three unlock together once a real hierarchy exists.)

> **Update (wave-6; source w6) — the over-lr failure mode is *ratio-dependent* (CM-5 addendum).** `v3-band-selection.md` item 3 (48 cells, mult$\in\{3,10,30,100\}\times N\in\{2,4\}\times$ ratio$\in\{4\times,16\times\}$, 3 seeds, 1500 ep) generalizes the sweet spot and sharpens the "100× inverts" clause. **mult ≈ 10 is a reliable inducer of the correct ordering across the whole grid** (align Spearman **+0.80…+1.00**, healthy log-spread 0.44–0.61) — a *usable default*, not a single-cell artifact. **High mult (≥30) is harmful, but the failure mode depends on the data-mass ratio:** at **16×** ratio it drives an *ordering inversion* (N=2 mult=100 → align **−1.00**; onset already at mult=30); at **4×** ratio the ordering does *not* invert even at 100× — instead over-lr triggers a **global-mass runaway that explodes the fit** (N=4 / 4× / mult=100 → eval-MSE **35.3 ± 47.3**). So "≈100× inverts" holds *for large ratio only*; for small ratio the same over-lr blows up the magnitude without flipping the order. Either way mult ≈ 10 is the safe operating point — and (reproducing the CM-5 MSE-trap) at N=2/16× mult=100 has the *lowest* MSE (0.332) yet align = −1.00, so **read align/spread, never MSE, as the hierarchy signal.**

> **🔴 Update (wave-19; source `clu-latent-io-audit` §Item 1 → N64, and `clu-memory-architecture` §2 Prop 6) — THE MECHANISM ABOVE IS WRONG ON THE EVAL PATH. THE CONCLUSION IS NOT.** Everything above is measured on the `train.py` lattice path and is **not retracted**. But on the **eval/CAFE path** (`clu_scorer.py`, FD001, 150 ep, `newtonian_learned`) the story inverts:
> - ⛔ **`log_mass` is NOT frozen and NOT in a null direction.** It moves **+3.56** (M 0.67 → 3.56), travels **4.28× more per parameter than $V_\theta$**, and receives a gradient **8,200× larger per parameter**. ⇒ **N7's "masses stay at init" is WRONG here, and the D1 premise (rich-gradient levers starve the slow ones) is INVERTED here.** *Any wording asserting frozen masses now needs a path qualifier.*
> - ✅ **But the *spectrum* is still inert, and the conclusion stands.** The drift is **common mode** (differential:common = **1:39**) and **$M_{\max}/M_{\min}$ ends at 1.153 — BELOW its random init of 1.265.** ***Training makes the mass spectrum more uniform than random initialization.*** So *"no timescale hierarchy, every channel the same inertia"* is **confirmed a fourth time** — by a new mechanism.
> - **The three causes, all newly identified:** (i) **99.8% of the mass gradient comes from `E_reg`**, a numerical-hygiene term whose only lever on $H$ is inflating $M$ *uniformly* — pure common-mode pressure; (ii) ⭐ **the EBM contrastive term has EXACTLY ZERO mass gradient** (negatives perturb $q$ only, so kinetic terms cancel identically in $H(\text{data}) - H(\text{neg})$) — **the objective's representational half is structurally blind to the mass**; (iii) the **softplus trap** — $M=\mathrm{softplus}(\log M)$ is linear for $x\gg0$, so once `E_reg` drives $\log M \to 3.5$ a *log*-scale spread stops buying *exponential* range (Std$(\log M)=0.56$ → ratio only **1.28**).
> - ⚠ **Both measured levers work by SUPPRESSING dynamics, not adding physics.** `mass_lr_mult=10` buys **+0.055** on FD001 (0.6540 → 0.7092) and the relax-budget lever buys +0.061, **but they are non-additive** (both plateau ≈0.714, still under a raw-statistics baseline at **0.7486**) — and the force share *falls* 0.170 → 0.101. *The gain is "less blow-up", not "more physics."*
> - ⚠ **Entangled with the `dt` units bug (§11.4 / N63):** 99.8% of that mass gradient is an `E_reg` artifact of running `dt=0.05` on cycle-indexed data, so **this mechanism may itself shift at `dt=1.0` — untested.**
> - ⭐ **The fix is architectural, and it is proven (Prop 6, §11.5): masses must be PER-ADDRESS codebook entries, not a global model parameter.** With per-item masses only the overall *scale* stays gauge and **all ratios become wake-visible and exactly learnable (verified to 2.2e-14)**. ⚠ **And the D1 lr-partition constant is K-dependent — $\eta_m/(\eta_m + K\eta_k)$, NOT the old $1/11$** (K=2 → 1/21, matched to 13 digits).
> **Net, and it is the wave's epistemic lesson in one line:** *the conclusion this section reached survived the death of both mechanisms proposed to explain it — because someone finally measured the mechanism instead of inheriting it.*

**→ CLU.** Practical mandate that came out of this: the analyst's "mass-spectrum" measurements must compute $\mathrm{eig}(M_{\rm eff}^{-1}\nabla^2V_\theta(q^\ast))$ at trained attractors — the $M$ vector alone is literally half the object. And every V2/V3 claim about timescales should be stated in $\mu$, with $M$ appearing only via its five irreducible roles.

# Part III — Forgetting, exactly: the mode taxonomy

This part is the mathematical core of V2 and of the ICLR thesis ("the spectrum is the memory–compute–forgetting budget"). Everything reduces to one exactly solvable object: a single mode under the damped Verlet map. We solve it completely, then read off the taxonomy.

## 3.1 The per-mode $2\times2$ map (full algebra)

Setup: one canonical mode, inertia $m$, stiffness $k$ (so spectral mass $\mu = \sqrt{k/m}$, dimensionless step $h := \varepsilon\mu$), state $(x, p)$. Apply the code's step (§1.6) with $\nabla V = kx$, $\nabla T = p/m$:

**Half kick:** $\quad p_{1/2} = p - \tfrac{\varepsilon}{2}kx$

**Drift:** $\quad x' = x + \tfrac{\varepsilon}{m}p_{1/2} = \Big(1 - \tfrac{\varepsilon^2k}{2m}\Big)x + \tfrac{\varepsilon}{m}p = \Big(1-\tfrac{h^2}{2}\Big)x + \tfrac{\varepsilon}{m}p$

**Half kick:** $\quad p'' = p_{1/2} - \tfrac{\varepsilon}{2}kx' = p - \tfrac{\varepsilon}{2}kx - \tfrac{\varepsilon}{2}k\Big[\big(1-\tfrac{h^2}{2}\big)x + \tfrac{\varepsilon}{m}p\Big] = \Big(1-\tfrac{h^2}{2}\Big)p - \varepsilon k\Big(1 - \tfrac{h^2}{4}\Big)x$

**Friction:** $\quad p_{\rm new} = (1-\gamma)p''$

So one step is the linear map $z_{n+1} = A z_n$ with

$$A = \underbrace{\begin{pmatrix}1 & 0\\ 0 & 1-\gamma\end{pmatrix}}_{\text{friction}}\underbrace{\begin{pmatrix} 1-\tfrac{h^2}{2} & \tfrac{\varepsilon}{m}\\[2pt] -\varepsilon k\big(1-\tfrac{h^2}{4}\big) & 1-\tfrac{h^2}{2}\end{pmatrix}}_{\text{Verlet}},
\qquad
\boxed{\ \operatorname{tr}A = (2-\gamma)\Big(1-\tfrac{h^2}{2}\Big),\quad \det A = 1-\gamma\ }$$

(the Verlet factor has determinant exactly 1 — check: $(1-\tfrac{h^2}{2})^2 + h^2(1-\tfrac{h^2}{4}) = 1$ — which is §1.6's symplecticity showing up concretely). Everything below is the eigenvalue analysis $\lambda_\pm = \tfrac12\big(\operatorname{tr}A \pm \sqrt{\operatorname{tr}^2A - 4\det A}\big)$ in three regimes of $k$. The general multi-mode case block-diagonalizes into these cells in the $W$-eigenbasis (§2.3), so this *is* the general local theory.

## 3.2 Flat modes ($\mu = 0$): the Goldstone latch **[proven, verified (a)]**

**Philosophy.** A flat direction feels no force. Naively "no force + friction ⇒ everything grinds to a halt and nothing interesting happens." The actual discrete answer is sharper and is the single most load-bearing fact in V2: friction stops the *motion* but is structurally incapable of touching the *value* — and by stopping the motion it *protects* the value.

**Math.** Set $k = 0$:

$$A = \begin{pmatrix} 1 & \tfrac{\varepsilon}{m}\\ 0 & 1-\gamma\end{pmatrix}
\quad\Rightarrow\quad
p_n = (1-\gamma)^np_0, \qquad
x_n = x_0 + \frac{\varepsilon}{m}\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}(1-\gamma)^jp_0 .$$

The geometric series closes:

$$\boxed{\; x_\infty = x_0 + \frac{\varepsilon\, p_0}{m\,\gamma}\;}, \qquad p_n \to 0 \text{ with half-life } \frac{\ln 2}{-\ln(1-\gamma)} \approx \frac{\ln 2}{\gamma}\text{ steps}.$$

Eigen-decomposition view (worth internalizing): eigenvalues $\{1,\ 1-\gamma\}$; the $\lambda = 1$ eigenvector is pure displacement $(1,0)$; the $\lambda = 1-\gamma$ eigenvector is $\big(-\tfrac{\varepsilon}{m\gamma},\, 1\big)$ — "momentum plus the displacement it will still deliver before dying." Decompose any initial state: the surviving component's coefficient is exactly $x_0 + \varepsilon p_0/(m\gamma)$.

Three readings, in increasing order of importance:

1. **Dissipation kills Goldstone momentum but cannot erase Goldstone displacement** — the roadmap's slogan, now an identity of the map.
2. **It's better than "cannot erase": friction *freezes* the register.** At $\gamma = 0$ the flat direction is a *marginal integrator*: any residual momentum makes the value drift forever ($x_n = x_0 + n\varepsilon p_0/m$). Any $\gamma > 0$ converts it into a **latch**: a write (a momentum impulse) moves the stored value by the finite, predictable amount $\varepsilon p_0/(m\gamma)$, after which it holds with *infinite* half-life. Friction is not the enemy of Goldstone memory; it is its write-settle mechanism.
3. **The write amplitude is $1/\gamma$-amplified:** lower friction ⇒ longer coast ⇒ bigger committed change per unit of injected momentum. $\gamma$ is simultaneously the settle-speed and the write-gain knob.

Relativistic drift version: replace $\varepsilon p/m$ by $\varepsilon\nabla_pT(p)$; the sum $x_\infty = x_0 + \varepsilon\sum_j \nabla_pT\big((1-\gamma)^jp_0\big)$ converges absolutely (geometric decay + bounded velocity); no closed form, same latch.

**Numbers.** Newtonian: predicted $x_\infty = 0.4$, observed $\lvert x_N - 0.4\rvert = 1.0\times10^{-15}$ after 4000 steps; companion curved mode decayed to $10^{-45}$. Relativistic: trajectory matches its series prediction to 0 ulp and moves 0.0 between steps 2000 and 4000 (frozen).

**→ CLU.** This is V2's mechanism in one box, and it kills paper-limitation §D.1 *in principle*: you can have $\gamma > 0$ (noise damping, hallucination cleanup) while symmetry-protected registers hold forever. The forgetting knob and the memory no longer fight — *if* the memory is flat by symmetry (§4) rather than by accident.

## 3.3 Massive modes ($\mu^2 > 0$): the exact retention law **[proven, verified (b)]**

**Underdamped/overdamped split.** Eigenvalues are complex iff $\operatorname{tr}^2A < 4\det A$:

$$(2-\gamma)^2\Big(1-\tfrac{h^2}{2}\Big)^2 < 4(1-\gamma)
\iff
\Big\lvert 1-\tfrac{h^2}{2}\Big\rvert < \frac{2\sqrt{1-\gamma}}{2-\gamma}.$$

For the relevant small-$h$ branch, solve for the threshold:

$$h^{*2} = 2\Big[1 - \frac{2\sqrt{1-\gamma}}{2-\gamma}\Big] = \frac{2\big[(2-\gamma) - 2\sqrt{1-\gamma}\big]}{2-\gamma},$$

and the bracket is a perfect square: $(2-\gamma) - 2\sqrt{1-\gamma} = 1 - 2\sqrt{1-\gamma} + (1-\gamma) = \big(1-\sqrt{1-\gamma}\big)^2$. Hence

$$\boxed{\ h^*(\gamma) = \big(1-\sqrt{1-\gamma}\big)\sqrt{\frac{2}{2-\gamma}} = \frac{\gamma}{2} + O(\gamma^2)\ }$$

**[verified: $h^* = 0.025643$ at $\gamma=0.05$, $0.111284$ at $\gamma = 0.2$; eigenvalues flip real↔complex exactly there]**. (There is a second, re-entrant boundary as $h \to 2$; at $h > 2$ the $\gamma=0$ map is unstable — the stiffness limit $\varepsilon < 2/\mu_{\max}$ from §1.7.)

**Underdamped regime ($h^* < h < 2$): decay is mass-independent.** Complex pair ⇒ $\lvert\lambda\rvert^2 = \lambda\bar\lambda = \det A = 1-\gamma$:

$$\boxed{\ \lvert\lambda\rvert = \sqrt{1-\gamma}\ \text{ regardless of } \mu\ }
\qquad
n_{1/2} = \frac{2\ln2}{-\ln(1-\gamma)} \approx \frac{2\ln 2}{\gamma}\ \text{steps}.$$

The mode *oscillates* at $\approx\mu$ (phase carries information!) while its envelope decays at a rate set by $\gamma$ **alone**. Surprise finding: in this whole band, curvature buys you *frequency*, not *lifetime*. **[verified: $\lvert\lambda\rvert = 0.974679434 = \sqrt{0.95}$ for both $m=1$ and $m=0.25$ at $k=4$]**

**Overdamped regime ($0 < h < h^*$): the memory leak.** Both eigenvalues real; use $\lambda_{\rm slow}\lambda_{\rm fast} = 1-\gamma$ and $\lambda_{\rm slow} + \lambda_{\rm fast} = \operatorname{tr}A$ to write $F(\lambda) := \lambda + \frac{1-\gamma}{\lambda} = \operatorname{tr}A$. At $h = 0$: $F(1) = 2-\gamma = \operatorname{tr}A\vert_{h=0}$ — the latch, recovered. Perturb: $F'(1) = 1-(1-\gamma) = \gamma$ and $d(\operatorname{tr}A)/d(h^2) = -\tfrac{2-\gamma}{2}$, so

$$\boxed{\ \lambda_{\rm slow} = 1 - \frac{(2-\gamma)\,h^2}{2\gamma} + O(h^4)
\qquad
n_{1/2} \approx \frac{2\gamma\ln2}{(2-\gamma)\,(\varepsilon\mu)^2} \xrightarrow{\gamma\ll1} \frac{\gamma\ln 2}{(\varepsilon\mu)^2}\ }$$

Continuum sanity check: per unit time the rate is $\approx \varepsilon\mu^2/\gamma = \mu^2/\gamma_c$ — the textbook overdamped relaxation rate. ✓

**The pseudo-Goldstone exponent (V2's headline number).** For a weakly-lifted flat direction ($\mu^2 \propto$ breaking $\delta$, §4.5), which necessarily sits overdamped:

$$n_{1/2} \propto \mu^{-2} \propto \delta^{-1}$$

— **exponent exactly $-2$ in spectral mass, $-1$ in the breaking parameter**, valid only up to the crossover, after which lifetime saturates at the mass-independent $2\ln2/\gamma$. The Thread-2 conjecture ("half-life ∝ 1/mass²") is confirmed, with a scope and a floor it didn't have. **[verified: measured $n_{1/2}$ = 1544 → 6165 → 24649 for $\mu^2 = .04 → .01 → .0025$; ratios 3.993, 3.998 vs 4.0 predicted; exact vs asymptotic eigenvalue agree to $5\times10^{-8}$]**

> **Update (wave-8; source `v2-referee-experiments` SF-3, 3 seeds) — every headline retention/GMOR/EP law SURVIVES past the erosion horizon under the shipped anchor cure (CM-6 amended).** The laws above were measured on 150-ep checkpoints (pre-erosion cleanliness); the referee asked the obvious question — *do your laws survive your own cure at long horizon?* Re-verified on checkpoints trained to **3000 ep with the V(data)-anchor $\lambda=100$** (fix-pack-4's bulletproof setting, §7.3), i.e. well past the sleep-erosion horizon that destroys the default-1000-ep vacuum:
> - vacuum intact (anchor holds): $r^\ast=0.917\pm0.007$, ring depth $+0.10$–$0.12$, flat-mode $\mu^2\approx10^{-15}$ (machine-flat), angular overlap 1.0000 (seed-42 wake loss reproduces `anchor-robustness` to 5 digits — cross-run repro passes);
> - **GMOR $\mu^2\propto\delta$ law EXACT:** $\mu^2_{\rm meas}/[\delta n^2/(M_{\rm ch}r^{\ast2})]=1.00000\pm{\le}1.5\times10^{-12}$ across **4.6 decades** ($\delta\,10^{-4}\to4$), 3/3 seeds;
> - **retention law survives:** overdamped slope **$-0.956$** (F5 predicts $-1$; cf. 150-ep $-0.985$ — marginally softer, law holds), underdamped saturation at the mass-independent floor **27.03**, latch/flat mode preserved ($\mu^2\approx10^{-15}\Rightarrow\infty$ retention on the coset);
> - **EP onset survives, bit-identical to 150 ep:** below EP $\varphi=0$ exactly (max freq-jac $0.0$, all seeds); above EP log-log slope $\varphi$ vs $(h-h^\ast)=$ **0.5165** (Cor-13/C3 predicts 0.5) — *bit-identical* to the 150-ep 0.5165.
> **Reading:** the GMOR/retention/EP laws are not artifacts of a short, clean training run; they hold under the anchored long-horizon training the V2 paper actually ships. `[measured, 3 seeds, anchored λ=100, 3000 ep; SF-3]` See §7.3 for the erosion + anchor mechanism.
>
> **Caveat on how retention is *reported* (wave-8; source `v2-referee-experiments` SF-2 — the "4×" retirement).** The primer's retention numbers are in **map-steps** (integrator steps), the physics-native unit. Any *compute* comparison to an RNN baseline must renormalize: one CLU dissipative-Verlet step costs **≈6.2× an LSTM cell / ≈3.1× a LEM cell in wall time** (14–15× FLOPs; two $\nabla_q V$ backprops per KDK step, not width-matched). So the headline "≈4× longer retention than an LSTM" (263 vs 69 map-steps) **inverts under compute normalization** (23.5× more wall / 54.8× FLOPs vs LSTM). The structural triad — **latch, $\mu^{-2}$ budget law, bounded drift** — is architecture- and compute-independent and is the load-bearing claim; the "4× longer" is retired as a compute statement (CM-4 amended).
>
> **Update (wave-10; source `v2-referee-2` "the 5× determination"; CM-4 **misprediction canonical**) — the trained-model misprediction is ≈3.2×; the "5×" is an EXACT-MAP-ONLY number and must never be quoted as a trained-model result.** The overdamped retention law above is the **overdamped face** of the budget; past the exceptional point (§3.3 crossover) the law *mispredicts*, and the size of that misprediction has two distinct, non-interchangeable values:
> - **On trained models:** the deepest measured breaking is $\delta=4$, where meas/pred $=$ **0.309** (exact-gap predictor) / **0.304** (Mo's own $\hat\lambda(128)$) $\Rightarrow$ **misprediction $\approx3.2\times$**. The full **14-$\delta$ sweep contains no deeper underdamped row** — the censored rows are on the *overdamped* side. So $\approx3.2\text{–}3.3\times$ is the trained-model ceiling, full stop.
> - **On the exact analytic map** (F5 note, check (k), $\gamma=0.1$, $\varepsilon=0.1$, further underdamped than any trained run): the deepest ratio is **0.19 $\Rightarrow$ $\approx5.3\times$**. This is the genuine "$\sim5\times$", and it lives at a **different $\gamma$ and a deeper $\delta$** than the trained battery.
> - **Provenance of the error** (why this is worth a primer note): the "5×" entered the program as a **mislabel in a source report** — `v2-full-runs` line 79 annotates the $0.309\pm0.012$ row as "*(5× failure, calculable direction)*" — and propagated into a draft abstract. A $0.309$ ratio is $3.24\times$, not $5\times$. The physics is unaffected (the law fails ballistically past the EP either way, in the *calculable* direction); only the quoted magnitude was wrong, and it was wrong in the direction that flatters us.

## 3.4 Expanding modes ($\mu^2 < 0$): two impossibility results **[proven, verified (b)]**

**Philosophy.** A saddle direction of $V_\theta$ is a hilltop: displacements grow. This is the chaos/creativity/sensitivity channel — and the gradient-explosion channel of §1.8. The natural hopes are "add friction" and "the governor will catch it." Both fail, *provably*, and knowing that rewires the engineering.

**Impossibility 1: no amount of friction stabilizes a saddle.** With $k < 0$, write $g := \varepsilon\sqrt{\lvert k\rvert/m}$; now $\operatorname{tr}A = (2-\gamma)(1 + \tfrac{g^2}{2})$, $\det A = 1-\gamma$, both eigenvalues real and positive. Could $\lambda_+ < 1$?

$$\lambda_+ < 1 \iff \sqrt{\operatorname{tr}^2 - 4\det} < 2 - \operatorname{tr}
\;\Rightarrow\; \operatorname{tr}^2 - 4(1-\gamma) < 4 - 4\operatorname{tr} + \operatorname{tr}^2
\iff \operatorname{tr} < 1 + \det = 2-\gamma .$$

But $\operatorname{tr}A = (2-\gamma)(1+\tfrac{g^2}{2}) > 2-\gamma$ whenever $g > 0$. Contradiction: $\lambda_+ > 1$ for **every** $\gamma \in [0,1)$. Friction only slows the escape — with a rate law that beautifully mirrors the memory law (same formula, sign flipped):

$$\lambda_+ \approx 1 + \frac{(2-\gamma)g^2}{2\gamma} \ (g \ll \gamma), \qquad \lambda_+ \approx e^{g}\ (\gamma\to0).$$

Physical picture: velocity damping cannot hold you *on top of a hill* — you slide arbitrarily slowly, but you slide. (Continuum check: $\ddot x + \gamma_c\dot x = \lvert\mu^2\rvert x$ has roots $\tfrac12\big({-\gamma_c} \pm \sqrt{\gamma_c^2 + 4\lvert\mu^2\rvert}\big)$; the $+$ root is positive for every $\gamma_c$.) **[verified: $\min_\gamma(\max\lvert\lambda\rvert - 1) = 1.4\times10^{-5} > 0$ over a $\gamma$-grid; escape rate: exact 1.000112449, asymptotic 1.000112500, simulation-fitted 1.000112449]**

**Impossibility 2: the governor cannot see it.** Sliding off a saddle converts $V \to T$ at **constant $H$** (§1.2's conservation identity). The governor's trigger is $\max(0, H - E^*)$ — unchanged during exactly this failure mode. So energy-gated friction is blind to isoenergetic instability. What *does* bound the blow-up: the relativistic cap $v^{\max}_i = c/\sqrt{M_i}$ — position growth at most linear in time, no matter what (§2.2). **[proven]**

**→ CLU (engineering consequence).** The anti-explosion levers for deep/long unrolls are exactly two: **curvature control of $V_\theta$** (a *working* Lyapunov-type penalty — the current one is provably inert, §1.8) and **the relativistic kinetic mode**. Not $\gamma$. Not the governor. Paper narratives and ablations should be built accordingly (V3's trainability story; the honest-correction line of the ICLR thesis).

## 3.5 The budget table (assemble the taxonomy)

Per step $\varepsilon$, friction $\gamma$; mode of spectral mass $\mu$, $h = \varepsilon\mu$:

| band | condition | behavior | half-life (steps) | functional reading |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **latch** | $\mu = 0$ (symmetry-protected) | frozen displacement, dead momentum | $\infty$ | permanent register |
| **register** | $0 < h \lesssim \gamma/2$ | slow monotone leak | $\approx \dfrac{2\gamma\ln2}{(2-\gamma)h^2}$ | long-term memory, lifetime $\propto 1/\mu^2$ |
| **working memory** | $\gamma/2 \lesssim h < 2$ | oscillation at $\approx\mu$, envelope decay | $\approx \dfrac{2\ln2}{\gamma}$ (mass-independent) | fast, phase-carrying scratch |
| **chaos channel** | $\mu^2 < 0$ | escape; slowed, never held | doubling $\approx \dfrac{2\gamma\ln2}{(2-\gamma)g^2}$ | sensitivity/creativity; explosion risk |
| **forbidden** | $h > 2$ | integrator instability | — | must not exist (stiffness bound) |

Design rules the table dictates: **(1)** memory capacity at a target lifetime = how much of $W$'s spectrum $V_\theta$ can park below $\mu \approx \gamma/(2\varepsilon)$; **(2)** $\gamma$ moves the register/working-memory boundary *and* the working-memory lifetime simultaneously — one knob, two effects, expect trade-offs; **(3)** $\varepsilon$ rescales the entire $\mu$-axis including the forbidden zone — step size is part of the budget, not a mere accuracy setting.

# Part IV — Symmetry, charge, and Goldstone memory

## 4.1 Symmetry: what it is, and its ML name

**Philosophy.** A symmetry is a family of transformations that the energy cannot detect: $V(gq) = V(q)$ for all $g$ in a group $G$. **Continuous** symmetry means the family has a dial (rotations by any angle), not a switch (mirror flip). ML anchor: this is exactly **equivariance/invariance** as in CNNs and equivariant nets — weight sharing so the model literally cannot distinguish transformed inputs. Physics adds the punchline ML doesn't have: continuous indifference has *dynamical consequences* (Noether, §4.2) and *storage consequences* (Goldstone, §4.3).

**Look-up.** Group, representation, irreducible representation ("irrep"), multiplet. You need only the intuitions: a *representation* is "how the group acts on your vector space"; an *irrep* is a minimal invariant subspace ("a block that mixes only with itself"); a *multiplet* is a set of coordinates the group shuffles among themselves.

## 4.2 Noether's theorem and what "charge" means here

**Philosophy.** Noether: **every continuous symmetry forces a conserved quantity.** If the landscape genuinely cannot feel a direction, then motion along that direction is free, and there is a bookkeeping number — the **charge** — that the dynamics cannot change. Electric charge is the Noether charge of a phase symmetry (look-up: *U(1) symmetry*); momentum is the charge of translation symmetry; angular momentum of rotation symmetry. In the CLU, a charge is **the write current of a memory channel**: the thing you inject to move along the protected direction.

**Math — continuous version, with the condition everyone misses.** Take rotations in the $(q_1,q_2)$ plane; charge $Q = q_1p_2 - q_2p_1$ (angular momentum). Differentiate along Hamilton's flow:

$$\dot Q = \dot q_1p_2 + q_1\dot p_2 - \dot q_2 p_1 - q_2\dot p_1
= \underbrace{\Big(p_2\frac{\partial T}{\partial p_1} - p_1\frac{\partial T}{\partial p_2}\Big)}_{\text{kinetic torque}}
+ \underbrace{\Big(q_2\frac{\partial V}{\partial q_1} - q_1\frac{\partial V}{\partial q_2}\Big)}_{\text{potential torque}} .$$

The potential torque vanishes iff $V$ is rotation-invariant — the familiar condition. The **kinetic torque**, with $\partial T/\partial p_i = p_i/M_i$ (all our kinetic modes have $\nabla T \parallel M^{-1}p$):

$$p_2\frac{p_1}{M_1} - p_1\frac{p_2}{M_2} = p_1p_2\Big(\frac{1}{M_1} - \frac{1}{M_2}\Big) = 0 \iff M_1 = M_2 .$$

**A symmetric potential is not enough: the inertial masses inside a channel must be equal**, or the kinetic term explicitly breaks the symmetry. (The rep-theory version: invariance of $T$ requires $M$ to commute with the group action; Schur's lemma then forces $M \propto I$ on each irrep — the HEP statement "members of a multiplet share a common mass," look-up: *Schur's lemma*, *multiplet mass degeneracy*.)

**Math — discrete version: the map conserves the charge *exactly* [proven, verified (g)].** Check each substep of §1.6:

- **Kick** ($\delta p = -\tfrac{\varepsilon}{2}\nabla V$): $\delta Q = q_1\delta p_2 - q_2\delta p_1 = -\tfrac{\varepsilon}{2}\,(q_1\partial_2V - q_2\partial_1V) = 0$ for invariant $V$ (zero torque, *pointwise*).
- **Drift** ($\delta q = \varepsilon\nabla T$): $\delta Q = \delta q_1p_2 - \delta q_2p_1 = \varepsilon\, p_1p_2\big(\tfrac1{M_1}-\tfrac1{M_2}\big) = 0$ under mass isotropy.
- **Friction** ($p \to (1-\gamma)p$): $Q$ is linear in $p$, so $Q \to (1-\gamma)Q$, exactly.

Hence at $\gamma = 0$: exact conservation at machine precision, any step size — not "small drift," none. At $\gamma > 0$: the **exact decay law** $Q_n = (1-\gamma)^nQ_0$. **[verified: Mexican-hat potential, equal masses: relative drift $3.0\times10^{-14}$ over $10^5$ steps; unequal masses $M = (1,2)$: drift $2.6$ — order one, the symmetry is really broken; damped case: decay-law error $9.3\times10^{-16}$]**

**→ CLU.** Two program-critical consequences. (1) *The V2 trap:* an SO(2)-channel experiment with per-coordinate learned `log_mass` can silently break its own symmetry through the kinetic term no matter how equivariant $V_\theta$ is — constrain $M$ in-channel, or measure whether learning isotropizes it (a free falsifiable). (2) *Charge ≠ memory* — next section.

## 4.3 Spontaneous symmetry breaking: where the memory actually lives

**Philosophy.** **Spontaneous symmetry breaking (SSB):** the *laws* are symmetric but the *resting state* cannot be — a pencil balanced on its tip must fall in *some* direction; a Mexican-hat potential must rest at *some* point on the rim. The set of equally-good resting states (the rim) is the **vacuum manifold**. Moving along it costs nothing: those are the **Goldstone modes** — guaranteed massless ($\mu = 0$) directions, one per broken symmetry dial (look-up: *Goldstone theorem*, *order parameter*, *vacuum manifold*). This is the physics fact V2 is built on, because §3.2 already told us what $\mu = 0$ means under our map: a latch.

**Math — the standard example, computed.** $V(q) = \tfrac{\lambda}{4}\big(\lVert q\rVert^2 - f^2\big)^2$ in 2D ("Mexican hat"; $f$ = rim radius). Gradient: $\nabla V = \lambda(\lVert q\rVert^2 - f^2)\,q$ — zero on the whole circle $\lVert q\rVert = f$. Hessian: $\partial_i\partial_jV = \lambda\big[2q_iq_j + (\lVert q\rVert^2 - f^2)\delta_{ij}\big]$; at the vacuum $(f, 0)$:

$$K = \begin{pmatrix} 2\lambda f^2 & 0\\ 0 & 0\end{pmatrix}
\qquad\Rightarrow\qquad
\mu^2_{\rm radial} = \frac{2\lambda f^2}{M}, \qquad \mu^2_{\rm angular} = 0 .$$

One stiff ("Higgs-like") mode pulling you back to the rim; one exactly flat mode along the rim. The rim coordinate — the angle $\vartheta$ — is the **memory register**; $f$ (the **decay constant**, HEP look-up: *pion decay constant*) is the size/stiffness of the dial.

**The division of labor, exactly [proven, verified (g)]:**

- the **angle** $\vartheta$ stores the value — under friction it *freezes* (the latch, now on a genuinely curved global manifold, not a linearization);
- the **charge** $Q$ *writes* the value — injecting angular momentum moves $\vartheta$; friction then spends the charge exactly as $(1-\gamma)^n$, and the accumulated rotation is the committed write;
- the **radial mode** is disposable scratch — it decays at the massive-mode rate of §3.3 and lands the state back on the manifold.

**Numbers** (Mexican hat, $\gamma = 0.005$): angle frozen to $2.3\times10^{-9}$ over the last 4000 steps; radius on the vacuum circle to $3.4\times10^{-10}$; charge followed $(1-\gamma)^nQ_0$ to $9.3\times10^{-16}$.

**→ CLU.** The tidiest one-sentence statement of V2: **memory lives on the vacuum manifold; the Noether charge is its write current; friction is the write-settle mechanism; the radial sector is self-cleaning scratch.** All four clauses are exact properties of the coded map.

## 4.4 Channel counting: how many registers a symmetry buys

**Philosophy.** Break a bigger symmetry, get more flat directions. The count is pure group arithmetic: if the energy is invariant under group $\mathcal G$ and the resting state is still invariant under a subgroup $\mathcal H \subset \mathcal G$ (the transformations that don't move it), the number of Goldstone registers is

$$\#\text{channels} = \dim\mathcal G - \dim\mathcal H = \dim(\mathcal G/\mathcal H).$$

(The Standard Model's famous "3" is the instance $SU(2)\times U(1) \to U(1)$: $4 - 1 = 3$. Look-up: *coset space* $\mathcal G/\mathcal H$, *electroweak symmetry breaking*.) Choosing $\mathcal G$, its representation, and the breaking pattern is **channel allocation by symmetry engineering** — memory capacity as a design-time group-theory decision. Caveat for us: learned potentials can also have *accidental* flat directions; those are real but unprotected (nothing forbids training from lifting them later). **[proven counting; engineering is a design hypothesis]**

> **Update (wave-13) — the counting above is CORRECT; the *universality-class label* it invites is not.** When this coset is put on a lattice (§8.6), the resulting statistical model is the **O($n$) model with**
> $$\boxed{\ n = \dim(\mathcal G/\mathcal H) + 1\ }$$
> **not** $n = \dim(\mathcal G/\mathcal H)$. The O($n$) model's spins live on $S^{n-1}$ = *the vacuum manifold itself*, and the Goldstone count is $\dim(\mathcal G/\mathcal H) = n-1$. So: $\mathbb Z_2$ double-well (0 Goldstones) → **Ising, O(1)**; $SO(2)$, $\mathcal M = S^1$ (1 Goldstone) → **XY, O(2)**; $SO(3)/SO(2)$, $\mathcal M = S^2$ (2 Goldstones) → **Heisenberg, O(3)**. Writing "$n = \dim(\mathcal G/\mathcal H)$" would label our $SO(2)$ register $n=1$ = **Ising** — the exact error this primer's own discipline forbids. **And O($n$) universality applies only when the coset is a *sphere*:** an abelian torus bank $T^k$ is **$k$ decoupled XY models**, *not* an O($k{+}1$) model; in general the class is that of the $\mathcal M$-valued sigma model. **[proven — `xy-lattice-theory` §3.2, correcting the Hub's Thread-10 write-up]**

> **Update (wave-13) — do the flat directions *emerge*? No.** The "accidental flat directions" caveat above is now measured, and it is worse than a caveat. On emergent (MLP) checkpoints the softest coset direction is **only 1.7–4.9× softer than the *stiffest* mode in the whole spectrum** ($1-|\lambda_{\rm coset}| = 1.06\times10^{-3}\ldots2.96\times10^{-3}$, vs $\le 1.1\times10^{-15}$ designed — **~12 orders**), and any written coset value relaxes **completely** home ($|{\rm retained}| \le 2.1\times10^{-3}$). Its capacity is $\log_2(2\text{–}3\ \text{washboard minima}) \approx$ **1–1.6 bits**, not a continuum. **⇒ Channel allocation is not something training discovers; it is something you build.** **[measured, 3 seeds — `v5-gate` §3.1–3.3; negative N46]**

## 4.5 Pseudo-Goldstones: imperfect symmetry, graceful forgetting

**Philosophy.** Perfect symmetry is an idealization; data, task gradients, or deliberate design will tilt the rim slightly (**explicit breaking**, as opposed to spontaneous). A tilted rim is no longer flat: the mode acquires a *small* spectral mass, and the register leaks — slowly, predictably. HEP's canonical instance: the pion is the pseudo-Goldstone of chiral symmetry broken explicitly by small quark masses, with $m_\pi^2 \propto m_q$ (look-up: *pseudo-Goldstone boson*, *Gell-Mann–Oakes–Renner (GMOR) relation*, *explicit vs spontaneous breaking*, *spurion*).

**Math.** Tilt the Mexican hat by $\Delta V = \delta\,(1 - \cos n\vartheta)$ ($n$ minima around the rim, depth $\delta$). Arc-length coordinate $s = f\vartheta$ (the canonical one); curvature at a minimum:

$$\frac{d^2\Delta V}{ds^2}\Big|_{s=0} = \frac{\delta\, n^2}{f^2}
\qquad\Rightarrow\qquad
\mu^2_{\rm pG} = \frac{\delta\,n^2}{M f^2} \;\propto\; \delta,$$

the GMOR pattern (mass² linear in the breaking). Feed this to §3.3's overdamped law:

$$n_{1/2} \approx \frac{2\gamma\ln 2}{(2-\gamma)\,\varepsilon^2}\cdot\frac{Mf^2}{\delta n^2} \;\propto\; \frac{1}{\mu^2} \;\propto\; \frac1\delta,$$

with the saturation floor at $2\ln2/\gamma$ once $\varepsilon\mu > h^*$. **Graceful degradation, quantified**: halve the symmetry violation, double the memory lifetime — until the underdamped floor. Note also what $f$ buys: a larger memory dial suppresses *all* breaking-induced masses by $1/f^2$ — stiffness of the vacuum is robustness of the register.

**→ CLU.** This is the falsifiable core of the V2 experiment: train with a controlled breaking $\delta$, measure retention vs. $\delta$, look for the $-1$ power law *and* the floor. The floor is the distinctive signature — a rival theory without our discrete-map analysis wouldn't predict it.

>

Measured, at written sites: $\texttt{tilt\_vacuum\_residual} = \sum_j w_j(\hat u\cdot\hat n_j)^2 = \mathbf{0.140}$ (envelope) / $\mathbf{0.343}$ (depth), 0.197–0.339 across race arms — against an **ideal of 0** and a **random-orientation baseline of $1/\mathrm{dim} = 0.167$**; a group's atom-centre spread is **1.19–1.95× the designed shell radius**. ⇒ **a written site is at or WORSE than random as a common vacuum of its own atoms.** *The rim you tilted is not the rim the store built.*
