## 4.6–4.7 Parameterizing the memory manifold: linear vs nonlinear realization (SMEFT-like vs HEFT-like)

**Philosophy.** Suppose you've decided the latent should contain a memory manifold $\mathcal G/\mathcal H$. Two ways to build it:

- **Linear realization ("SMEFT-like").** Keep the ambient space; make $V_\theta$ a function of group *invariants* (an invariant/equivariant network). The symmetry acts linearly on coordinates; the manifold *emerges* as the minimum set; radial modes exist and are heavy. This is "the Higgs field lives in a linear multiplet." (Look-up: *linear sigma model*, *SMEFT* — Standard Model EFT.)
- **Nonlinear realization ("HEFT-like").** Discard the radial direction; let coordinates live *on* the manifold itself (angles/coset coordinates); the symmetry acts by shifting them — nonlinearly in any chart. "There is no Higgs multiplet, only the rim." (Look-up: *nonlinear sigma model*, *coset construction / CCWZ*, *HEFT* — Higgs EFT.)

The physics folklore: HEFT is the more agnostic parameterization; SMEFT the more predictive one; **HEFT = the limit of SMEFT where the radial mode is infinitely heavy** (look-up: *decoupling limit*).

**Math — the implementation constraint that decides it for us.** Write the 2D example in polar coordinates $q = r(\cos\vartheta, \sin\vartheta)$. The kinetic energy of a Newtonian particle of mass $M$:

$$T = \frac{M}{2}\big(\dot r^2 + r^2\dot\vartheta^2\big) .$$

Freeze the radial mode at $r = f$ (the HEFT move): $T = \tfrac12\,(Mf^2)\,\dot\vartheta^2$ — the angle's inertia is $Mf^2$. Fine in 2D; but on a general coset the induced metric is **position-dependent**: $T = \tfrac12\,\dot\phi^\top g(\phi)\,\dot\phi$, i.e. in Hamiltonian form a **position-dependent mass matrix** $M(q)$, i.e. a *non-separable* $H(q,p) = \tfrac12p^\top g(q)^{-1}p + V$. §1.2's standing scope guard fires: our explicit integrator loses exact symplecticity, and the constant-diagonal `log_mass` cannot represent $g(q)$ at all. Options: (i) constrained integrators on the manifold (look-up: *RATTLE/SHAKE*), (ii) implicit generalized leapfrog, or (iii) **stay linear with a deliberately heavy radial mode** and inherit HEFT as its decoupling limit, with corrections controlled by $1/\mu^2_{\rm radial}$ — implementable *today* with equivariant $V_\theta$ + the §4.2 mass-isotropy constraint.

**→ CLU.** Route (iii) is the pragmatic V2 recommendation; routes (i)/(ii) are real engineering projects to price before promising the colleague a true sigma-model latent. This subsection is precisely the collaboration surface with the Manchester HEFT theorist: the vocabulary above is their native language.

## 4.8 The EFT of memory: principled coarse-graining

**Philosophy.** Effective field theory is physics' formalization of "you don't need the fast microscopic details to predict slow behavior — but their *imprint* survives as small, systematically organized corrections." You pick an accuracy budget, **integrate out** everything faster than your cutoff, and get: a simpler theory for the slow modes + a tower of correction terms with coefficients (**Wilson coefficients**) suppressed by powers of (slow scale / fast scale) (**power counting**). Explicit breaking enters via **spurions** — bookkeeping fields that mark exactly how the symmetry fails. (Look-ups: *effective field theory*, *integrating out*, *matching*, *power counting*, *Wilson coefficient*, *spurion analysis*. ML anchors: model distillation; adiabatic elimination of fast variables; multiscale modeling.)

**The corrected direction (Def-2 discipline).** What gets integrated out is the **fast = large-$\mu$** sector — which in allocator language is the *light-inertial-mass or stiff* sector. Coarse-grained inference *keeps* the heavy-$M$ slow backbone. Mechanically this is **Born–Oppenheimer** (fast modes instantaneously minimize out, leaving an effective potential on slow coordinates) plus, at next order, **Mori–Zwanzig** (eliminated modes leave memory kernels and effective noise — friction and temperature *emerge* from coarse-graining, pleasingly consistent with our γ/T levers). (Look-ups: *Born–Oppenheimer approximation*, *Mori–Zwanzig formalism*.)

**What the EFT program would deliver for memory (sketch, [conjectured program]):** an effective theory on the memory manifold where — leading order: free Goldstone latches; power-suppressed operators: anharmonic corrections to retention curves and mode–mode "memory crosstalk" scaled by $1/\mu^2_{\rm heavy}$; spurion terms: pseudo-Goldstone masses ⇒ the §4.5 lifetimes. I.e. a *systematic expansion for how real, imperfect CLU memory deviates from the ideal latch* — with fit-able coefficients. This is the possible fourth short (V2b) with the colleague.

## 4.9 Modulus vs Goldstone: which object actually protects the register **[proven, verified — deep-dive S5; new wave-13]**

**Philosophy.** Everywhere above we have said "the register is a Goldstone mode." That is *almost* right, and the almost matters — because it decides **what you are allowed to break.** A physicist distinguishes two reasons a direction can be flat:

- **Goldstone** — a flat direction protected by a symmetry of the **full** Hamiltonian $H = T + V$. Protecting it therefore requires the *kinetic* term to respect the symmetry too (kinetic isotropy, $[M, X] = 0$; §4.2's Schur constraint).
- **Modulus** — a flat direction of the **potential alone**, $V$. It may be protected by $V$'s symmetry, or it may be accidental. Nothing is asked of $T(p)$. (Look-ups: *moduli space*, *flat direction*, *pseudo-modulus*; in SUSY phenomenology "modulus" is exactly this: a direction along which the potential is exactly flat.)

**The CLU fact.** Read §3.2's latch derivation again and notice what it actually used: along a direction where $\nabla V = 0$, the momentum decays as $p_n = (1-\gamma)^n p_0$ and the position converges. **It used $V$-flatness and $\gamma > 0$. It never used a symmetry of $T$.**

$$\boxed{\ \text{$V$-flatness} + \gamma>0 \ \Longrightarrow\ \text{a neutral, non-decaying register.}\ }$$

**Math — the two objects come apart, and we have already measured them apart.** Take $\mathcal G = SO(2)$ acting on $(q_1,q_2)$ with generator $X$, an invariant $V$, and an **anisotropic** inertial mass $M = \mathrm{diag}(M_1, M_2)$, $M_1 \ne M_2$:

- The Noether **charge** $Q_X = p^\top Xq$ is **no longer conserved** — $\dot Q_X = p^\top(XM^{-1} - M^{-1}X)p \ne 0$ — because $[M, X] \ne 0$. The *symmetry* is broken by the kinetic term.
- The **register** survives anyway: $V$ is still flat along the orbit, so $\nabla V = 0$ there, so the latch still latches, exactly. The vacuum manifold $\times\ \{p=0\}$ are exact fixed points of the damped map **for any $M$** (F5's global statement).

> **The symmetry buys the *charge* (the write current). The potential's flatness buys the *register* (the stored value).** Two different objects, two different protections. Break kinetic isotropy and you lose the clean write mechanism, not the memory.

**Consequence — equivariance is *sufficient but not necessary* for neutral memory.** This is a free, sharp differentiator against the Goldstone-propagation literature (Mo/Welling-style results assume an **equivariant map**; F5 Prop-16 adapts their Thm-1 under that assumption). **V2's own broken-isotropy battery is an unremarked counterexample to the necessity direction**: those runs are *not* equivariant ($E^T_{\rm eq} = 0.0016\text{–}0.108$) and they **latch anyway**. We were sitting on the counterexample and calling it a control. **[proven; verified — and already *measured* in `v2-full-runs` item 5]**

**Why the distinction has teeth (three places it changes an answer):**
1. **§4.2's kinetic-isotropy constraint is about the charge law, not the latch.** Diagnose kinetic breaking by measuring $Q_X$, *never* by checking whether the register still holds (it will).
2. **Accidental flatness is a real modulus** — it stores just as well as a symmetric one, and is exactly as unprotected against training lifting it later. (§4.4's update: on emergent nets it *is* lifted, by ~12 orders.)
3. **Thermodynamics doesn't care.** The momentum integral factorises for **any** $T(p)$, so the inertial mass matrix and the kinetic mode drop out of the Gibbs measure entirely (§8.6). `tie_channel_mass` is a **dynamical** (Noether) requirement, not a thermodynamic one. **[proven; verified 1.0002–1.0009 at a 6.25× channel-mass ratio]**

**→ CLU.** The tightest honest one-liner for V2: ***CLU's memory is a modulus with a Goldstone's paperwork*** — flatness of $V$ protects the register; the symmetry protects the current that writes it.

### 4.9.1 Nomenclature ledger entry (binding, per Def-2 discipline)

| term | means, in this program | do **not** use it for |
|---|---|---|
| **Goldstone (mode)** | a flat direction of $V$ **whose flatness is enforced by a symmetry of the full $H = T + V$** — i.e. $V$-symmetric **and** kinetically isotropic ($[M,X]=0$) | any flat direction whose kinetic sector is anisotropic |
| **modulus** | a flat direction of **$V$ alone** (symmetric or accidental). **This is what the CLU latch actually is.** Sufficient for a neutral register | the *charge*; a modulus carries no conserved current unless it is also a Goldstone |
| **pseudo-Goldstone** | a modulus lifted by **explicit** breaking $\delta$ ⇒ small spectral mass $\mu^2 \propto \delta$ (§4.5) | an *emergent* soft mode: those are **massive modes**, not pseudo-Goldstones (their $\mu^2$ is mid-spectrum, N46) |
| **Noether charge $Q_X = p^\top Xq$** | the **write current**; conserved exactly iff $[M,X]=0$ | a proxy for whether the register is holding — it is not (§4.9 point 1) |
| **inertial mass $M$** (Def-2) | the learned per-coordinate learning rate of the state | never bare "mass" |
| **spectral mass $\mu$** (Def-2) | $\sqrt{\lambda(M_{\rm eff}^{-1}K)}$ — the forgetting rate | never bare "mass" |
| **decay constant $F$** | $F = \sqrt{M_{\rm ch}}\,r^*$, via $Q = F^2\dot\theta$ at **zero momentum**; $F^2 = M_{\rm ch}r^{*2}$ is an **inertia** | ⚠ **never** the spin stiffness $\rho_s$ (§8.6) — that is the *exchange* $J$ |

# Part V — Heat: temperature, noise, and the sampler we actually shipped

## 5.1 Temperature and the Boltzmann distribution

**Philosophy.** Temperature is the size of random kicks the environment gives the state. A noisy, damped physical system doesn't settle at the minimum; it settles into a *distribution* concentrated near low energies:

$$P(q,p) \propto e^{-H(q,p)/T},$$

the **Boltzmann/Gibbs distribution**. High $T$: exploration, barrier crossing; $T \to 0$: greedy settling. ML anchor: this is exactly the EBM sampling story (SGLD/Langevin sampling of $e^{-E}$), and simulated annealing is the $T$-schedule version. The CLU's generative mode *is* this: anneal $T$ so noise crystallizes into a low-energy configuration of $V_\theta$ (Exp III/MNIST).

**Math — equipartition in one line.** For $T(p) = p^2/2M$ the momentum marginal of Gibbs is Gaussian $\propto e^{-p^2/2MT}$, so

$$\mathrm{Var}(p_i) = M_iT \qquad\text{(equivalently } \langle p_i^2/2M_i\rangle = T/2\text{)}.$$

(Look-up: *equipartition theorem*.) Keep this line in view — it is the acceptance test the code's sampler fails.

## 5.2 Langevin dynamics and the fluctuation–dissipation theorem **[proven, verified (e)]**

**Philosophy.** To sample Gibbs you add *both* friction and noise: friction drains energy, noise injects it, and only one precise pairing makes them balance at temperature $T$. That pairing is the **fluctuation–dissipation theorem (FDT)**: noise and friction are two faces of one heat bath and their magnitudes are locked to each other. Get the lock wrong and you still get *a* stationary distribution — just not the one you advertised. (Look-ups: *Langevin equation*, *fluctuation–dissipation theorem*, *Ornstein–Uhlenbeck process*; ML: *SGLD*; best-practice integrators: *BAOAB*, Leimkuhler–Matthews.)

**The code's step** (`langevin_step`): full Verlet step, then

$$p \leftarrow (1-\gamma)\,p + \sigma\,\xi,\qquad \xi \sim\mathcal N(0, I),\qquad \sigma_{\rm code} = \sqrt{2\gamma T\varepsilon}\ \ (\text{uniform across coordinates}).$$

**Math — the exact discrete balance.** Isolate the damp+noise ("O") sub-step, an AR(1) process $p' = ap + \sigma\xi$ with $a = 1-\gamma$. Stationary variance solves $v = a^2v + \sigma^2$:

$$v_\infty = \frac{\sigma^2}{1 - (1-\gamma)^2} = \frac{\sigma^2}{\gamma(2-\gamma)} .$$

Demand equipartition $v_\infty = M_iT$ and solve for the noise:

$$\boxed{\ \sigma_i^\star = \sqrt{M_iT\,\gamma(2-\gamma)}\ }\qquad\text{(exact discrete FDT)} .$$

Compare the shipped $\sigma_{\rm code} = \sqrt{2\gamma T\varepsilon}$: it produces $v_\infty = \tfrac{2T\varepsilon}{2-\gamma}$, i.e. an **effective per-mode temperature**

$$T_{{\rm eff},i} = \frac{v_\infty}{M_i} = \frac{2\,\varepsilon\,T}{(2-\gamma)\,M_i} \ \ne\ T .$$

Where the slip came from: the continuous SDE $dp = -\gamma_c p\,dt + \sqrt{2\gamma_cM T}\,dW$ discretizes (Euler–Maruyama) to noise std $\sqrt{2\gamma_cMT\,dt} = \sqrt{2\gamma MT}$ (with $\gamma = \gamma_c dt$). The code has $\sqrt{2\gamma T\,dt}$: **$M$ was swapped for $dt$** — a units-level slip that the "$\sqrt{2\gamma T dt}$" folklore formula invites.

**Observed** (harmonic well, $m=2$, $k=1$, $\varepsilon = .05$, $\gamma = .1$, $T = .5$): code noise → $\mathrm{Var}(p) = 0.0263$ vs Maxwell–Boltzmann target $mT = 1.0$ (predicted $T_{\rm eff} = 0.0132$ vs nominal $0.5$); corrected noise → $\mathrm{Var}(p) = 1.000000$ (exact discrete Lyapunov-equation solve) and $0.9994$ (long simulation). Bonus precision: with corrected noise, $\mathrm{Var}(q) = 0.500156$, which is *not* $T/k = 0.5$ but exactly $T/\tilde k$ with $\tilde k = k(1 - h^2/4)$ the **shadow curvature** of §1.7 — the corrected chain samples the Gibbs measure of the shadow Hamiltonian, an $O(\varepsilon^2)$-tilted (and precisely known) version of the target. If exactness matters, Metropolis-adjust (look-up: *Metropolis-adjusted Langevin*, *HMC*) or shrink $\varepsilon$.

**Consequences, in order of practical bite:**
1. Code "temperatures" are not in energy units ($\varepsilon$, $M$ absorbed): annealing schedules still work (monotone rescale), but $T$ values are not comparable across step sizes, masses, or architectures.
2. With *learned, non-uniform* $M$ (Exp C: relativistic conv model), **each mode equilibrates at its own temperature** — FDT is violated per-mode and there is *no* Gibbs invariant $e^{-H/T}$ at all. This was floated as the candidate explanation for the MNIST digit-mode imbalance — **refuted in w3, RE-OPENED in w13, UPHELD on a nearly-correct instrument in w14, and CLOSED in w16 on the *exact* F2 sampler (§5.3 wave-16 update): the imbalance is the landscape, never the sampler.**
> **Update (wave-5 audit; source w3) — the imbalance conjecture is REFUTED; the mechanism is confirmed exact.** `generative-studies.md` Study A ran the test on 3 real Exp-C checkpoints. The **Prop-9 mechanism is exactly right** on trained models: legacy sampling is mass-blind ($\rho(T_{\rm eff}, 1/M)=0.994$; $\mathrm{Var}(p)$ vs closed-form ≤0.06%), and the $\sigma^\star_i$ fix restores a single temperature (slope 1.000, T flat to 0.8% CV; $T_{\rm eff}\propto\varepsilon$ verified). **But the imbalance attribution is refuted:** scale-matched, correcting the per-mode violation changes essentially nothing generated ($\chi^2=0.08$, $p=1.00$; **2/192 mode-flips**). The digit imbalance is a property of the **learned energy landscape** (basin depths/volumes + annealed relaxation from the centroid), not the sampler. *Why the violation is harmless today:* the mass spectrum is too narrow (max/min ≈1.5 ⇒ temperature anisotropy ≤±13%) — the same "mass-narrowness pivot" as §2.3. **Conditional promotion:** the `fdt` flag is cosmetic *now* but becomes load-bearing the moment V3 banding or mass-aware objectives create a real hierarchy. (Side-finding: no *positional* equilibrium under either sampler — mean $H$ falls monotonically and chains exit the trained cube — a direct consequence of the non-coercive Conv/Deep potentials, §7.7/Prop-10; any long-free-run generative or energy-anomaly claim is valid only for the momentum marginal on current checkpoints.)
3. The fix is one line (per-mode $\sigma_i^\star$), but retrained temperature hyperparameters will shift — ship behind a compat flag.
4. Relativistic $T(p)$: even the corrected O-step targets a *Gaussian* momentum marginal, while relativistic Gibbs wants $\propto e^{-T(p)/T}$ (non-Gaussian) — ~~residual bias unquantified (F5 Open-3)~~ **→ this is no longer an "unquantified residual bias." It is a proven no-go, and it is §5.3.**

> **⚠ Update (wave-13) — the "refuted" in consequence 2 is SUSPENDED, and the reason is §5.3.** The w3 refutation compared `legacy` vs scale-matched `fdt` **on Exp-C checkpoints**, and Exp-C runs `kinetic_mode="relativistic"` (`config.py:220`). By §5.3, **neither arm had a Gibbs invariant** — the comparison was between **two non-Gibbs samplers**, using a **variance-level instrument blind to a tail-shape error.** The sampler-bias hypothesis has therefore **never been tested with a nearly-correct sampler.** Negative **N10 is re-opened, UNDER RE-TEST** (→ `relativistic-gibbs-expc`, F-9; cheap, checkpoints exist). *Nothing about the Prop-9 mechanism on Newtonian modes is in doubt* — slope 1.000, $T$ flat to 0.8% CV, all reproduces. What is in doubt is the **attribution verdict** only.

> **Update (wave-13) — F5 Prop-9's $\sigma^\star$ needs a kinetic-mode qualifier everywhere it appears.** The boxed $\sigma_i^\star = \sqrt{M_iT\gamma(2-\gamma)}$ above is **exact for the Newtonian kinetic modes and for those only.** In `relativistic` mode **no $\sigma$ works at all** (§5.3). Prop-9 → **Prop-9′ (a no-go)**; V2 App-F's fine print inherits the qualifier. *As with the first corrigendum, the corrected statement is stronger than the false one: "no $\sigma$ exists" is a sharper theorem than "here is $\sigma^\star$."*

**→ CLU.** The generative vertical and any "energy-calibrated anomaly score" claim rides on the sampler being the sampler we say it is. This is the highest-priority code fix after the dead regularizer.

## 5.3 Why you must damp the **velocity**, not the momentum: the linear-OU vs Maxwell–Jüttner no-go **[proven, verified — deep-dive §7bis R8; new wave-13]**

> *This is the most ML-legible physics lesson of wave-13. It is also the second time this program has inferred physics from a sampler it had not validated (§10.3, and the ledger's wave-13 addendum). Read it as a lesson about instruments, not about relativity.*

**Philosophy.** Langevin sampling has a division of labour: the **kinetic term** decides how a state *moves*, and the **damping + noise** decide what distribution it *settles into*. The two are coupled by FDT. Every textbook writes the damping as acting on the **velocity** $\dot q = \nabla_p T(p)$ — because friction is a force opposing *motion*, and motion is velocity. For a **Newtonian** kinetic term this is a distinction without a difference:

$$T(p) = \tfrac{1}{2}p^\top M^{-1}p \quad\Longrightarrow\quad \nabla_p T = M^{-1}p \ \propto\ p .$$

Damping the velocity and damping the momentum are the same operation up to the constant $M^{-1}$ (absorb it: $\Gamma = \gamma M$). **The moment the kinetic term stops being quadratic, they are different operations — and only one of them samples Gibbs.**

**The code damps $p$.** `langevin_step`'s O-step is $p \leftarrow (1-\gamma)p + \sigma\xi$. Under the relativistic governor,

$$T(p) = \sqrt{c^2 p^\top M^{-1}p + m_0^2c^4}\ ,\qquad \nabla_p T \ \propto\ \frac{p}{T(p)}\ \ne\ \frac{p}{\rm const} .$$

So the coded step damps the wrong object, and FDT is violated *structurally* — not by a mis-set constant.

**Math — the no-go, in three lines.** Take a free particle ($V = 0$). The O-step is then the whole dynamics in $p$, and it is an **autonomous, linear** recursion:

$$p_{n+1} = (1-\gamma)p_n + \sigma\xi_n \qquad\text{(an AR(1) / Ornstein–Uhlenbeck process).}$$

A linear recursion driven by Gaussian noise has a **Gaussian** stationary law — exactly, for every $\sigma$: $p_\infty \sim \mathcal N\!\big(0, \sigma^2/(\gamma(2-\gamma))\big)$. But the Gibbs momentum marginal for a relativistic $T(p)$ is the **Maxwell–Jüttner** distribution,

$$\pi(p) \ \propto\ \exp\!\Big(-\tfrac{c}{T}\sqrt{p^\top M^{-1}p + m_0^2c^2}\,\Big),$$

which is **not Gaussian** — it has *exponential* tails, not Gaussian ones. A Gaussian stationary law cannot equal a non-Gaussian target.

$$\boxed{\ \textbf{No choice of }\sigma\textbf{ gives the coded relativistic Langevin a Gibbs invariant.}\ }\qquad\blacksquare$$

(Look-ups: *Maxwell–Jüttner distribution*, *relativistic Ornstein–Uhlenbeck process*, *Ornstein–Uhlenbeck stationary law*, *BAOAB*. The correct relativistic O-step damps $\nabla_pT$ and its noise is state-dependent; alternatively, refresh $p$ from Maxwell–Jüttner exactly.)

**The defect is controlled by one dimensionless number.** Everything depends on $T/(m_0c^2)$ — the temperature in units of the rest energy, i.e. *how relativistic the bath is*:

| $T/(m_0c^2)$ | $\mathrm{Var}_{\rm MJ}/(M_{\rm eff}T)$ | excess kurtosis | $\mathrm{KL}(\text{MJ}\,\|\,\text{Gauss})$ |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0.01 | 1.0150 | 0.030 | $7.4\times10^{-5}$ |
| 0.10 | 1.1534 | 0.295 | $6.8\times10^{-3}$ |
| **1.00** | **2.6995** | **1.857** | **0.384 nats** |
| 8.00 | 16.282 | 2.907 | 6.31 nats |

**Verified two ways.** (i) *Dynamically*, in a harmonic well: $\mathrm{Var}(q)/(T/k) - 1 = -0.0036$ for the **Newtonian control at every $T$** (that is just the $O(\varepsilon^2)$ shadow floor of §1.7), versus $-0.073 / -0.115 / -0.313 / -0.538 / -0.729$ for relativistic at $T/(m_0c^2) = 0.056 / 0.1 / 0.5 / 2 / 8$. The two configurations sharing $T/(m_0c^2) = 8$ — namely $(c{=}1, T{=}8)$ and $(c{=}0.5, T{=}2)$ — give **bit-identical** $-0.7290074$: *the defect is a function of $T/(m_0c^2)$ alone.* (ii) *On a real lattice code path* (`xy-lattice-theory` §5(v)): $\langle\cos\Delta\theta\rangle/\text{Gibbs} = 1.006 / 1.040 / \mathbf{1.134}$ at $T = 0.02/0.05/0.10$, with **drift $\approx 0$** ⇒ this is a **genuinely different stationary measure, not a slow transient.**

**⚠ The subtlety that is easy to get backwards.** The exact **Gibbs measure** *is* relativity-insensitive: $p$ is a flat Cartesian coordinate, so $\int dp\, e^{-T(p)/T}$ is $q$-independent **for any** $T(p)$, and the configurational marginal is exactly $e^{-V(q)/T}$ regardless of the kinetic mode or the mass matrix. **The failure is in the *sampler*, not in the *thermodynamics*.** (Corollary, useful: the inertial mass matrix and the kinetic mode do not affect the equilibrium statistics **at all** — they affect only the dynamics.)

**Where this bites us, in order.**
1. **`experiment_c` — the published Exp III — runs relativistic at $T/(m_0c^2) = 1$** ($m_0 = c = 1$, `sleep_temperature = 0.5`, anneal $1.0\to0.01$): the true momentum law has **2.70×** the variance the sampler enforces, KL = **0.384 nats**. The paper-run project `finalA` used $c = 5 \Rightarrow T/(m_0c^2) = 0.04$ — benign, **which may be why it behaved better.**
2. **Negative N10 is weakened, not refuted** (§5.2 update above): both arms of its comparison were non-Gibbs.
3. **Thread-10's "FDT noise + $\gamma>0$" prerequisite is satisfiable only in Newtonian mode** (§8.6's P4).
4. **F5 Prop-9 is Newtonian-only** and must be re-scoped.

**Fixes, in increasing cost.** ~~(iii) **Raise $c$ (or $m_0$) so $T \ll m_0c^2$** — *free*, one config line, and already validated by `finalA`;~~ (ii) Metropolis-adjust the chain; (i) replace the O-step with an exact Maxwell–Jüttner momentum refresh.

> **Update (wave-14) — the fix ranking is CORRECTED, and "raise $c$" is NOT free.** Two w14 reports (`f5-corrigendum-2`, `relativistic-gibbs-expc`) showed the control parameter is **$d\cdot\Theta$, not $\Theta$** ($\Theta := T/(m_0c^2)$): ultra-relativistically $\mathrm{Var}_{\rm MJ}/(M_{\rm eff}T) \to (d{+}1)\Theta$, so at **Exp-C ($d{=}784$) the defect is $785\times$ at $c{=}1$ and $31.4\times$ at $c{=}5$** ($\mathrm{KL} = 3.24\times10^6 / 3.05\times10^5$ nats — *not* the $0.384$ a $d{=}1$ table implies). Raising $c$ needs $c \gtrsim \sqrt{dT/m_0} \approx 28$ at Exp-C, is $O(d\Theta)$-biased, and **is empirically refuted as a native fix** — on a potential trained at $c{=}5$ it makes the imbalance *worse* and under `legacy` noise collapses Exp-C onto the deterministic mode (N56). **The exact cheap fix is the latent-mass thermostat (F2, new):** Maxwell–Jüttner is a Gaussian scale mixture, so draw $s\mid p \sim \mathrm{InvGauss}(\cdot)$ and run the *same* linear Gaussian O-step at variance $M/(2s)$ — the exact relativistic FDT noise is the coded Gaussian noise with a **randomized inertia equal to the relativistic mass $m_0\gamma_{\rm Lorentz}$**; Prop-9's $\sigma^\star$ is its $d\Theta\to0$ limit (bias $-0.727 \to +0.0011$). **Corrected ranking: F1 (approximate, needs $d\Theta \ll 1$) / F2 (exact, cheap) / F3 (exact MJ refresh).** Blast radius: Prop-MH1 step 1 (CM-14) is false in relativistic mode, one-line repairable. See §10.3 (N56/N57) and the ledger's w14 addendum.

> **Update (wave-15, `scout-relativistic-samplers` + `fix-pack-7`) — teach this no-go as ESTABLISHED, not as ours; and the exact fix now SHIPS.** A prior-art pass (`scout-relativistic-samplers`) tested whether the no-go and the thermostat could carry a standalone methods-paper, and the honest answer is **no** — the *only* new object is the $d\cdot\Theta$ amplification (N59). Read this section as pedagogy about a **known** result, with the CLU-specific dimensional twist highlighted:
> - **The no-go is a known corollary.** *"An additive fixed-covariance Gaussian kick as the last substep ⇒ a Gaussian-smoothed momentum marginal"* is exactly the observation behind **Monomial-Gamma samplers (Zhang, Chen, Li, Carin, ICML 2017)**, where it is the *stated motivation* for going beyond quadratic kinetic energies. Present it as "the standard reason a linear O-step cannot sample a non-Gaussian momentum law," not as a CLU discovery.
> - **The thermostat is known math.** The latent-mass (InvGauss scale-mixture) construction is that **Maxwell–Jüttner is a Gaussian scale mixture — Barndorff-Nielsen's Normal-Inverse-Gaussian (1977/1997)** — and the continuous relativistic-OU form is **Dunkel–Hänggi (2009)**. F2's *momentum-preserving O-step packaging* is plausibly novel but rests entirely on cited prior.
> - **Do NOT claim "a bug in relativistic SGHMC."** **Lu, Chen, Carin (2017)'s MH-adjusted method is EXACT.** Only the *unadjusted* rel-SGHMC carries an additive-Gaussian last step, and that is the standard $O(\varepsilon)$ SG-MCMC discretization bias they never claimed to avoid. The CLU sampler's defect is that it has *no* MH correction **and** runs the linear O-step in relativistic mode — a **coded-sampler** issue, not a flaw in the published method.
> - **What IS ours: the $d\cdot\Theta$ amplification** — the $(d{+}1)\Theta$ ultra-relativistic blow-up that turns a benign $d{=}1$ defect ($\mathrm{KL}=0.384$) into a $785\times$ / $\mathrm{KL}=3.24\times10^6$ catastrophe at Exp-C's $d{=}784$ (N56/N57). That, and the demonstration that it *drives nothing* in the MNIST imbalance (N10 upheld), are the genuine contributions.
> - **The exact fix F2 now SHIPS + is toy-verified** (`fix-pack-7`): `langevin_noise="fdt_relativistic"` (behind a flag, no default changed), verified bias $-0.727 \to +0.0011$ (the Newtonian shadow floor); `CHLU.gibbs_defect_parameter(T)` exposes $d\cdot\Theta$ and the warning reports it. **Real-Exp-C validation is still pending** (`fdt-relativistic-expc`, w16) — the number that closes the N10 loop on the *corrected* sampler.
> - **Binding:** this is a **methods-note / F5-appendix correction, not a standalone paper** (N59); CM-17's wording is **cite-don't-claim**. *(Provenance caveat: the Monomial-Gamma exact-no-go quote is WebFetch-single-sourced — verify before citing.)*

> **✅✅ Update (wave-16, `fdt-relativistic-expc`) — F2 is validated on the REAL d=784 Exp-C path, and the N10 loop CLOSES. ⚠ Folded from the report; no Hub §10 review entry yet.** For the first time the exact fix ran on the real chain (not the toy well of fix-pack-7):
> - **(Q1) F2 samples relativistic Gibbs, exactly.** The momentum marginal **inverts Gaussian→Maxwell–Jüttner**: $\mathrm{Var}(p)/(M_{\rm eff}T)$ = **783.5** (MJ theory 785.0), the discriminating radius $r_{\rm MJ}/r_{\rm obs}$ = **1.00×** (was **90×** too small under `legacy`, **28×** under `fdt`), $\mathrm{KL}(\text{radial}\|\text{MJ})=0.004 \ll \mathrm{KL}(\text{radial}\|\text{Gauss})=0.142$ — on two checkpoints, no NaN. **This discharges fix-pack-7's Exp-C-scale extrapolation caveat: the latent-mass thermostat is now verified on the real chain, not just the measure.** (Kurtosis stays blind, as pre-registered — 1-D marginals of a high-$d$ isotropic law are Gaussian by concentration of measure; the discriminating statistic is the *radius/total variance*, not kurtosis.)
> - **(Q2) The correct sampler does NOT fix the imbalance — it *worsens* it.** F2 moves the digit histogram *away* from uniform: $f(3589)$ **0.609→0.896**, TV 0.596→0.649, collapse onto '8' (130/192). **The exact-Gibbs sampler is *more* mode-imbalanced than the defective shipped one**, and lands statistically closest to the *other* momentum-correct arm (`mj_refresh`, $\chi^2{=}15.7$). Mechanism: F2 slots monotonically onto the quench-depth curve — **Spearman(final $H$, $f_{3589}$) = +1.000** over {legacy, fdt, F2, mj_refresh} — the imbalance is set by *how deep the annealed quench descends into $V_\theta$*, a **landscape** property, exactly N10.
> - **⇒ N10 is CLOSED**, and it is the clean payoff of §10.3's *instrument validity precedes inference* discipline: the negative was drawn from a broken sampler (w3), re-opened when a second sampler defect was found (w13), upheld on a nearly-correct instrument (w14), and now **closed on the exact one (w16)** — the sampler was never the cause.
> - **One prereg sub-prediction was FALSIFIED and reported (honest).** I predicted F2 ≈ `fdt` (§5.3's velocity-saturation argument: a 90× momentum-law error maps to 0.66% velocity error, so the momentum law should be dynamically invisible). It is **distinguishable** ($\chi^2{=}35.6$): the MJ momentum's larger *noise variance* keeps the quench **shallower**, so the momentum law is invisible to the **drift** but **not** to the **quench depth**. This *sharpens* N10 (depth is the sufficient statistic) and corrects the earlier "dynamically invisible" overstatement — *the drift the dynamics feel is nearly invariant; the quench depth is not, and depth sets the mode.*

**→ CLU (and the general lesson for ML).** *If you damp a state variable rather than the thing that actually moves, your sampler's stationary law is the law of your damping, not the law of your energy.* A linear O-step **hard-codes a Gaussian answer**; it can only ever be correct when the target's momentum marginal is Gaussian — i.e. when the kinetic energy is quadratic. **Any ML system that swaps in a non-quadratic kinetic/preconditioner term (relativistic caps, gradient clipping, trust regions, normalized-momentum optimizers) and keeps a linear damping+noise step has silently changed the distribution it samples.** That is a general statement, it is one line to check, and nobody checked it here for eleven waves.

# Part VI — Boosts, squeezes, and the retry that can't hurt you

## 6.1 Lorentz boosts and rapidity (the philosophy we import)

**Philosophy.** In relativity, a **boost** is the change of perspective between observers moving at different velocities. It is not a rotation (which preserves lengths) but a **hyperbolic rotation**: it mixes time and space coordinates with $\cosh/\sinh$ factors while exactly preserving the spacetime interval. The natural parameter is **rapidity** $\zeta$ — boosts compose by *adding* rapidities, the way rotations add angles. (Look-ups: *Lorentz boost*, *rapidity*, *Minkowski interval*.) Per P1, we import the *math* — hyperbolic mixing that exactly preserves an invariant — into latent phase space, where the invariant worth preserving is the symplectic form.

## 6.2 The squeeze: a boost in phase space **[proven, verified (c)]**

**Math.** For a conjugate pair $(q_i, p_i)$, define the **squeeze** of rapidity $\zeta_i$:

$$S_\zeta:\quad q_i' = q_i\cosh\zeta_i + p_i\sinh\zeta_i,
\qquad p_i' = q_i\sinh\zeta_i + p_i\cosh\zeta_i .$$

Symplecticity in one line: for the $2\times2$ block, $S^\top\Omega_2 S = (\cosh^2\zeta - \sinh^2\zeta)\,\Omega_2 = \Omega_2$, hence also $\det S = 1$. These maps are the *non-compact* directions of the symplectic group $Sp(2d)$ — the group of all linear maps preserving $\Omega$; the compact directions are rotations (which conserve a mode's energy), the non-compact squeezes *re-weight* it between position and momentum. (Look-ups: *symplectic group $Sp(2d,\mathbb R)$*; *squeezed states* in quantum optics — the same operator, used there to trade uncertainty between quadratures.) **[verified: $\lVert S^\top\Omega S - \Omega\rVert \le 2.2\times10^{-16}$; composed with a Verlet-step Jacobian, still $2.2\times10^{-16}$]**

**→ CLU.** V1's mechanism: an inference-time *re-framing* of the latent — no weight changes — that provably stays inside the structure class every guarantee of Part I applies to. The remaining questions are "what does it cost?" (§6.3) and "who moves?" (§6.4).

## 6.3 What a retry costs: the exact energy-injection bound **[proven]**

Squeezes are not symmetries of $H$ — they inject energy (that's the point: they push the state over ridges plain relaxation can't cross). The injection is *bounded by rapidity*, exactly. For a mode in canonical units, $H = \tfrac12(x^2 + p^2)$:

$$x'^2 + p'^2 = (x^2+p^2)\cosh 2\zeta + 2xp\,\sinh 2\zeta
\;\le\; (x^2+p^2)\big(\cosh2\zeta + \lvert\sinh2\zeta\rvert\big) = (x^2+p^2)\,e^{2\lvert\zeta\rvert},$$

using $\lvert 2xp\rvert \le x^2 + p^2$. So:

$$\boxed{\ H(S_\zeta z) \le e^{2\lvert\zeta\rvert}\, H(z)\ }\qquad\text{(quadratic sector, canonical units)}.$$

**The two-part certificate for the escalation cascade** (this is the precise content of "the retry that can't hurt you"):
- **C1 (structure):** every retry is symplectic ⇒ the composed trajectory (relax → squeeze → relax → …) remains (conformally-)symplectic ⇒ all Part-I guarantees hold verbatim; a retry cannot leave the stability class.
- **C2 (bounded cost):** energy injection per retry ≤ $e^{2\lvert\zeta\rvert}$ ⇒ a bounded-rapidity retry schedule injects bounded energy ⇒ the governor (§2.2) re-absorbs it in bounded time.

What the certificate does **not** say: that energy never rises (it does, deliberately), or that a retry helps (that's the empirical gate). Honest phrasing matters here; "provably stable retries" = C1 + C2, nothing more.

## 6.4 The mass-weighted squeeze: who actually moves **[proven, verified (c)]**

**The subtlety that almost broke L0.** Thread 3's claim: "a single global rapidity is automatically region-differential because of the learned mass matrix." Check it. The *raw* squeeze mixes $q' = q\cosh\zeta + p\sinh\zeta$: the position response $\partial q'/\partial\zeta\vert_0 = p$ contains **no mass at all** — raw squeezes are mass-blind.

The claim becomes true only if the squeeze is defined in the canonically normalized coordinates of §2.3. Let $\mathcal N = \mathrm{diag}\big(M_{\rm eff}^{1/2},\ M_{\rm eff}^{-1/2}\big)$ (itself symplectic: $\mathcal N^\top\Omega\mathcal N = \Omega$, since the diagonal blocks cancel). Define the **mass-weighted squeeze**

$$S^{(M)}_\zeta := \mathcal N^{-1} S_\zeta\, \mathcal N
\qquad\Longrightarrow\qquad
q_i' = q_i\cosh\zeta + \frac{p_i}{M_i}\sinh\zeta,
\quad p_i' = M_iq_i\sinh\zeta + p_i\cosh\zeta,$$

symplectic as a product of symplectic maps. Now $\partial q_i'/\partial\zeta\vert_0 = p_i/M_i$: **at comparable momenta, light coordinates reframe strongly and heavy ones barely** — the Head's intuition, restored, with a spec.

One more honest nuance: $p'/M = q\sinh\zeta + (p/M)\cosh\zeta$, so in $(q, \text{velocity})$ variables $S^{(M)}$ is the plain mass-blind squeeze. The mass-differentiation therefore depends on what is comparable across modes at query time: comparable *momenta* (or thermal equilibrium, where $\mathrm{Var}(p_i) = M_iT$ ⇒ $p \sim \sqrt{M_iT}$ ⇒ response $\sim\sqrt{T/M_i}$, still mass-suppressed) ⇒ differential; comparable *velocities* ⇒ not. This is exactly why "L0 boosts search light modes preferentially" is a *falsifiable to measure* (per-mode displacement vs $M_i$), not a theorem about all conditions. **[proven algebra; regime-dependence noted]**

**→ CLU.** Engineering spec: implement $S^{(M)}$, not $S$. The escalation cascade (V1) is then: relax under the governor → residual $R = H - \tau_k$ (τ a *learned, training-time-calibrated* margin) → if $R > 0$, up to $B$ certified retries $S^{(M)}_{\zeta_b}$ → else shell-jump (lift to the next mass sector). Physics content: Parts I–II; everything else is policy.

## 6.5 The retry as a certified Markov kernel: squeeze + Metropolis **[proven; verified (Thread-9, w8); experiment = w9 backlog]**

*New subsection, wave-8 (`thread9-mh-kernel.md`, Head's Thread-9 colleague question — CM-14). This upgrades the "retry that can't hurt you" (§6.3) from a stability statement into a **sampling** statement, and connects §6 (squeezes) to §5 (Langevin/FDT) and §8.4 (paid access). It is deliberately honest about where the certificate is narrower than the pitch.*

**Philosophy.** If retries are cheap, why not accept/reject them by an energy rule so the *ensemble* of accepted states has a known distribution? That is Markov-chain Monte Carlo: propose a move, accept it with probability $\min(1,e^{-\Delta H/T})$, and the chain converges to the Gibbs measure $\pi\propto e^{-H/T}$ of the trained energy. "Test-time compute as MCMC with a stationarity certificate." (Look-up: *Metropolis–Hastings*; *Hamiltonian Monte Carlo* (HMC, Duane 1987 / Neal 2011); *MALA* — Metropolis-adjusted Langevin.)

**Math — why the squeeze is a *legal* proposal.** HMC avoids a Jacobian (Hastings) correction because its proposal is volume-preserving. The mass-weighted squeeze $S^{(M)}_\zeta=\mathcal N^{-1}S_\zeta\mathcal N$ (§6.4) has $\det J=1$ **exactly** (F5 Prop-12), so the Metropolis ratio collapses to the pure energy ratio $e^{-\Delta H/T}$ — **no correction needed**. One sweep = *(i)* Gibbs-refresh the momentum $p\sim\mathcal N(0,M_{\rm eff}T)$ (leaves $\pi$ invariant), *(ii)* propose a sign-symmetrized squeeze $z'=S^{(M)}_{\pm\zeta}(z)$, accept at $\min(1,e^{-\Delta H/T})$. This satisfies detailed balance w.r.t. $\pi$. **[proven; verified — reproduces the Gibbs $q$-marginal to $L1=0.0095$, accept 0.66].**

**Two conditions the bare pitch omits.**
- **Reversibility.** $S_\zeta$ is *not* an involution ($S_\zeta\!\circ\!S_\zeta=S_{2\zeta}$); the reverse move needs $S_{-\zeta}$, supplied by sign-symmetrizing $\zeta$ (or, as found numerically, by the momentum-refresh's own $\pm p$ symmetry — sign-symmetrization is load-bearing only in partial/no-refresh regimes).
- **Ergodicity.** $\{S^{(M)}_\zeta:\zeta\in\mathbb R\}$ is a one-parameter subgroup: its orbit of any point is a single hyperbola, so **squeeze-only is reducible and does NOT sample $\pi$** (verified: stuck, $L1=1.81$). Ergodicity requires the HMC-style momentum refresh. So the squeeze is best understood **not as a sampler** but as a *gradient-free, mass-metric-preconditioned **global** move* (the reach asset of §8.4) layered on a proper local sampler. Against HMC's leapfrog it trades gradient-informed mixing (cost $\sim N^{1/4}$) for a landscape-blind, high-rejection random-walk move (cost $\sim N$) — buying certificate/implementation simplicity (no $\nabla V_\theta$ at test time, no step-size tuning) and the learned mass preconditioner.

**The governor breaks the invariant — the honest deflation.** The actual retry cascade interleaves *governed relaxation* $\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma(H)}$ between proposals. State-dependent friction $\gamma(H)>0$ has $\det J=(1-\gamma)^d<1$ (§1.8): it strictly drains energy and does **not** preserve $\pi$. So the composite squeeze-then-relax kernel is **Metropolis-within-annealing**, converging not to $\pi$ but to a *colder, MAP-seeking measure* $\pi_{T_{\rm eff}}$ (verified: $T_{\rm eff}$ falls $1.0\to0.61$ as $\gamma:0\to0.2$). This may be *desirable* for retrieval (you want the low-energy answer), but **never claim Gibbs stationarity for the governed composite** — the certificate is an annealing/optimization statement unless $\gamma$ is frozen to 0 within the certified segment.

**Certified retry ERODES the latch (the memory caution).** The Gibbs measure is exactly flat along the Goldstone coset (§4.3; $\mu=0$, $V$ constant), so any proposal with a coset-tangent component has $\Delta H=0$ and is **accepted with probability 1** — the stored register then performs an *unbiased random walk*: $D_\varphi=\tfrac12 s^2$ per accepted step, $\mathrm{Var}(\varphi_N)=s^2N$, so it is erased after $N_{\rm erode}\approx(\Delta_{\rm read}/s)^2$ retries (verified $D=1.29\text{e-}3$ vs predicted $1.25\text{e-}3$). **Critical caution for V1:** the w7 result that a channel-isotropic squeeze *preserves the Goldstone charge* $Q=p^\top Xq$ ($\Delta Q\approx10^{-7}$) does **NOT** protect the stored coset *position* — the refreshed momentum enters the shear and diffuses $\varphi$ regardless ($D=1.7\text{e-}2$). **Charge-preservation $\ne$ position-preservation** — state this before any MH paragraph ships (prevents a "latch is preserved" vs "retries erase memory" contradiction).

**Three design rules (the recommended, certified kernel).**
1. **$\gamma=0$ inside certified segments;** the governor is an *outer* BIBO-safety layer applied only *between* segments (so it never corrupts the invariant). Per-segment detailed balance is then the clean certificate.
2. **Mixture kernel $K=\tfrac12\,\mathrm{MALA}(\sigma^\ast)+\tfrac12\,\text{sign-symmetrized squeeze-MH}$.** MALA supplies gradient-informed mixing; the squeeze supplies the mass-aware global/reach move local diffusion misses. A mixture of two $\pi$-reversible kernels is $\pi$-reversible. The MALA noise scale is the **FDT-correct** $\sigma^\ast_i=\sqrt{M_{\rm eff,i}T\gamma(2-\gamma)}$ (§5.2, Prop-9) — **load-bearing:** with it MALA samples exactly ($L1=0.0065$), without the Metropolis adjustment ULA is $15\times$ worse ($L1=0.0995$). This FDT-calibrated scale is the CLU's unique asset.
3. **Project proposals off the coset tangent** (sample only the massive/orthogonal sector) ⇒ the latch is quenched ($D_\varphi=0$, moved only by explicit writes). Without the projection, quote $N_{\rm erode}=(\Delta_{\rm read}/s)^2$ as an explicit retry cap.

**Temperature semantics (§5-adjacent).** The acceptance rule is **intra-model** ($\Delta H$ within one chain on one trained $H$), so the raw-$R$ cross-model incomparability lesson (§10.3; pooled AUROC 0.43) does **not** break validity — detailed balance holds for any fixed $T>0$. *But* the *useful* operating $T$ is model-specific and, by Prop-9, FDT-miscalibrated (nominal $T$ is $\sim11\times$ off, $T_{\rm eff}\propto dt$), so a hardcoded global $T$ is meaningless across $dt/M$/architecture; a principled $T$ is **per-model, calibrated from write-time probes** — i.e. it re-imports the same learned calibration head as the τ-gate. **Honest conclusion (CM-3 discipline):** MH's contribution is the **stationarity certificate + explicit latch-erosion accounting**, *not* parameter parsimony and *not* — on current evidence — performance (energy $\approx$ learned-signal, three times over).

**→ CLU.** This makes the V1 retry cascade a member of a principled family: *governor-composed, coset-projected, FDT-calibrated, bounded-injection Metropolis moves on a trained conservative associative memory, with an explicit accounting of when certified compute preserves vs destroys the thing it retrieves.* `[theory-complete + 4 float64 checks; NO trained-model runs — the discriminating w9 experiment (arms: learned-τ / router / squeeze-MH / MALA(σ*) / coset-projected mixture / un-projected) is spec'd and on the BACKLOG]` (CM-14). Reading: §5.2 (FDT/σ*), §6.3 (retry cost), §8.4 (squeeze as reach); ledger Ch.4/6; Neal 2011, Roberts–Tweedie 1996 (MALA), Wales–Doye 1997 (basin-hopping); `thread9-mh-kernel`.

# Part VII — Black-hole modules: localized forgetting with exact accounting

## 7.1 The imported philosophy (four look-ups, one paragraph each)

- **Event horizon:** a one-way surface — things cross inward and cannot return; from outside, information appears destroyed. CLU import: a *designated region of latent space where forgetting is total and local*, instead of a global $\gamma$ that forgets everything a little (paper limitation §D.1). (Look-up: *event horizon*, *black hole thermodynamics*.)
- **Hawking radiation:** horizons are not perfectly black; they re-emit as *heat*. CLU import (Thread 1, study S2): destroyed structure can be recycled as exploration temperature — destruction funding creation, closing the wake–sleep loop. (Look-up: *Hawking radiation*.)
- **Unruh effect:** an *accelerating* observer feels a temperature proportional to acceleration. CLU import: adaptive exploration $T_{\rm eff} \propto \lvert\nabla V\rvert$ — heat switches on exactly where the landscape is violent. Kept as a separate module family. (Look-up: *Unruh effect*.)
- **Lindblad/GKSL:** the rigorous framework for "mostly-conservative system + designed, localized dissipation channels" in quantum theory. We use its *classical shape*: symplectic bulk + local non-unitary channels — which is exactly what the theorem below certifies. (Look-up: *Lindblad equation*, *open quantum systems*.)

Per P1: zero claims about real black holes; we are borrowing a mathematical shape with an ML-measurable job.

## 7.2 The friction field theorem **[proven, verified (h)]**

**Def.** Replace the scalar $\gamma$ by a learned field $\gamma_\phi(q) \in [0, \gamma_{\max}]$, applied after the Verlet substeps as $(q, p) \mapsto \big(q,\ (1-\gamma_\phi(q_{n+1}))\,p\big)$.

**Theorem (position-gated volume destruction, exact).** The damping stage's Jacobian is

$$\begin{pmatrix} I & 0\\ -\,p\,\nabla\gamma_\phi(q)^{\top} & (1-\gamma_\phi(q))\,I\end{pmatrix}$$

— block lower-triangular, so its determinant ignores the messy off-diagonal block entirely:

$$\boxed{\ \det D\Phi = \big(1-\gamma_\phi(q_{n+1})\big)^{d}\ }\qquad\text{exactly, for any smoothness or steepness of } \gamma_\phi .$$

Phase-space volume (information, in the §1.4 sense) is destroyed **exactly and only where $\gamma_\phi > 0$**: the "horizon" is the superlevel set of the learned field; outside it, the step is exactly conservative. What is lost relative to constant $\gamma$: conformality (and hence the singular-value pairing) wherever $\nabla\gamma_\phi \ne 0$ — the *volume* accounting survives perfectly, the *pairing* accounting doesn't; claims must say which they use. **[verified: $\lvert\det J - (1-\gamma(q'))^d\rvert \le 1.1\times10^{-16}$ at random states, machine-precision Jacobians]**

**→ CLU.** Thread 1's metaphor is now a theorem with an audit trail: per-step information destruction is a computable, learnable scalar field. The contrastive training proposal (wake pushes $\gamma_\phi\downarrow$ at data, sleep pushes $\gamma_\phi\uparrow$ at persistent hallucinations) plugs into this object; S1 (how many holes) and S2 (re-emission) are experiments *on top of* an exact substrate. Also note the composition with §3.2: memories far from the horizon are latched (exactly conservative region + frozen registers) — "forgets garbage completely and memories not at all" is componentwise exact.

> **Update (wave-4/5) — S1 status: the two queued cures are validated; one composition is a negative; the erase-scope clause is now doctrine.**
> - **Oracle Pareto-dominance holds** (pilot, `gamma-field-build.md`, 3 seeds): the position-gated horizon dominates the entire global-$\gamma$ curve *and* the energy governor (retention 0.712 at the conservative ceiling, rejection 0.844 vs governor 0.669). Prediction (iii-horizon)≻(i-global) holds for the learned $K{=}4$ field; (iii)≻(ii-governor) needs the oracle.
> - **Adaptive-K spawning [validated at scratch]** (`v2-prefreeze-baselines.md` item 3; seed-sweeps): spawns $K{=}1\to8$ on persistent-hallucination density, fixes the gradient-locality locus-discovery failure (2/3 seeds vs the pilot's 2/6), and gives the **best rejection of any learned arm (0.861)**.
> - **Compact-support gates [validated at scratch]:** a hard cutoff cuts on-manifold friction leakage **~200×** ($6.9\times10^{-6}$ vs sigmoid $1.35\times10^{-3}$) — the oracle-compact is **exactly 0.0 outside its radius**, sitting at the $\gamma{=}0$ retention ceiling (0.677 = base) *while still rejecting*. Caveat: a hard cutoff has no reach, so **compact gates need accurate placement — which adaptive-K supplies**; combined compact+adaptive-K is the queued next step.
> - **Governor + learned field do NOT compose [negative result]** (`seed-sweeps.md` item 3a, 3 seeds): the composed arm is **Pareto-dominated by both components** (rejection collapses to 0.48 vs field-only 0.84 / governor 0.74) — multiplicative over-damping freezes garbage off-manifold. **Deploy the field alone.**
> - **Erase-scope clause (F5 v1.1 Cor-13) [proven]:** friction *latches* flat-direction (coset) content — it **cannot erase it**. A friction-only trash region deletes massive-mode amplitude but leaves symmetry-protected registers exactly intact; erasing coset content needs local tilt/curvature or noise (Hawking re-emission, S2). "Shiva needs Brahma": destruction alone is incomplete.
> - Design principle (`gamma-field-build.md`): **"objectives must not legislate friction"** — stop-grad the field in wake+sleep rollouts, energy-gate the hallucination term, use a two-timescale lr, else the accuracy loss drives $\gamma_\phi$ to critical damping ($\approx2\varepsilon\mu$, fastest *forgetting*), which is exactly wrong for a *protection* field.

## 7.3 When training erodes the geometry it was given: sleep-CD vacuum inversion and the V(data) anchor **[demarcation = proven theory; anchor cure = measured, 5 seeds]**

*New subsection, wave-6. The friction knobs above ($\gamma$, $\gamma_\phi$) are **deliberate** erosion. There is a second, **accidental** one: the wake–sleep contrastive-divergence trainer can itself destroy a designed memory geometry. Wave-6 (`anchor-robustness.md`) promoted this from a training-dynamics caveat to a demarcation law + a characterized cure — the reason V2's designed vacua need an anchored trainer. Ledger Chapter 7; negatives N5.*

**The demarcation law [proven as theory].** Wake–sleep CD inverts a designed vacuum **iff** that vacuum has a **flat direction unconstrained by the wake objective**. The wake trajectory-MSE cannot see a flat coset coordinate, so the sleep phase (raising the energy of hallucinations that sit in the ring band) fills it in: ring depth flips $+\to-$, the settled radius $r^\ast\to0$. Three independent lines make this *theory*, not a patch note (`anchor-robustness §2`):
- **$\mu^2$-witness.** At $\lambda=0$ the settled-point flat mode stiffens from $\approx0$ to $+0.04$–$0.21$ exactly as the ring inverts; with the anchor it stays $\approx10^{-6}$. Erosion **is** the destruction of the flat mode.
- **Tilt-immunity (erosion $\propto$ flatness).** Lifting the flat direction with a tilted potential by $\delta\ge0.05$ — **no anchor at all** — immunizes the vacuum ($r^\ast$ 0.00 → 0.75–1.36). As soon as the wake-MSE can *see* the angular coordinate, CD can no longer invert it.
- **Non-degenerate control.** The Exp-B sine vacuum (no flat direction) is immune (wake+sleep $\approx$ wake-only). Crisp statement: *wake–sleep CD inverts a designed vacuum iff that vacuum has a flat direction unconstrained by the wake objective.*

**The cure and its envelope [measured, 5 seeds, 3000 erosive epochs].** A **V(data) energy-anchor** — add $\lambda\cdot(\mathrm{mean}_i V(\text{anchor}_i)-\text{target})^2$ to the wake loss — holds the designed SO(2) vacuum through 3000 epochs for $\lambda\in\{1,10,100\}$ where $\lambda=0$ dies 5/5. The envelope is a **strength↔robustness tradeoff**: $\lambda\approx10$ gives the strongest noise rejection (gap 1.85) but is *not* seed-bulletproof (1/5 seeds collapses — the "seed-45" pathology); **$\lambda=100$ is bulletproof 5/5** ($r^\ast=0.911\pm0.016$) at $\sim35\times$ wake-MSE cost and halved noise rejection (0.60). Operating rule: $\lambda\approx10$ where seed-robustness can be checked, $\lambda=100$ for a single-shot guarantee.

**Anchor ⟂ volume conservation [measured].** The anchor is **orthogonal** to the symplectic substrate: it does **not** rescue the broken-volume (non-symplectic) vacuum, which diverges under CD regardless (wake MSE $3.5\text{k}$–$176\text{k}$, $r^\ast\to\infty$, noise_gap $-16$ to $-49$). CD-vacuum survival is a *volume-conservation payoff* (the §1.5 attribution table); the anchor is a *separate* cure that operates only on a bounded symplectic map (CM-1/CM-6 sharpened). It is also the **wrong tool for capacity-limited memory** — anchoring a memory EBM pins its *random init* (see §-memory: fidelity 0.44 anchor < 0.66 baseline).

**→ CLU / placement.** `[demarcation = proven theory; anchor cure = measured, 5 seeds, 3000 ep]`. **Placement: V2 Appendix B default** (recipe folded into §3.5), **pending the Jul-11 novelty confirm** — landscape *distortion* is classical (Fischer–Igel, Nijkamp); the *designed-vacuum inversion / degeneracy-specificity / frequency-horizon law / anchor cure* appear novel but are unconfirmed. Reading: ledger Ch.7; `negative_results.md` N5; F5 Cor-13 (friction latches coset content, cannot erase it — the same flat-direction structure that erosion attacks).

> **Update (wave-7; source `fix-pack-4`) — the anchor is now a first-class flag, and exp-d guards the erosion regime.** `training.anchor_data_energy_lambda` (default 0.0 = off, **bit-compatible**) ships in `train_chlu`: the wake term $\lambda\cdot(\mathrm{mean}_i V(\text{anchor}_i)-\text{target})^2$ with the anchor target captured **once** from the epoch-0 model (`mean V(data[:,0,:dim])`) and evaluated against the live differentiable potential. `chlu exp-d --anchor-lambda F` exposes it. `run_experiment_d` now emits a loud erosion-regime `RuntimeWarning` iff sleep is on **and** epochs > 300 **and** the vacuum is designed (`so2_invariant`) **and** no anchor is set — the guard the erosion law implies (UX-only, fires before training, no behavior change). $\lambda=0$ is a never-entered static branch ⇒ bitwise-identical to the legacy loop (verified across all PyTree leaves). Full suite 189 passed / 1 skipped on this branch.

# Part VIII — Many-body: the CLU lattice

## 8.1 One Hamiltonian, many units **[proven]**

**Def.** Units $i = 1..N$ with states $(q_i, p_i)$, a coupling graph $E$, and a **single joint Hamiltonian**

$$H_{\rm net} = \sum_i\big[T_i(p_i) + V_i(q_i)\big] + \sum_{(ij)\in E} V_c(q_i, q_j).$$

"Depth" = rollout time; "width" = $N$; "architecture" = the graph. Because it is *one* Hamiltonian evolved by *one* symplectic map, every guarantee of Parts I–III applies to the whole network **by construction, at any scale** — that is V3's entire pitch — *provided*:

1. **couplings are position-only** ($V_c$ depends on $q$'s, never $p$'s) — momentum coupling breaks separability (§1.2 scope guard) and voids the exactness;
2. **one global step $\varepsilon$**, kicks computed from the full joint force;
3. **damping discipline:** uniform $\gamma$ ⇒ globally conformal (pairing intact); per-unit $\gamma_i$ ⇒ exact volume law $\det J = \prod_i(1-\gamma_i)^{d_i}$ but no global pairing; fields $\gamma_\phi$ ⇒ §7.2.

Noether/Goldstone channels survive at the network level iff the couplings are *equivariant* under the shared symmetry and the §4.2 mass-isotropy holds per channel.

## 8.2 Communication has a mass **[proven at quadratic order]**

**Philosophy.** Couple two units and you haven't just wired them — you've *created a particle*: the disagreement between them becomes a mode with its own spectral mass, and all of Part III applies to it.

**Math.** Two units, each with an SO(2) memory angle ($\vartheta_1, \vartheta_2$, common angular inertia $I_\vartheta = Mf^2$), coupled by $V_c = \tfrac{\kappa_c}{2}(\vartheta_1 - \vartheta_2)^2$ (invariant under *simultaneous* rotation — the equivariant choice). Normal modes:

- **sum** $s = (\vartheta_1 + \vartheta_2)/\sqrt2$: $V_c$ doesn't feel it ⇒ exactly flat ⇒ a *shared latch* across units (the network-level Goldstone);
- **difference** $r = (\vartheta_1 - \vartheta_2)/\sqrt2$: $V_c = \kappa_c r^2$ ⇒ curvature $2\kappa_c$ ⇒

$$\mu_{\rm rel}^2 = \frac{2\kappa_c}{Mf^2}.$$

Feed §3: synchronization timescale $\propto 1/\mu_{\rm rel} \propto \kappa_c^{-1/2}$; retention of *disagreement* (relative information), overdamped: $n_{1/2} \propto 1/\kappa_c$. **Coupling strength is a price dial: strong coupling = fast agreement = fast forgetting of relative state.** A falsifiable, architecture-level design rule that came for free from the two-oscillator normal-mode computation. (Look-up: *normal modes*, *coupled oscillators*.)

> **Update (wave-6; source w6) — the pricing law is PREDICTIVE, and banding is a METHOD with a measured price for guessing wrong.** Two w6 results turn the quadratic pricing math above into (i) a *registered-before-measurement* prediction and (ii) a from-data recipe:
> - **Pricing predicts the task (CM-10, `v3-interference-ntk.md` item 2, 3 seeds).** $\kappa_{\rm eff}$ read *blind* from the learned coupling-potential curvature (mass-weighted Hessian of $V_c$ along the relative mode, before any rollout) predicts the recall-horizon **ranking** (Spearman $n_{1/2}$ pred-vs-meas **1.0**, $\kappa_{\rm eff}\leftrightarrow n_{1/2}$ **−1.0**) and the synchronization time **pointwise to ≤8 %** across a **91× $\kappa_{\rm eff}$ decade** on trained lattices — the prediction was registered *before* the task was run. Higher price ⇒ strictly shorter recall horizon: the $\mu_{\rm rel}^2 = 2\kappa_c/(Mf^2)$ law, now measured on *trained* couplings. *"The price list is predictive, not descriptive."*
> - **Banding is a method, not an oracle gift (CM-11, `v3-band-selection.md` items 1–2, 5 seeds).** A *wrong* inertial-mass prior of the **same log-spread budget COSTS**: the degradation curve is **matched (1.18) < uniform (2.42) < orthogonal (6.92) < anti (12.79)** @300 ep — an inverted band is 5.3× worse than uniform, an orthogonally-placed one 2.9× worse. So the banding win is a *correct-prior* effect with a steep price for guessing wrong, not "any structure helps" and not "we told the model the answer for free" (the V3.2 confound, killed). And the band can be **chosen from data**: a per-unit spectral (FFT) timescale estimate $\to M\propto1/\omega^2$ recovers the oracle band with **MSE gap 0.000, 5/5 seeds** — *when timescales are spectrally separable* (state the qualifier; on overlapping timescales the FFT estimate degrades and the gap opens). The mass-lr induce-then-snap alternative is the fragile foil (4/5 correct; the one inversion snaps to the anti-band and pays the full penalty).

## 8.3 Wormholes and shells

- **Wormholes** = sparse *non-local* edges in $E$ (long-range $V_c$), energy-gated: long-range routing at $O(Nk)$ instead of attention's $O(N^2)$. Structure verdict: **smooth** gates (gate value flows through the potential) keep every guarantee; **hard top-$k$** selection makes $H$ piecewise-defined — exactly symplectic within an epoch, with bounded energy *jumps* at switches that must be budgeted explicitly. **[proven structure; ledger = design hypothesis]**
- **Shells** = mass sectors (§2.3 discipline: sectors of the $\mu$-spectrum, implemented by banding $M$ and/or curvature): heavy-$M$/low-$\mu$ = slow coarse backbone, light = fast perception. The V1 cascade's "shell-jump" = re-posing a query from a fast sector to a slow one. **[design hypothesis with proven timescale math]**

> **Update (wave-4 + wave-6; sources w4, w6) — wormhole routing is now MEASURED, and the honest verdict REVERSED once a proper baseline existed. Present both legs.**
> - **Leg 1 (w4, `v1-wormhole-routing.md`): the mechanism works.** Energy-gated sparse non-local routing on a chain lattice beats the local-only and dense ceilings (gated overall 0.875/0.812 vs 0.50), gate-selectivity AUROC 0.954–0.960, bounded gate-energy injection (F5 §7.4). Crucially the **direct 1-hop edge beats the $N{-}1$-hop chain diffusion**, whose cost *scales* with $N$ while the edge stays flat.
> - **Leg 2 (w6, `v1-router-baseline.md`, 5 seeds — CM-7 REWRITTEN): the energy *gating* LOSES to a physics-free learned router.** A **449-param** 2-layer MLP on the raw query cue (no energy, no relaxation) beats the energy-gated wormhole in **FLOPs AND accuracy at both N and every workload mix**: router **1.000/0.948 @ 8.81e7 FLOP** vs gated **0.887/0.715 @ 1.18e8** (the old 2-seed 0.812 was optimistic); router AUROC(→distant) = 1.000 vs energy-residual 0.865–0.963. So **energy-as-routing-signal is FORBIDDEN** — the third instance of the CM-2/CM-3 pattern ("energy $\approx$/$<$ a learned signal; the gate mechanism is memory-agnostic"). The impostor-composition cause of the calibrated head's N=8 over-routing was isolated (all-others probes → 53 % local false-positive; archive_only → 7 %).
> - **What survives (the salvaged, physics-independent claim):** the **1-hop edge is flat in $N$ (1.18e8 FLOP) where the $N$-hop chain scales (1.76e8 → 2.94e8) and loses distant accuracy (0.41 → 0.28).** Claim *the edge as the mechanism*, never the energy gating of it. **Caveat:** the task's linearly-separable cues (disjoint per-unit keys) give the router a ceiling; a harder band is untested — the honest read is "on the laptop testbed, physics buys nothing over the cheap classifier." `[mechanism measured; energy-gating reframed to CM-2]`

> **Update (wave-7; source `regime-remap-2000ep` Item 3, CM-8 refined) — the same "a cheap baseline is cheaper" lesson recurs in the CLU-vs-Hopfield *memory* regime; the "9–10× savings" is INTRA-CLU, not vs Hopfield.** The w6 provisional claim (a 2000-ep CLU-gate "matches/beats Hopfield at 9–10× savings", primer §10.3 / N2) needed a fairness correction. Modern Hopfield retrieval is *iterable* ($z_{t+1}=\mathrm{softmax}(\beta z_t X^\top)X$, key-half clamped each step); swept over iterations × $\beta$, at $\beta\ge5$ it hits its accuracy ceiling in a **single matvec** (0.947–0.979; 10× more iterations move accuracy by ≤0.003). So Hopfield's cost floor is **1 matvec, $O(kv\cdot d)$** — cheaper per query than even the *gated* CLU (≥300 Verlet steps, each a potential-MLP forward+grad). **Honest statement:** at matched accuracy Hopfield is the cheaper retriever; the "9–10× savings" is **CLU-gate-vs-CLU-full-budget** (intra-CLU compute-rationing), NOT savings against Hopfield; "matching Hopfield" means the gate *reaches* Hopfield's accuracy, not that it beats its cost. **Split verdict:** the *cost* story is now **FINAL**; the *accuracy-reversal* (2000 ep closes the w4 "losing" cells) is still small-$n$ and **grid-pending** → w8 `regime-remap-complete`. (The `impostor_policy` / `--train-epochs` knobs that make these runs reproducible shipped in `fix-pack-4`.)

> **Update (wave-8; source `regime-remap-2000ep` §w8-completion, full 198-job grid — CM-8 now SETTLED).** The accuracy story is resolved, and it is **regime-specific, not a general win**. The "Hopfield-dominant 26/26" map was confirmed an **under-training artifact** (500 ep: CLU gate 0.02–0.31 vs Hopfield 0.95–0.98). Trained to convergence the CLU storage fidelity → ≈1.0 and the reversal appears — but only under specific conditions:
> - **Clean / correlated cues, kv≤64:** gated-CLU **reverses** Hopfield ($n=8$ pooled — gate 1.00/0.99 vs Hopfield 0.98/0.97, $\Delta\,{+}0.02$; holds at $\rho=0.5$; *widens* to $\Delta\,{+}0.08$–${+}0.16$ at $\rho=0.9$ **only because Hopfield itself collapses on correlated keys** — 0.72/0.59 — not because the CLU rises).
> - **The wall beyond kv64 is an EPOCH-BUDGET wall, not capacity.** At a fixed 2000-ep budget gate acc falls 0.99(kv64)→0.92(kv96)→0.60(kv128); but at **4000 ep all four kv reach fidelity ≈1.0** and gate acc ≥/≈ the Hopfield band (kv96 → 0.975, $\Delta\,{+}0.03$; kv128 → 0.95, a **tie**). *Required epochs scale with kv* — a diagonal compute–fidelity ridge, not a ceiling.
> - **Small cells OVER-TRAIN (a negative).** kv32 gate acc **drops 1.00→0.89 from 2000→4000 ep** (below its own Hopfield 0.976) — there is **no single epoch at which all four cells simultaneously beat Hopfield**. Also a **non-monotone intermediate-epoch fidelity dip** (kv96 0.39→0.24→0.97; kv128 0.40→0.09→0.71 at 500/1000/2000 ep) — do not read the 1000-ep point as progress.
> - **THE NOISE WALL — the dominant negative, and it travels with every reversal claim.** Under cue noise $\sigma\in\{0.3,0.6\}$ **NO cell closes at any capacity, even kv32** (gate 0.36 vs Hopfield 0.71 at $\sigma=0.6$/kv32) *despite* storage fidelity ≈1.0 — the governed relaxation gate is markedly **less noise-robust than Hopfield's single matvec**. This is the axis most relevant to real retrieval and the sharpest negative in the study. **Tally at 2000 ep: 6/15 cells close** (all correlation-axis, kv≤64); eval-noise axis **0/6**.
> - **Cost unchanged and controlling** (as w7): Hopfield remains cheaper (~1 matvec ceiling) **and** the more noise-robust retriever; the "6–10× savings" is intra-CLU (gate vs full-budget). **Net (approved CM-8 wording):** *the CLU gate reaches/exceeds Hopfield accuracy only for clean/correlated cues at kv≤64 (kv-scaled epochs extend this on the clean axis); Hopfield keeps both the cost and the cue-noise-robustness advantage.* `[SETTLED — n=8 capacity / n=5 stress / n=3 frontier; laptop, MQAR, f32, legacy noise]`

## 8.4 The two forbidden cheats and paid access (the economic frame) **[frame = design hypothesis; reach/escape dichotomy = PROVEN (w6); end-to-end experiments = PASS all four gate criteria (w7); pillar-4 ADOPTED; oracle-placement scope]**

*New subsection, wave-5 (brainstorm Thread 8; Head 2026-07-07, reacting to the minus-the-physics fit gap). This is the candidate unifying framing device for the ICLR long — it ties Threads 1, 3, 5 and the wormhole line under one economic picture. Status is deliberately mixed: the frame is a hypothesis, one of its two legs is now measured.*

**The idea.** The "physics costs raw fit" result (§1.5 update: the unconstrained twin fits ~15× better) is not a defeat — it is the *price of two guarantees*. An unconstrained network uses two cheats freely that the physics prior **forbids by construction**:

1. **Unpaid contraction.** Volume conservation ($\det J = 1$, §1.4) means errors *cannot* be flowed onto the data manifold — you may not shrink phase-space to collapse a bad prediction onto a good one. **This is the measured, dominant term in the wave-5 fit gap:** the `BrokenVolumeCHLU` arm recovers ≈2.4× of the fit purely by *leaking volume* ($\log|\det J|\to-0.065$), and the causal cap was **not even active** in that arm (`minus-the-physics.md` Part A). So the fit cost is, empirically, *contraction-forbidden* — the volume law, not the light-cone.
2. **Unpaid long-range moves.** Reach is priced: the causal velocity cap $v^{\max}_i = c/\sqrt{M_i}$ (§2.2) bounds how far latent content travels per step, and coupling sync scales as $\kappa^{-1/2}$ (§8.2) — a CLU rollout cannot instantly consult a distant region of its own learned potential the way an attention layer reads any token. This is the Head's original intuition (legacy Exp-II: LSTM/NODE explore freely, CLU is causal).

**Each forbidden cheat has a *designed, priced* escape hatch already in the program** — you *sell the guarantees and rent the cheats back with certificates*:

| forbidden cheat | licensed, priced escape hatch | price / certificate | status |
|---|---|---|---|
| unpaid contraction | **friction field $\gamma_\phi(q)$** (§7.2) — licensed *local* contraction | exact metered volume loss $\det J=(1-\gamma_\phi)^d$; latches, can't erase coset (Cor-13) | validated at pilot/scratch |
| unpaid long-range move | **wormholes** (§8.3) — priced non-local channel | 1-hop routing beats $N{-}1$-hop diffusion; bounded gate energy (F5 §7.4) | measured (w4) |
| unpaid long-range move | **squeezes** (§6.3) — certified bounded energy injection to cross a ridge | $H(S_\zeta z)\le e^{2|\zeta|}H(z)$; retry stays in-class | parked (retry-for-correctness null; access-claim untested) |

**New mechanism candidate: intra-unit wormholes.** Current wormholes are *inter-unit* couplings (§8.3); a **within-node** non-local channel across one unit's own latent space does not exist yet. It would be paid access to a region of a single unit's $V_\theta$ that a causal rollout can't reach in time. **[design hypothesis, engineering scope unassessed.]** With squeezing, it is a **V1-conditional 4th pillar** — joins the V1 short *iff* the wave-7 `paid-access-experiments` return substantial positives (gate criteria: theory-matched effect size + certificates hold + latch preserved + beats a no-physics router; miss ⇒ documented negative, mechanisms stay V3/future-work). **Scope discipline:** the squeeze claim here is *reach/access* (crossing to an otherwise-unreachable basin), **not** the retry-for-correctness claim the `v1-l0-gate` null already killed — the two are different experiments.

**Falsifiables (tasked to w6 `fit-gap-anatomy`).** (a) the fit gap decomposes measurably into contraction-forbidden vs reach-priced parts; (b) fit-vs-horizon curves *cross* — the unconstrained advantage is a **loan** that stability repays at long horizon (legacy Exp-I/II, now against the matched twin); (c) each paid mechanism ($\gamma$, $\gamma_\phi$, squeeze, wormhole) buys back a *measurable* share of the gap at a *measurable* structural price. Until (b) reports, do not claim the fit gap persists at long horizon — the crossing is unmeasured (claims-matrix scope on CM-1).

> **Update (wave-6; source w6) — the three falsifiables reported, and the two mechanisms are now theory-complete.**
> **`fit-gap-anatomy.md` (3 seeds) settled (a)/(b)/(c):**
