## 8.5 Reversible training: the conservative memory learns for free **[measured, w7; exact only at $\gamma=0$]**

*New subsection, wave-7 (`v3-reversible-o1.md`; adopted V3/ICLR systems claim, backlog #1 discharged). A property not of the physics but of the physics-plus-autodiff-graph: the lattice can be trained at $O(1)$ activation memory in sequence length, which is why it is a genuine systems asset at ICLR scale. Ledger Ch.5.*

**Philosophy.** Backprop-through-time normally stores every intermediate state so the backward pass can use it — memory $O(T)$ in the rollout length $T$, which is what makes long sequences OOM. A **reversible** integrator lets you *recompute* each previous state from the next one on the backward leg, so you only ever keep the final state: activation memory becomes $O(1)$ in $T$. (Look-up: *reversible integrators*, *RevNet*, *Momentum-ResNet*, *gradient checkpointing* as the $O(\sqrt T)$ middle ground.)

**Math.** The CLU / lattice Hamiltonian $H=T(p)+V(q)$ is **separable**, so $\partial H/\partial q=V'(q)$ is independent of $p$ and $\partial H/\partial p=T'(p)$ is independent of $q$. The dissipative velocity-Verlet map therefore has a **closed-form exact inverse** given $(q',p')$:

$$p^\ast=\tfrac{p'}{1-\gamma},\quad p_{\tfrac12}=p^\ast+\tfrac{dt}{2}V'(q'),\quad q=q'-dt\,T'(p_{\tfrac12}),\quad p=p_{\tfrac12}+\tfrac{dt}{2}V'(q).$$

At $\gamma=0$ this is bit-equivalent to a forward step with $dt\to-dt$. The shipped rollouts are already `lax.scan`-pure, so an $O(1)$ reverse scan (reconstruct-then-VJP each step) is a drop-in — no core changes.

**Measured (laptop CPU, JAX 0.9.0, untrained models — reversibility is a graph property, training-independent):**
- **Gradients match standard stored-activation BPTT** to **≤2e-6 rel (float32) / ~1e-15 (float64)** at $T\le1024$ — five-plus orders below the SGD gradient-noise floor, i.e. training-indistinguishable.
- **Peak activation memory $O(1)$ vs $O(T)$:** **6.3 KB (reversible) vs 6.00 MB (standard) at $T=1024,\ N=2$ → 946×** (940× at $N=8$); standard grows linearly in $T$, reversible is flat (scales only with state size $O(D)$). Ratio grows without bound in $T$.
- **Wall-time $\approx0.9\times$** (parity) — but **CPU / small-$D$ regime only, not GPU-validated**; on a GPU or large potential the recompute may surface as up to $\sim2\times$.

**The $\gamma$ boundary [the honest scope, a C-9 negative].** Exactness holds **only at $\gamma=0$.** The dissipative $(1-\gamma)$ contraction is invertible only in exact arithmetic and amplifies reconstruction error as $(1-\gamma)^{-n}$, giving a **finite usable reversal horizon**: $\approx3.3\times10^4$ steps @ $\gamma=10^{-3}$, $4.1\times10^3$ @ $10^{-2}$, $5\times10^2$ @ $0.05$, $1.3\times10^2$ @ $0.1$ (float64; ~100× shorter in float32). Dissipative segments must fall back to stored activations / checkpointing.

**→ CLU.** *"Reversibly-$O(1)$-trainable memory = the conservative ($\gamma=0$) memory."* This dovetails the program's $\gamma=0$-memory / $\gamma>0$-rationed-forgetting budget framing (§1.8, §3): the very knob that buys forgetting also buys the memory cost of training. **Not yet wired into `train_chlu`** — only the gradient mechanics are validated (untrained model, final-state loss); a `reversible_bptt` training path + accelerator measurement is the load-bearing V3 systems result. Position against gradient-checkpointing $O(\sqrt T)$ as the fair Pareto middle-ground. `[measured, 3 seeds, CPU, D≤16, laptop; exact-at-$\gamma=0$; not in shipped trainer]` (CM-13). Reading: ledger Ch.5; `negative_results.md` (reversible-exact-only-at-$\gamma=0$); `v3-reversible-o1`.

## 8.6 The lattice *is* the XY model — and the KT transition *is* the memory transition **[proven; verified — new wave-13]**

**Philosophy.** Parts IV and V gave us one register that forgets by diffusing around its ring. Now couple many of them. The question a physicist immediately asks is not *"how much can they store?"* but ***"is stored memory a phase?"*** — because if the array has a thermodynamic phase in which something is stable, then memory stops being a decaying transient and becomes a **property of the equilibrium state**, protected by the same mathematics that protects a magnet's magnetization. That question has a completely standard answer, and it is one of the most beautiful results in statistical physics: **the Kosterlitz–Thouless transition** (Nobel 2016). (Look-ups: *XY model*, *Kosterlitz–Thouless transition*, *Mermin–Wagner theorem*, *spin stiffness / helicity modulus*, *Nelson–Kosterlitz universal jump*, *vortex unbinding*.)

### 8.6.1 The reduction — exact, for the *designed* coupling

`SO2InvariantPotential` is exactly $SO(2)$-invariant on its channel, so restricted to the product of vacuum rings **every unit potential is constant in $\theta_i$**: all $\theta$-dependence of the joint $V$ comes from the couplings. With the **designed** `channel_spring_coupling` ($W_i = W_j = \mathbb 1$ on the channel), on $r_i = r_j = r^*$:

$$V_c = \kappa\lVert q_i - q_j\rVert^2 = 2\kappa r^{*2}\big(1 - \cos(\theta_i - \theta_j)\big),$$

a **pure first harmonic** — no $\cos2\Delta\theta$, no anisotropy, exact to all orders. Marginalizing the momenta (which factorise, §5.3) and the radii gives

$$\boxed{\ P(\{\theta\}) \ \propto\ \exp\Big(\tfrac{1}{T}\textstyle\sum_{\langle ij\rangle} J\cos(\theta_i-\theta_j)\Big),\qquad J = 2\kappa r^{*2}\ }$$

— **the classical XY model at the Langevin temperature.** **[proven; verified: $|J_{\rm meas}/J_{\rm pred} - 1| = 2.2\times10^{-16}$, all other harmonics $\le 7.4\times10^{-18}$; $\langle\cos\Delta\theta\rangle/\text{Gibbs} = 1.00028 \pm 0.00218$ over 9 cells spanning $20\times$ in $\gamma$]**

Two facts fall out immediately, both worth internalizing:

- **The stationary measure is $\gamma$-independent.** The Fokker–Planck adjoint $\mathcal L^* = \mathcal L^*_{\rm Ham} + \gamma\mathcal L^*_{\rm OU}$ annihilates $e^{-H/T}$ for **every** $\gamma>0$ (Liouville and FDT-OU each separately). **Friction changes the approach, never the destination.** For a *single* register this is the sharp form of "friction slows the inevitable": equilibrium on a compact coset is **uniform (Haar)**, so memory is a transient, with $n_{\rm uniform} = 2F^2\gamma/(\varepsilon^2T(2-\gamma))$. At $T=0$ there is no stationary measure to reach at all — and *that* is why the latch is exact.
- **The retro-derivation.** Linearising, the $N{=}2$ relative mode is $\mu^2_{\rm rel} = 2J/F^2 = 4\kappa/M_{\rm ch}$ and the spin-wave velocity is $a\sqrt{2\kappa/M_{\rm ch}}$ ⇒ sync time $\propto \kappa^{-1/2}$. **These are exactly V3's measured "communication pricing" law** (§8.2; measured slope $-0.499$). *V3 measured the XY spin-wave spectrum before anyone recognised it as one.*

### 8.6.2 The dictionary (with the two corrections that cost us a thesis rewrite)

| XY / O($n$) object | CLU object | note |
|---|---|---|
| $\beta = 1/T_{XY}$ | $1/T_{\rm Langevin}$ | **only** with `fdt` noise, $\gamma>0$, no governor, Newtonian mode |
| exchange $J$ (per bond) | $2\kappa r^{*2} = 2\kappa F^2/M_{\rm ch}$ | exact for the designed coupling |
| **spin stiffness $\rho_s(T\to0)$** | $\boxed{J = 2\kappa r^{*2}}$ | ⚠ **NOT $F^2$** — see below |
| spin-wave velocity | $a\sqrt{2\kappa/M_{\rm ch}}$ | ⇒ sync $\propto\kappa^{-1/2}$ |
| moment of inertia (**dynamics only**) | $F^2 = M_{\rm ch}r^{*2}$ | sets $D_\theta$; **cancels from the statics** |
| lattice sites / bonds | CLU units / coupling-graph edges | |

> ⚠ **Correction 1 — $\rho_s \ne F^2$.** $F^2$ is a **temporal** stiffness (an inertia) and it **cancels out of the Gibbs measure**. The **spatial** stiffness that appears in Mermin–Wagner and KT is the **exchange** $J = 2\kappa r^{*2}$. Both carry the condensate factor $r^{*2}$, but $F^2$ carries $M_{\rm ch}$ where $\rho_s$ carries $2\kappa$. $F^2$ *is* a stiffness — but only in the **0+1D** sigma model of a *single* register, $S = \int\!dt\,\tfrac12 F^2\dot\theta^2$, where it sets $D_\theta$.
> ⚠ **Correction 2 — $n = \dim(\mathcal G/\mathcal H) + 1$** (§4.4's update). O($n$) universality applies **only for spherical cosets**; a torus bank $T^k$ is $k$ *decoupled* XY models.

### 8.6.3 The memory–vortex correspondence (the sharpest claim in the program)

**First, kill the naive statement.** *"A 2-D array remembers"* is **false as usually said.** Equilibrium **forgets any generic written pattern $\{\theta_i^0\}$ — in every dimension.** Mermin–Wagner forbids long-range order in $d\le2$ for a continuous symmetry; the KT phase has only *quasi*-LRO, and the order parameter never acquires a mean.

**What a 2-D array below $T_{\rm KT}$ actually remembers is topological:** the **winding numbers**

$$(w_x, w_y) \in \pi_1(S^1)^2 = \mathbb Z^2 = H^1(T^2;\mathbb Z).$$

To change one, a **free vortex must cross the sample**, at free-energy cost $\Delta F = (\pi\rho_s - 2T)\ln(L/a) + E_{\rm core}$ — energy $\pi\rho_s\ln(L/a)$ against entropy $2T\ln(L/a)$. Hence, by Arrhenius (Ambegaokar–Halperin–Nelson–Siggia),

$$\boxed{\ \tau_{\rm winding} \ \propto\ L^{\,\pi\rho_s(T)/T\ -\ 2}\ }$$

The exponent is positive **iff** $\rho_s/T > 2/\pi$ — i.e. **iff $T < T_{\rm KT}$** — and it **vanishes exactly at $T_{\rm KT}$** by the **Nelson–Kosterlitz universal jump** $\rho_s(T_{\rm KT}^-)/T_{\rm KT} = 2/\pi$.

> ### ⇒ **The KT transition *is* the memory transition.**
> $T_{\rm KT}$ is *precisely* the temperature at which the topological register's lifetime stops growing with system size. The universal jump is not a curiosity sitting next to the memory statement — **it is the memory statement, rewritten.** In CLU variables, $T_{\rm KT} = 0.8929\,J = \mathbf{1.786\,\kappa r^{*2}}$.

**[proven modulo standard KT theory; the CLU content is the identification $\rho_s = J = 2\kappa r^{*2}$. $\tau_{\rm winding}$ was NOT simulated in 2D — that is the honest gap.]** Supporting numerics (Metropolis, $J{=}1$): $\rho_s$-vs-$2T/\pi$ crossings drift $1.0135\,(L{=}4) \to 0.9623\,(8) \to 0.9376\,(16) \to 0.9232\,(32)$; a $1/\ln^2 L$ fit extrapolates to $T_\infty = 0.8892$, **0.4% below** the literature $0.8929$.

### 8.6.4 Aliasing and vortices are the same winding

Here is the structural punchline, and it connects Part IV's *single*-register physics to this section's *many*-body physics through one invariant.

§4's compact coset has $\pi_1(S^1) = \mathbb Z$. That $\mathbb Z$ is what makes a **single** register *alias* when you write too hard ($p_{\rm crit} = 2\pi M_{\rm ch}r^*\gamma/\varepsilon$: the angle wraps). **The same $\mathbb Z$ is what permits vortices**, and vortices are what destroy the 2-D memory phase.

| coset $\mathcal M$ | $\pi_1$ | single register | 2-D array |
|---|---|---|---|
| $S^1$ ($SO(2)$) | $\mathbb Z$ | **aliases** at $p_{\rm crit}$ | **KT transition** at $T_{\rm KT}$ |
| $T^k$ (abelian bank) | $\mathbb Z^k$ | $k$-fold aliasing | $k$ decoupled KT transitions |
| $S^2$ ($SO(3)/SO(2)$) | $0$ | **no aliasing** | **no KT**: 2-D O(3) is asymptotically free, $\xi \sim e^{2\pi\rho_s/T}$ — finite at every $T>0$ |

> **This is the precise answer to "what do multiple symmetry modes buy?"** A non-abelian (spherical) bank buys **freedom from aliasing** and pays with **the loss of any thermodynamic memory phase in 2-D**. An abelian torus bank keeps $k$ independent KT phases and pays with $\mathbb Z^k$ aliasing. **Capacity and thermodynamic stability are traded through the same topological invariant.** **[proven + standard]**

### 8.6.5 The honest nulls, and the prerequisites (read before quoting anything above)

**Two nulls that must always be stated**, or the result is oversold:
1. **The global mode is not the transition.** $\Theta = \frac1N\sum\theta_i$ is an exact zero mode of any U(1)-invariant coupling with inertia $NF^2$, so $D_\Theta = D_\theta/N$ **for any graph, any $T$, any dimension** *(verified: $D_\Theta$ falls $2.390\times10^{-2} \to 1.505\times10^{-3}$ from $N{=}1$ to $16$, a factor **15.9 vs predicted 16**)*. **Coupling always buys a trivial factor $N$ of global-register lifetime — in 1-D as much as in 3-D. Never sell this as a phase transition.** The transition concerns the **relative** coordinates.
2. **1-D memory gets *worse* with size.** No vortices in 1-D; a phase slip is a *local* saddle of cost $O(J)$, so the slip rate is **extensive**: $\tau_{\rm winding} \propto 1/N$ *(verified: per-site rate $0.00656$, CV 11%)*. The 1-D chain has an exact, **parameter-free** correlation length $\xi = -1/\ln\!\big(I_1(J/T)/I_0(J/T)\big)$. **1-D degrades with size; 2-D below $T_{\rm KT}$ improves.** That contrast is the cleanest possible control.

**Five prerequisites, each measured rather than asserted** (all live in `future_work.md` as P1–P7):
- ⛔ **P1** — the `fdt` noise has a **NaN gradient at $\gamma=0$** ($\texttt{sqrt(0)}$ differentiated w.r.t. the learnable `log_mass`). At all repo defaults except `langevin_noise="fdt"`, `train_chlu` NaNs **every parameter at epoch 0's sleep step.** **No FDT-correct model has ever been trainable at defaults.** (N47)
- **P2** — under the `legacy` default the XY temperature is **47.5× off**; the dictionary is void.
- **P3** — **no governor.** A governed array is *always more ordered than its temperature says* ($\langle\cos\Delta\theta\rangle$/Gibbs $= 1.10, 1.36, \mathbf{1.93}$ at $T = 0.02, 0.05, 0.10$, **still drifting** after 30k steps). *It is annealing, not sampling — it has no temperature, hence no phase diagram.*
- **P4** — **Newtonian mode only.** The relativistic Langevin does not sample Gibbs (§5.3).
- **P5** — **the coupling must be designed.** The shipped `spring_coupling`'s random $W$ **breaks the global U(1)**: at init $P(J<0) = 0.52$ (*antiferromagnetic half the time*); trained 400 epochs on ground-truth-XY data it reaches $J/J_{\rm true} \approx 0.02$ with $h_2/|J| = 0.6\text{–}2.1$, while a **conformal** init ($W = \mathbb 1_2$) gives $J/J_{\rm true} = 1.03\text{–}1.07$ **at equal-or-better loss**. And a $p{=}2$ anisotropy has scaling dimension $x_2 = 1/2 < 2$ at the KT fixed point ⇒ it is a **relevant** perturbation that destroys the phase. **The shipped default would silently destroy any 2-D memory phase; the symmetry is free.** (N49; JKKN 1977)

**→ CLU.** The Nature-MI thesis in one sentence: ***a learned dynamical system has a memory–forgetting phase diagram whose universality class is set by the symmetry it stores in, and whose transition is set by the topology it stores across*** — provided you say that the stable memory is **topological**, that the universal jump **is** where winding lifetime stops growing with $L$, and that $D_\Theta = D_\theta/N$ is free in every dimension. The cheapest decisive test is **not** the 2-D run: it is the **1-D control**, whose $\xi = -1/\ln(I_1/I_0)$ has **nothing to fit**. `[proven + verified reduction (designed coupling); KT theory standard; $\tau_{\rm winding}$ unsimulated; $L{=}4$ — our largest lattice ever — is 13.5% off $T_{\rm KT}$ and cannot see the jump]`

> **⭐ Update (wave-15, `xy-1d-control`) — the dictionary is now validated on the REAL code path, and the 1-D go/no-go PASSED with nothing fitted. KT is GO.** Everything in §8.6 above was proven+verified on analytic/idealized maps; wave-15 ran the actual CLU-Langevin chain (`channel_spring` coupling, `fdt` noise, `newtonian` mode, no governor — prereqs P1–P5) and:
> - **The XY reduction holds exactly on the code path:** residual power in the non-first-harmonic part is $6.7\times10^{-34}$.
> - **The parameter-free correlation length matches:** the chain reproduces $\xi = -1/\ln\!\big(I_1(J/T)/I_0(J/T)\big)$ at $J = 2\kappa r^{*2}$ to **1.5–6.8% over 5 temperatures** — *no free parameter fitted*. This is the "$\xi$ has nothing to fit" test above, passed on real code.

# Part IX — Interference: the NTK bridge

## 9.1 The problem, formalized in our own training rule **[proven]**

**Philosophy.** All coordinates/units share $V_\theta$'s parameters. An update that helps memory $A$ moves the landscape *everywhere*, including under memory $B$ — θ-space catastrophic interference even when $A, B$ are far apart in state space (the Head's scaling caveat; the reason "just make one giant unit" cannot work).

**Math.** First-order Taylor in the update: $V_{\theta + \delta\theta}(q) \approx V_\theta(q) + \nabla_\theta V_\theta(q)^\top\delta\theta$. Our contrastive-divergence update from a wake/sleep pair is $\delta\theta = -\eta\big[\nabla_\theta V(q_{\rm wake}) - \nabla_\theta V(q_{\rm sleep})\big]$. Substitute:

$$\boxed{\ \delta V(q) = -\eta\,\big[\Theta(q, q_{\rm wake}) - \Theta(q, q_{\rm sleep})\big],
\qquad \Theta(q, q') := \nabla_\theta V_\theta(q)^\top \nabla_\theta V_\theta(q')\ }$$

— the **potential NTK**. Every CD update's side effect on the landscape anywhere is an evaluation of this kernel. (Dynamics-level version: the force kernel $\Theta_F(q,q') = \nabla_q\nabla_\theta V^\top\nabla_{q'}\nabla_\theta V$, a $d\times d$ matrix kernel — forces are what move states.) **Interference of $B$ on $A$ := off-diagonal kernel mass $\lVert\Theta(q_A, \Omega_B)\rVert / \lVert\Theta(q_A, q_A)\rVert$.** The design goal (P3-compliant) is *controllably gated* off-diagonals, not zero — shared structure is also generalization.

## 9.2 The mechanism catalog, with the math notes that matter

| mechanism | kernel effect | verdict |
|---|---|---|
| **modularity** (per-unit $V_i$; interaction only via $V_c$) | $\Theta$ *exactly* block-zero across unit-owned parameters; cross-talk confined to $\theta_{V_c}$, bounded by coupling magnitude | the only hard firewall — this is why V3 scales by mass **and** size **[proven structure]** |
| **irrep firewalls** (§4.1 language) | at an $\mathcal H$-symmetric point the Hessian commutes with the group action ⇒ Schur ⇒ block-diagonal over isotypic components: modes in different irreps don't mix **dynamically** at linear order [proven]. The *learning* firewall additionally needs the parameterization to factor across irreps | dynamics proven; NTK version architecture-dependent **[design hypothesis]** |
| **separable $V$** ($\sum_b V_b$ + weak cross-terms) | explicit zero blocks within one unit | modularity's little sibling **[proven structure]** |
| **local parameterizations** (RBF / local experts) | compactly supported $\Theta(q,q')$ ⇒ edits are local | strongest locality per parameter; costs smoothness/generalization **[evidenced, classic]** |
| **replay** (the CD buffer) | doesn't shrink $\Theta$; *constrains updates* — persistent negatives + old positives ≈ projecting $\delta\theta$ to preserve stored energies | the anti-interference machinery we already ship (and why buffer persistence, handover §7.4, matters) **[evidenced]** |
| **EWC / "mass for weights"** | penalty $\tfrac{\rho}{2}(\theta-\theta^*)^\top F(\theta-\theta^*)$ adds a *restoring force* in θ-space | see below — real, but it's a **spring, not inertia** |

**Resolving the "mass for weights" aesthetic flag [proven at linear order].** Under gradient flow, a Fisher penalty makes deviations from $\theta^*$ decay at rate $\rho\varphi$ along a Fisher eigendirection $\varphi$ — that is a **stiffness**: in our Def-2 language, a *spectral mass* $\mu_\theta^2 = \rho\varphi$ for the weight. True θ-**inertia** is something else we also have: momentum-SGD's $\beta$ (§1.1: $m_\theta = \varepsilon^2/\eta$). The two protect differently — **inertia filters transient noise but still yields to persistent pull (it integrates force); stiffness resists persistent pull indefinitely**. So EWC-style protection is precisely the θ-space twin of §3's spectral-mass law (large $\mu_\theta$ ⇒ deviations die fast ⇒ memory protected), and the q-space and θ-space memory stories unify under **spectral mass** — not under inertial mass, which is where the original "cute duality" was pointing. A satisfying consistency check of the whole vocabulary.

**→ CLU.** F5 Open-2 stays open (no solution claimed); the first measurement is defined: estimate $\Theta(q_A, q_B)$ across stored attractors, single big unit vs 2-unit modular lattice at matched parameters.

> **Update (wave-6; source w6) — the interference firewall is MEASURED, not asserted (CM-9; P12/V3.1 ANSWERED).** `v3-interference-ntk.md` (3 seeds, N=4/8) measured the *operational* interference of unit A on unit B as the **CD-update-induced basin displacement** $R_{B\leftarrow A}=\lVert\Delta F\text{ over B-block}\rVert/\lVert\Delta F\text{ over A-block}\rVert$ ($\Delta F$ = force-field change after one wake−sleep update localized on A; $R_{A\leftarrow A}\equiv1$):
> - **Shared-$V_\theta$ monolith:** $R\approx$ **0.20**, spatially **unstructured** ($R_{\rm nn}\approx R_{\rm far}\approx0.2$) — a single unit's update moves *every* other unit's landscape by ~20 % of the intended change. This is the θ-space catastrophic interference §9.1 warns of, measured.
> - **Modular lattice:** $R\approx$ **$2\times10^{-5}$** nearest-neighbour-only, **$R_{\rm far}\equiv0.0$ exactly** (zero cross-talk beyond the coupling graph). Ratio modular : monolith $\approx$ **1 : 9,000**. The leak is **$\propto\kappa^2$** (log-log slope 1.99, exactly 0 at $\kappa=0$), **mass-independent** (banded ≡ uniform to every printed digit — $\Theta$ is a *potential* NTK, blind to inertial $M$, which enters only $T(p)$), and **persists through training** (epochs 0/150/300, ~3–4 orders below monolith throughout — not an init artifact).
> - **Scaling (the V3.1 risk):** aggregate per-unit *received* interference $S_B=\sum_{A\ne B}R_{B\leftarrow A}$ is **O(N) for shared $V_\theta$** ($S=0.64\to1.38$ for N=4→8 — it *exceeds the unit's own signal* by N=8) and **O(1) for the modular lattice** ($\sim10^{-4}$, flat). ⇒ *"V3's guarantees survive scaling **only because of modularity**"* — quotable, with the monolith as the measured failure foil. F5 §6 catalog (modularity = the only hard firewall) confirmed quantitatively.
> - **Metric discipline (load-bearing for drafters).** The **raw NTK cosine is $\approx0.99$ for BOTH architectures** and does NOT distinguish them (shared low-level MLP features make $\nabla_\theta V$ correlated across any inputs). The firewall lives in the **wake−sleep *difference* structure**, where off-unit gradient components cancel. **Report the basin-displacement $R$, NEVER the NTK cosine, as the interference metric.**
> - *Limitation:* this isolates the θ-kernel; a trajectory-mediated banding channel (mass changes which states training visits, hence which loci drive updates) is unmeasured (named follow-up). A block-structured monolith (cat. iii) is the remaining "is it modularity or is it separate nets" control. `[measured, 3 seeds, N≤8, laptop]`

> **Update (wave-10; source `v3-referee` MF-1 + `v3-scaling-figure`; CM-9 → ⚠ UNDER RE-SCOPE) — the O(1) is *structural* (coordination-bounded), NOT an empirical growth rate. This correction is load-bearing for how §9 is read and written.** The wave-6 bullet above says the modular lattice is "**O(1)** ($\sim10^{-4}$, flat)" and the monolith "**O(N)**". A referee checked that against our own numbers and found the *growth-rate* reading is not supported:
> - The separation is measured at **exactly two lattice sizes, N=4 and N=8.** Across that single doubling: modular aggregate $S$ grows **$6.79\times10^{-5}\to1.74\times10^{-4}$ ($\times2.56$)**; monolith **$0.635\to1.384$ ($\times2.18$)**. The modular curve grew *faster in relative terms than the monolith*; the nearest-neighbour leak $R_{\rm nn}$ itself **doubles** ($4.53\times10^{-5}\to9.92\times10^{-5}$), and the seed error bars do not overlap — so the modular growth is real, not noise. **"Flat in N" is false as an empirical growth-rate statement**; on the log-y headline figure a reader sees two roughly-parallel positive-slope lines four decades apart.
> - **The result is nonetheless real — the correct argument is structural/topological, not empirical:** (a) **$R_{\rm far}\equiv0.0$ exactly** — non-adjacent units contribute *nothing*, so per-unit received interference is bounded by the chain's **coordination number ($\le2$), independent of $N$**; (b) the **~4-decade absolute separation** (modular $\approx2\times10^{-5}$ vs monolith $\approx0.20$; ratio $\approx1{:}9{,}000$); (c) the $\propto\kappa^2$ law (slope 1.99), mass-independence, and persistence through training — all unchanged.
> - **The honest sentence** (the one to carry): *"structurally $O(1)$: interference is confined to graph neighbours ($R_{\rm far}\equiv0$ exactly), so per-unit received interference is bounded by coordination number, not width — four decades below a monolith that has no such confinement."* A **scoped** growth-rate claim survives only if `v3-interference-extra` (N∈{2,6} + block-structured-monolith control, w11) shows **divergent** slopes.
> - **Physics reading (why this is the right lesson, not a retreat).** A locality statement and a scaling statement are different claims. What modularity buys is a **support property of the kernel** — $\Theta$ is exactly block-zero off the coupling graph — and support properties are *exact and $N$-independent*, whereas a growth exponent is an *asymptotic* claim needing several decades of $N$. We measured the former and wrote the latter. The same distinction separates "the propagator has finite range" from "the correlation length scales as …": the first is structure, the second is phenomenology. `[metric R, 3 seeds, N∈{4,8} only; framing final at w11]`
> - Metric discipline (unchanged, still load-bearing): report the basin-displacement $R$, **never** the raw NTK cosine ($\approx0.99$ for both architectures).
> - Related honesty note (`v3-referee` SF-1): "**the monolith interferes with itself more than it stores**" ($S=1.38>1$) leans on the $S{=}1$ reference line, which is an artifact of the $R$ normalization — *summed cross-unit $\Delta F$ equals one unit's own $\Delta F$* — **not a measured storage capacity**. Licensing the storage gloss needs the (unmeasured) **dynamical interference half-life**.

> **Update (wave-8; source `scout-modular-interference` — where this sits in the literature, for §9/related-work pointers).** The scout mapped the neighbourhood and returned **CLEAR at the specific-claim level, CROWDED at the neighbourhood level (cite the neighbours).** The *ingredients* are all owned and must be cited, never re-claimed:
> - **the phenomenon** — catastrophic interference from *shared* representations: McCloskey & Cohen 1989; French 1999.
> - **prevent-by-construction** — modular nets / mixture-of-experts (Jacobs 1991 → Shazeer 2017) and parameter-isolation continual learning (Kirkpatrick 2017 EWC; Mallya & Lazebnik 2018 PackNet).
> - **measure-to-mitigate** — the closest prior: **Doan et al. 2021** measure forgetting via an **NTK-overlap matrix**, and gradient-alignment / gradient-surgery methods (Riemer 2019 MER; Yu 2020 PCGrad) treat negative-inner-product gradients as the interference signal to suppress. Background: Jacot 2018 (NTK). Adjacent: **Boopathy et al. 2025** (modularity ⇒ *sample-complexity* scaling separation).
> - **What is unclaimed (CLU's defensible conjunction):** *a **measured** cross-module interference **kernel** with an explicit **coupling power-law** ($\propto\kappa^2$), **exactly zero beyond the coupling graph**, an **O(1)-vs-O(N)-in-width** architectural separation, on a **physics-structured lattice**, persisting through training.* The two sharp foils to name: **Doan** (interference measured, but as a data-dependent NTK-*overlap* on a monolith, not a coupling-law) and **Boopathy** (modularity gives a *sample-complexity* separation, not a *cross-talk* separation). Prior work either **prevents** interference or **measures** it only as a diffuse monolithic property to mitigate; none **prices** it as a graph-local coupling-law. Metric-discipline reminder for drafters: report the basin-displacement $R$, never the raw NTK cosine (≈0.99 for both — uninformative). `[bib verified; novelty = the conjunction, stated with in-sentence scope]`

## 10.4 If you want to go deeper (names to search, no obligation)

- **Mechanics & geometry:** Goldstein *Classical Mechanics*; Arnold *Mathematical Methods of Classical Mechanics* (advanced).
- **Structure-preserving integration (our §1 in full):** Hairer, Lubich & Wanner *Geometric Numerical Integration*; Leimkuhler & Matthews *Molecular Dynamics* (BAOAB, Langevin done right); Neal, *MCMC using Hamiltonian dynamics* (2011) — the ML-native entry point.
- **Symmetry & SSB (our §4):** any QFT text's SSB chapter (Peskin & Schroeder ch. 11; Schwartz ch. 28); Coleman, *Aspects of Symmetry* ("Secret Symmetry" lecture) for the classic treatment; David Tong's free online lecture notes for gentler ramps.
- **EFT (our §4.8):** A. Manohar, *Introduction to Effective Field Theories* (lectures); Georgi, *Weak Interactions and Modern Particle Theory*.
- **Statistical mechanics of the lattice (our §8.6, new w13):** Kosterlitz & Thouless (1973) and Kosterlitz's own 2016 Nobel lecture (the gentlest correct account); Nelson & Kosterlitz (1977) for the **universal jump** $\rho_s/T\to2/\pi$; Ambegaokar–Halperin–Nelson–Siggia (1980) for the **vortex-crossing Arrhenius law** behind $\tau_{\rm winding}\propto L^{\pi\rho_s/T-2}$; José–Kadanoff–Kirkpatrick–Nelson (1977) for the **relevance of $p$-fold anisotropy** ($x_p = p^2\eta/2$; our P5/N49); Hasenbusch (2005) for $T_{\rm KT} = 0.8929J$; Chaikin & Lubensky ch. 9, or Tong's *Statistical Field Theory* notes, for the ramp. Mermin–Wagner (1966) for what is and is **not** forbidden in $d\le2$.
- **Relativistic Langevin & the §5.3 no-go:** Jüttner (1911) for the momentum law; Dunkel & Hänggi, *Relativistic Brownian motion* (Phys. Rep. 2009) — the definitive account of why damping $p$ rather than $\nabla_pT$ changes the stationary measure; Leimkuhler & Matthews (again) for what a correct O-step looks like.
- **Open systems & horizons (our §7):** Breuer & Petruccione *Open Quantum Systems* (Lindblad); any black-hole-thermodynamics review for Hawking/Unruh vocabulary.
- **MCMC / test-time-compute-as-sampling (our §5.2, §6.5):** Neal, *MCMC using Hamiltonian dynamics* (2011) — HMC; Roberts & Tweedie 1996 — MALA (Metropolis-adjusted Langevin); Kirkpatrick 1983 / Wales & Doye 1997 — simulated annealing / basin-hopping (the governed cascade's family); the coset-diffusion caution is Mermin–Wagner-flavoured (no restoring force along a broken continuous symmetry ⇒ unbounded phase diffusion).
- **Interference & continual learning (our Part IX):** McCloskey & Cohen 1989 / French 1999 (catastrophic interference); Jacobs 1991 → Shazeer 2017 (MoE); Kirkpatrick 2017 (EWC) / Mallya & Lazebnik 2018 (PackNet); **Doan et al. 2021** (NTK-overlap matrix — the closest measured-interference prior); Riemer 2019 (MER) / Yu 2020 (PCGrad); Boopathy et al. 2025 (modularity ⇒ sample-complexity scaling separation). Full verified bib in `scout-modular-interference`.

# Part XI — The addressable memory: write, address, retrieve **[new wave-19]**

> **Why this Part exists, and why it is numbered XI rather than X.** Wave-19 turned the program's components into a **named architecture**: CLU as an *addressable dynamical memory*. This Part teaches what that means, what has actually been demonstrated, and — most importantly — **where the line between DESIGNED and EMERGENT falls**. It is appended after Part X rather than inserted before it because `§10.1`–`§10.4` are cross-referenced throughout the program; renumbering them would break citations.
>
> **⭐ Update (wave-24): this Part gained §11.14 — the Head's two plain-language records (**atoms** · **the two gates**), reproduced verbatim, with the measured numbers behind each image.** It is the onboarding entry point: read §11.14 first, then the sections it points at. The organising thesis it serves is the w24 reframe — **the store's value is control over memory in time (admission · lifetimes · isolation under sequential writes · compute-adaptive reads), not static lookup** — and §11.6 gained the **R2 boundary note** (the masked-recall demotion does NOT apply to the capacity law).**
>
> **⭐ Update (wave-23): this Part gained three subsections — §11.11 the learned read-in `φ` (and the *laundering control*, now a mandatory instrument), §11.12 the MVC-0 controller (the "single largest hole" of §11.8, now built), §11.13 test-time compute on a memory read (the retry curve and the three controls that make it evidence) — plus dated updates to §11.6 (the capacity law is PINNED: `min(2^d, ~32)`), §11.9 (the ceiling belongs to the WRITE OPERATOR), §11.7 (five new guardrails) and §11.8 (the honest position, revised).**
>
> ⚠ **READ THIS BEFORE YOU QUOTE ANYTHING IN THIS PART.** Every positive result below was obtained on a **hand-designed landscape with ZERO learning** (the sole w23 exception is `φ` in §11.11, which is learned **off** the CLU side and never through the store — the store itself stays designed). Nothing here is evidence of emergence, and the program's own strongest negatives (**N46**: the emergent arm has no coset register; **CM-16a**: the latch is designed-only, a **12-order** flatness gap) say the emergent versions do *not* appear. **The designed-vs-emergent boundary is the single most misquotable thing in the program.** It is restated at every subsection below, deliberately and repetitively.

## 11.1 The object: memory lives in the landscape, not in a buffer

**Philosophy.** A transformer stores representations in a buffer and *mixes* them with a weighted sum. A CLU stores items **in the shape of its own energy landscape** and *retrieves* them by **launching a particle and watching where it goes**. There is no separate memory array — writing means deforming `V_θ`, and reading means integrating the equations of motion.

**Look-up.** Content-addressable memory; attractor networks (Hopfield); *location-addressable* dynamical retrieval (Kong, Brewer & Lai, *Nat. Commun.* 2024 — see the warning in §11.7).

**Math.** The memory is the pair $\mathbb{M} = (V_\theta, \mathcal{A})$, where $\mathcal{A} = \{a_i\} = \{(m_i, q_{0i}, p_{0i})\}$ is the **address codebook** — an index, not a store. A **stored item** is a triple $(S, \rho, \tau)$:
- $S \subset \Gamma$ — an invariant or slowly-decaying structure of the flow: a basin/fixed point $(q^*,0)$, a periodic orbit or invariant torus, a coset orbit (register), or a broken-vacuum point set;
- $\rho$ — the map from trajectories near $S$ to the payload, **required to be linearly decodable** from the trajectory (the program's read bar). Payload channels: *location* ($q^*$, $d$ reals), *spectrum* (local Hessian frequencies, readable as trajectory frequencies), *register value* (coset coordinate), *identity* ($\log_2 K$ bits);
- $\tau$ — retention: $\tau = \infty$ **iff** $\mu^2_S \equiv 0$ exactly (the latch — **designed-only**, CM-16a/N46); otherwise the Part III laws apply unchanged.

**Write** = a parameter displacement $\delta\theta$ that creates or moves $(S,\rho,\tau)$. **Read** = launch address $a$, collect $\Phi^N(a)$, apply a linear functional.

**→ CLU.** The **address is $(m, q_0, p_0)$** and the **read is the trajectory**. The roles are now sharp (Props 1–3, §11.3): $q_0$ selects **location**; $(m,p_0)$ jointly select the **energy shell** (= which barriers are crossable = scope); $m$ alone selects **timescale**, and anisotropically, **shape**. ⚠ **Correction that is load-bearing: in the shipped code `log_mass` is a *global model parameter* shared by every rollout — as shipped, `m` is not an address component at all.** Making the architecture's "select the appropriate mass particle" step meaningful requires a **per-launch** mass supplied by the selector (see §11.5).

**Status: [proven] (the formal object) / [design hypothesis] (that it is the right architecture).** Source: `clu-memory-architecture` §1.

## 11.2 The loop, as actually demonstrated — **designed, zero learning**

**What was built.** A hand-designed `RingRegisterPotential` / `ThreeModePotential` (`chlu/core/memory_potentials.py`, `chlu exp-retrieval`), with **no training anywhere** except gradient descent on the address in the one arm that failed (§11.4).

**What works, with numbers:**

| quantity | measured | reading |
|---|---|---|
| payload write→retrieve error | **9.98e-4** | the loop closes |
| linear-codebook read, 2 / 4 / 8 items | **1.000 / 1.000 / 0.992** | selective retrieval |
| ⚠ **blank-landscape control (payload-only)** | **0.469** vs chance 0.500 | **the load-bearing number** |
| ⚠ **blank-landscape control (full-state read)** | **1.000** | ⛔ **it reads the address back** |
| durability, read at 60…1188 steps | **1.000 at every position** | retrieval is a **fixed point**, not a decaying trace |
| durability at **γ = 0** | **0.813** | ⚠ **retrieval REQUIRES dissipation** |
| permanent-item drift | **4.17e-7** | designed SO(2) ⇒ torque-free |
| **corruption of the permanent item by a decaying write** | **4.17e-7** | **zero cross-talk** |
| decaying half-life | **66.87** vs $2\ln 2/\gamma$ = **69.31** | −3.5%, the Part III law holds |

**⛔ Three qualifiers, each of which changes what the table means:**
1. **The full-state read scores 1.000 on a BLANK landscape.** It is reading the *address* back out, not the memory. This is why the payload-only read exists. **Any retrieval claim that reads the address plane is decorative** — a reader can verify a memory claim by asking one question: *what does it score on an empty landscape?*
2. **Write locality is DESIGNED, not emergent.** The zero-corruption result follows from **additive separability + exact symmetry**, by construction. An MLP potential has **no locality by default** — every write perturbs every stored item (the measured instance is sleep-phase erosion, CM-6). This is a property of the design, not a discovery.
3. **Retrieval requires dissipation** (γ=0 → 0.813). The conservative particle never settles, so the read is unstable. Hold this next to §11.4, where dissipation turns out to be exactly what makes the memory *unlearnable*.

**Status: [verified] under design; [no evidence] for any emergent counterpart.** Source: `clu-retrieval-demo` §1/§3 (single seed 42, single 2-D geometry).

> **🔴 Update (wave-20/21): the loop does NOT survive a LEARNED landscape, and one row of the table is superseded.** (i) **No learned rung clears strict 0.9 at both K=4 and K=8** (5 seeds); the minimum viable design is *essentially the whole designed landscape* plus a small residual. **Write locality is design-only:** the 4.17e-7 above becomes **2.9e-2…5.0e-1** under learning, and the best learned rung is **destroyed by one subsequent write (1.000 → 0.000)** — registry **N67** (`learned-landscape-write-read`; w21 amends the *curve* — a better learned family passes rung 1 — not the conclusion). (ii) **The blank-control protocol above was strengthened (N68):** under a learned `V` a nearest-centroid blank scores **0.992–1.000 on an empty landscape** — take blanks over the STRONGEST read in use, prefer value-recovery. (iii) **The "retrieval REQUIRES dissipation" row (0.813 at γ=0) is SUPERSEDED by the two-phase read:** fidelity depends on **γ_address only** (spread over γ_read = 0.0000) — γ_read = 0 is *permitted*, which is exactly what keeps the read Prop-4 gradient-safe. What survives learning: write→relax consistency (basin ≥0.98, rungs 0–2) and value recovery.

## 11.3 Prop 2 — hard read isolation: the guarantee attention lacks **[proven, verified]**

**Philosophy.** In attention, softmax touches **every** key: a query is always a blend, and "isolation" is a matter of degree. In a landscape memory, a particle that lacks the energy to climb a barrier **cannot reach what is behind it** — not approximately, but as a theorem about the flow.

**Look-up.** Energy shells; accessible phase-space regions; basin of attraction.

**Math.** The reachable set of address $a$ lies in the connected component of $\{q : V(q) \le E(a) - \min T\}$ containing $q_0$. Crossing a barrier of height $h$ from rest requires $p_0^2/2M > h$, giving the threshold
$$M^* = \frac{p_0^2}{2h}.$$
Heavier than $M^*$ ⇒ confined. *Verified:* predicted $M^* = 0.36$; the sweep **crosses at $M=0.34$ and confines at $M=0.38$** — within one grid cell.

**→ CLU.** **This is the program's strongest defensible differentiator from attention.** A sub-barrier particle **cannot** read a foreign item; softmax mixes every key. ⚠ **State it as a guarantee about what a read CANNOT touch — never as a capability or accuracy claim.** Note also its cost: the same barrier that guarantees isolation is the separatrix that makes addressing unlearnable by gradient (§11.4).

**Two companions worth knowing** (both [proven, verified]):
- **Prop 1 — mass is an exact time dilation.** For any kinetic $T = \tau(\|p\|^2/M)$ (Newtonian *and* the shipped relativistic), $p = \sqrt{M}\tilde p$, $t = \sqrt M \tilde t$ maps the $M$-flow to the $M{=}1$ flow. **With $p_0 = 0$ the path image in $q$-space is exactly $M$-independent** — only traversal *speed* changes. Verified: turning-point gap between $M=0.25$ and $M=4.0$ is **1.07e-4** (Newtonian *and* relativistic); frequency ratio **3.993 / 4.007** vs the exact $\sqrt{16}=4$. ⚠ **Consequence: mass-only keys with a path-image read PROVABLY cannot discriminate.** To make mass a key you need $p_0 \neq 0$ (energy gating), anisotropic per-channel masses, or a time/frequency-resolved read.
- **Prop 3 — reach and latency.** The governor caps $|\dot q_i| \le c/\sqrt{M_i}$ ⇒ $N$-step reach $\le N\varepsilon c/\sqrt{M_i}$. **Mass is a scope key**: heavy = local read with long latency to distant content, light = global. Wormholes implemented as *potential couplings* modify the topology of $\{V \le E\}$ **without touching symplecticity** — they break *locality*, not the physics guarantees.

**Status: [proven, verified].** Source: `clu-memory-architecture` §2.

> **🔴 Update (wave-20/22): Prop 2 does NOT transfer to a TRAINED block — scope every use of this section.** (i) **As a recall advantage over attention it is RETIRED:** in a matched harness, attention is flat over an 8× distractor sweep (drop **0.001**) while the trained CLU degrades (drop **0.137**) — the Hub's own task premise, falsified as the agent pre-registered (**N69**, `primitive-harness`). Attention has an O(T) KV cache; *barrier confinement can protect an already-stored item, it cannot create capacity.* (ii) **As input-noise robustness it does not transfer either:** the trained gated block is worse at every noise level on a steeper slope (**N80**, `gated-write-performance` §4). (iii) **And "symplectic ⇒ stable extrapolation" fails in the trained, driven slot** (worst of three, blows up past the trained horizon — **N79**; UnICORNN occupies the symplectic-stability ground with a theorem we lack). **What Prop 2 remains:** an exact guarantee about a *designed* store (it is why the designed CLU out-denoises a one-step Hopfield read, N81), the fiber-payload answer to Ramsauer, and partial support for the one surviving *performance* edge — **capacity under item load, 2.1× vs a matched, rescued GRU at kv=16 (never 19×; N78)**, where attention still wins.

## 11.4 Props 4 & 5 — the gradient dichotomy, and the inversion that killed address learning **[proven, verified]**

**Philosophy.** The folk objection to training through a long rollout is *"gradients explode or vanish."* For a CLU, **both halves of the folk story are wrong, and the truth is worse than either.**

### Prop 4 — a conservative read has no vanishing-gradient problem, ever

**Math.** The $\gamma=0$ Verlet tangent map is **symplectic**, so its singular values pair as $(\sigma, 1/\sigma)$. Therefore
$$\|J_N\| \ge 1 \quad \text{always.}$$
Growth is **algebraic (exponent 1)** in regular, shear-dominated regions and $e^{\lambda N\varepsilon}$ in chaotic ones. *Verified (exact tangent maps):* quartic-well log-log slope **1.0014** (pre-registered 1.00 ± 0.15); Hénon–Heiles $\lambda = $ **0.098** (matrix-norm fit) cross-checked at **0.111** by independent vector-Benettin — 12% apart, both inside the registered band. At $t=1000$: chaotic $\|J\| = 10^{47.6}$ vs regular $10^{2.8}$ — **44.8 orders apart**.

**→ CLU, and it is good news.** ⭐ **The memory-useful structures and the gradient-benign structures are the same set.** Basins, coset registers and invariant tori are *regular*; the pathological regime is *chaotic transport between them*. So BPTT-through-rollout is usable exactly where the memory lives. This also explains why CLU's long-horizon non-decay does not doom its gradients: **the non-decay comes from $|\lambda| = 1$ marginal (regular) dynamics, not from chaos.**

### Prop 5 — dissipation, not conservation, is what kills address gradients

**Math.** For $\gamma_r > 0$ the read endpoint **is** the basin fixed point — locally **constant** in $(q_0, p_0, m)$. The residual gradient through a finite window decays as $((1-\gamma_r)|\lambda_{\rm int}|)^n$ (≈ $e^{-18}$ at $\gamma_r = 0.02$, $n=900$: below any finite-difference or SGD noise floor).

*Verified, in the most dramatic form available:* the damped-read GD arm's loss is **frozen at 7.9035443 to seven significant digits across 4000 gradient steps** — a literal zero-gradient plateau, 0/18 — against the $\gamma=0$ arm which moves (loss 7.90 → 2.65 median). The engineer's independent γ-scan agrees: $\|\nabla_{\rm address}\|$ falls **7 orders** (3.3e-1 → 5.0e-8) across $\gamma = 0 \to 0.05$, while the read only becomes **usable** at $\gamma \gtrsim 0.01$. **There is no window where both are good.**

**→ CLU — the mechanism, stated as a candidate no-go.** A **readable** memory requires the retrieved state to be a fixed point: the same item must come back regardless of small query variation. That is exactly $\partial(\text{final state})/\partial(q_0) \to 0$ inside a basin. **And the address gradient IS that derivative.** Hence:

> **Candidate proposition: *a dynamically-robust addressable memory has no useful address gradient.*** Making retrieval robust is precisely the act of destroying the signal that gradient descent on the address would need.

The measured consequence is a **staircase** loss surface — 8 local minima at K=8, **cliff ratio 1.7e7**, flat plateaus separated by discontinuities — on which gradient descent does nothing. **Address learning by GD failed under four protocols across two independent agents and harnesses:** engineer **4.2% (1/24)**, 0% antipodal; theorist plain GD **2/18 strict**, Adam **1/18**, naive hybrid **3/18 ≈ chance**, energy-annealed **0/18**. ⚠ **The failure is localized to CAPTURE** — cross-basin re-localization, an *energy* criterion requiring energy to be shed *while positioned over the target*, across a separatrix layer with fractal basin boundaries. **Escape and transport DO work** (9/18 basin-level).

**⚠ What this retracts.** The Hub predicted **exploding** gradients from non-contraction; the engineer pre-registered *"gradient norm grows with rollout length."* **Both falsified.** Friction contracts, and contraction is what kills addressability.

**Design consequence (adopt it):** **learn addresses at $\gamma_r = 0$; deploy content-addressable retrieval at $\gamma_r > 0$.** And do not fund further gradient-based cross-basin address search without a new mechanism — the surviving story is **derived addresses** (the codebook as a *function of the landscape* — the writer knows where it wrote), **write-side restructuring**, and **retry-as-capture** (the boost ladder becomes load-bearing, not a luxury).

**Status: [proven, verified] (both props); [strongly evidenced] (the cross-basin negative — 4 protocols, mechanism identified).** Sources: `clu-memory-architecture` §2/§5, `clu-retrieval-demo` §5b/§6. Registry: **N61, N62**.

## 11.5 Prop 6 — why masses must be per-address, and the corrected D1 partition **[proven, verified to machine precision]**

**Philosophy.** Part II's inertial mass $M$ is the primitive's *budget allocator*. But a single global $M$ shared by every rollout is a **gauge** direction — the loss cannot see it — which is why the program has measured "no mass hierarchy" three separate times (N7/N64, CM-5).

**Math.** With one global $(V, M)$, mass is gauge: $\log\omega^2 = \log k - \log M$, and D1 freezes it. With **$K$ items sharing one landscape but carrying per-item masses**, only the *overall scale* remains gauge — **all ratios become wake-visible**. The GD dynamics decouple: the difference coordinate converges at rate $2\eta_m$, independent of the fast lever.

*Verified:* $|d - d^*| = $ **2.2e-14**. The scale coordinate collapses with partition **0.047619 = 1/21**.

⚠ **A pre-registration that failed, and the corrected law.** The registered constant was $1/11$ (the two-lever form). **It is wrong for multi-item settings.** With $K$ items the shared fast lever receives a $K$-fold richer gradient, so
$$\text{partition} = \frac{\eta_m}{\eta_m + K\,\eta_k}, \qquad K=2 \Rightarrow 1/21,$$
which matches the measurement to **13 digits**. **D1 scale-collapse worsens linearly in the number of items served by the shared lever.**

**→ CLU.** Two consequences, both architectural:
1. ⭐ **Masses must be PER-ADDRESS codebook entries, not a global model parameter.** Once per-address, the D1 frozen-mass threat **dissolves**: the ratios that matter train exactly, even while the global scale never will. As shipped this requires an API change (a per-launch mass override) — *the single code change the architecture cannot proceed without*.
2. **The Head's "allow arbitrary choices where they don't affect performance" is exactly the surviving gauge** (the overall scale). Pin it with a deadband; let the ratios restructure.

**Status: [proven, verified to 2.2e-14 / 13 digits].** Source: `clu-memory-architecture` §2 Prop 6. ⚠ **Anyone reusing the D1 partition in a multi-item setting must use the $K$-form** (now canonical in `claims_matrix.md` §1).

## 11.6 Capacity has THREE regimes — and an emergent CLU is stuck in the worst one

**Philosophy.** "How many items fit?" has no single answer. Packing items into a landscape passes through a regime where the barriers that protect them have *partly* merged — and that intermediate regime is **worse than maximal packing**, which is genuinely counter-intuitive.

**Math / measurement** (2-D ring, $R/s \approx 5.7$, jitter $\sigma = 0.12$, time-averaged linear read):

| K | 2 | 4 | 8 | **16** | 24 | 32 | 48 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| window-mean read acc | 1.00 | 1.00 | 1.00 | **0.49** | 0.96 | 0.89 | 0.73 |
| physical selectivity | 1.00 | 1.00 | 1.00 | **0.77** | 0.96 | 0.90 | 0.74 |

- **Regime 1 — barrier-protected** ($K \le K_{\rm pack} \approx 2\pi R/4.4s \approx 8$): selectivity **100%**, guaranteed by Prop 2 — a written particle *cannot* visit a foreign item. Long-horizon survival exact ($N = 20\,000 \equiv N = 2000$). Packing bound in $d$ dimensions: $(1 + 2R/w)^d$ — **exponential in latent dim**; capacity in *count* is not the obstruction.
- **Regime 2 — ⚠ the washboard death zone** ($K \approx 16$): partial well merger leaves finite azimuthal barriers; **17.2% of items are transported ≥1 sector** by the residual washboard force and selectivity **crashes to 0.49–0.77**. ⭐ **This is exactly where v5-gate's emergent register lives (2–3 washboard minima, 1–1.6 bits): AN EMERGENT CLU IS STUCK IN REGIME 2.**
- **Regime 3 — continuum register** ($K \ge 24$): wells merge into a ring valley, the azimuthal direction becomes Goldstone-like, and **the written angle is retained EXACTLY** (drift ≥1 sector = **0.000** at both $K=24$ and $K=48$). Selectivity **recovers to 0.96**; capacity becomes **write-resolution-limited, not dynamics-limited**: $K_{\max} \approx$ circumference$/(\approx\!4\max(\sigma_{\rm write}, \ell_\theta))$, with $\ell_\theta$ the §CM-16(d) thermal persistence length. Per-register capacity $\log_2(\text{range}/\ell_\theta)$ — **tens of bits**.

⚠ **The non-monotone recovery was NOT predicted** — the source calls the regime split a genuine surprise.

**⛔ Do NOT quote "8 items" as CLU's capacity.** The independent retrieval harness measured a ceiling of 8, but the law behind it is a **ring** statement, $K_{\max} \approx 0.2 \cdot 2\pi/\sigma_\theta$ (→ 8.4 predicted, 8 measured) — *query precision vs site spacing*, exactly like a physical addressable memory. **The failure is ADDRESSING, not storage**: payload regression $R^2 = 0.971$ at $K=8$ where addressing still works. **Both w19 agents independently warned against quoting it** (registry **W2**; the $d$-dimensional scaling test is its promote-trigger and the cheapest decisive follow-up in the program).

**→ CLU, said plainly.** With **emergent** structure the honest per-register capacity is **1–1.6 bits** and the system sits in regime 2 — *not a transformer competitor*. With **designed** structure: tens of bits per register, $\infty$ retention, item count exponential in $d$, read isolation guaranteed. ⇒ **the competitor claim is CONDITIONAL ON DESIGNED-IN STRUCTURE**, which is the SDR-prerequisite conclusion restated.

**Status: [strongly evidenced] (one 2-D geometry per harness, two independent harnesses agreeing on regime 1's ceiling).** Sources: `clu-memory-architecture` §4, `clu-retrieval-demo` §4.

> **⭐ Update (wave-20/22): the d-scaling test RAN, and this section's two open numbers are settled — one in CLU's favour, one against.**
> **(i) DESIGNED capacity is EXPONENTIAL — the 8-item ceiling is DEAD.** `K_max = 4·2^d` exactly over d=2–8 (log₂K_max = d+2, **R²=1.0000**; **1024 items at d=8**), blank passing every cell; selectivity 1.000 at every K and d; **no washboard death zone at any d ≥ 2 — regime 2 is a 1-D-ring property.** Watch-item **W2 is CLEARED**. ⚠ But the packing bound quoted above, `(1+2R/w)^d`, is **FALSIFIED as a law** (measured β = 0.96/1.28/1.65 vs β = d): use the repair **`K_max = (2R/Δ_req)^{d_eff}`** with **`Δ_req ≈ 3.1·max(w, σ_query)`** (a d-independent resolution floor — also the mechanism behind the capacity↔noise trade measured on MNIST, N81) and `d_eff ≈ 0.72–0.83·d` (**N70**, `address-space-dimension-scaling`).
> **(ii) The LEARNED wall is GEOMETRY, not a learning ceiling — but its exponent is UNPINNED.** With a designed atom mechanism and learned content: **K_learned = 4, 8, 8, 32, 8 at d = 2,3,4,6,8** — H-LEARNING (flat wall at 8) **rejected** (d=6 clears 32), yet not a clean exponential (5-pt base 1.18 R²0.26; d≤6 base 1.64 R²0.94), and **the d=8 point is an optimizer failure, near-provably** (more geometric room than d=6; the write stalled on trivially-separated cells; the budget must scale with the address DIMENSION). Learned pays a widening tax vs designed: ¼ → ⅛ → ≤1/16 (**N82**, `designed-mechanism-learned-content` §1).
> **(iii) Positioning discipline that now travels with ANY capacity sentence:** exponential capacity is the modern-Hopfield field's own headline (Hu–Wu–Liu 2024: optimal capacity = optimal spherical codes, `M* ≍ c^D`) — **our novelty is the measured constant and mechanism, not the exponential**; and **capacity-per-parameter is a comparison we LOSE (≈1.1–1.6 bits/param vs the transformer's measured 2)** — the exponential is in *address dimension*, not parameters; always carry the denominator (**N77**).

> **⭐⭐ Update (wave-23) — the learned law is now PINNED, and item (ii) above is SUPERSEDED: `K_learned(d) = min(2^d,\ K_{\rm ceiling} \approx 32)`.**
> Re-run with a **dimension-aware atom budget** (`n_atoms ≥ 512·(√2)^d`, floors {1024, 1448, 2048, 2896, 4096, 8192} at d={2,3,4,5,6,8}), **per-point budget-adequacy re-checks at 2× atoms**, and a d=4 capacity-vs-budget **saturation curve across a 16× atom range** (`dimension-aware-budget`):
> - **Budget-adequate walls: {4, 8, 16, 32, 32, 32}.** For **d ≤ 5 the wall is exactly `2^d`** — one ladder-rung doubling per dimension, **the same rate as the designed `4·2^d`** ⇒ ⭐ **"the wall is geometry" is VINDICATED for d ≤ 5.**
> - **For d ≥ 5 a d-INDEPENDENT WRITE CEILING at K ≈ 32 binds.** K=64 is unwritable at **d=6 AND d=8** despite 4× different geometric room (site sep 0.795 vs 0.908); d=6 K=64 **fails HARDER at 2× atoms** (0.855 → 0.809, 36.9k → 73.7k params) and d=4 K=32 is **flat at ~0.83 across a 16× atom sweep**. ⇒ **the ceiling is neither geometry (designed reaches ≥256) nor parameter count — it is the static GLOBAL WRITE OPERATOR** (§11.9).
> - **The w22 d=8 = 8 stall was BUDGET STARVATION and is gone (d=8 = 32; monotonicity restored); H-LEARNING-at-8 stays rejected; the designed/learned tax stops widening** (w22 ¼→⅛→≤1/16 monotone; now plateaus ~1/8 for d ≥ 5).
> - ⛔ **Never quote the raw-sweep base √2 (1.44) — or `d^1.62` — as the capacity exponent.** That fit averages base-2 growth (d≤5) with the ~32 plateau (d≥5); it is an artifact of the crossover, not a law (**N92**).
> ⚠ **Thin numbers:** d=4=16 and d=5≥32 rest on **2-seed** re-checks and the write is **seed-fragile exactly at the 0.9 rung** (d=4 K=16: 3-seed 0.876 vs 2-seed 0.93–0.98); d=2,3,6,8 are 3-seed. **Reading pointers:** `dimension-aware-budget` §0–§3 (adequacy probe, saturation curve, the d=8 verdict); registry **N92** (supersedes N82's "unpinned exponent").

> **⭐ Update (wave-24) — the ceiling SURVIVES every write lever, the low-`d` walls get a third seed, and "the ceiling is the WRITE OPERATOR" is now CONTESTED (do not quote as settled).**
> - **`write-ceiling-break` (N96):** no write operator raises `K_ceiling≈32` at d∈{4,6,8} — masked/sequential writes *backfire* (capacity collapses below 32), crowding-aware and combo also fail; the scale-invariance ablation is null (\|Δ\|=0.010) ⇒ scale-dilution ruled out. The law `min(2^d, ~32)` is **robust to the write operator.**
> - **`multi-seed-w23` follow-up:** **d=4 = 16 SURVIVES at 3 seeds (0.928)**; **d=5 = 32 SOFTENS — 0.898 mean, straddling the 0.90 bar** ⇒ quote the low-`d` regime as **"≥16, consistent with 32", NOT a clean 32.**
> - ⚠⚠ **MECHANISM CONTESTED (Hub ruling, w24):** the phrase *"the ceiling belongs to the WRITE OPERATOR"* (in the w23 update above and §11.9) must now carry a qualifier — a competing **geometric fixed-width-lock** account (`lattice-capacity-theory` §4.2: `sep_crit ≈ 2.4–3.0×well width` at every wall d=2…8; predicts the d-independence via `minsep ∝ K^{-1/d}`; reproduces the `2^{-d}` tax from `well_width 0.15` vs `atom_init_width 0.30`) is under test. The scale-invariance arm rescaled **depths/margins, not width+radius**, so it does not test it. **Decisive free check:** dump the trained `log_width` (`lattice §5.0`); the one-flag `atom_init_width 0.30→0.15` run (predicted ×`2^d`) is a route to R2 that needs no lattice. ⇒ **write "learned capacity is capped write-side (mechanism CONTESTED — optimization/representational limit vs geometric width-lock, adjudicator = the trained-`log_width` dump)", never "the ceiling is the write operator" as settled.** See **N92 (w24 update) / N96**.

> **✅⭐⭐ Update (wave-25) — the CONTEST above is RESOLVED, and the answer is a third thing: the ceiling is a READ-OUT REACH condition (`r2-geometry-revival`, N100; the full picture is §11.15 Record 3).**
> - **The adjudicator ran and behaved as designed.** The free `log_width` dump **passed** its registered falsifiers (trained widths **0.215–0.364**, never near 0.18; ratio range **1.36×**; a **perfect d ≥ 4 PASS/FAIL classifier**, boundary **2.30 | 2.49**, *including the out-of-sample d=8 K=64 crack cell*) and therefore **authorised** the one-flag experiment — which **refuted the account that authorised it**: `atom_init_width 0.30 → 0.15` moves `K_learned(4)` **below 16** (**0.9368 ± 0.0133 → 0.5944 ± 0.0164**, 3 seeds, **28 SE**, flat at 2× atoms). ⛔ **`lattice §5.2` is REFUTED BY EXPERIMENT and its `×2^d` prediction is dead.**
> - **What binds instead:** the read launches on the payload = 0 manifold, so the width is squeezed **below** by the query noise ($s \gtrsim \sigma_q$) and **above-usefully** by the read-out excursion (**$s \gtrsim |a|_{\max}/\kappa$, $\kappa \approx 3$**). Measured per-item (corr **−0.887** with $|a_i|$; large-$|a|$ items retrieve at **0.172** while small-$|a|$ items are at **1.000**; force at the launch manifold collapses **5.1×**) and **confirmed by intervention** (half excursion, tolerance halved: **d=4 K=32 goes 0.824 → 1.000**, d=4 K=64 → **0.9922**, at *both* widths).
> - ⛔ **BOTH prior attributions are retired as CAUSES** — *"the ceiling belongs to the WRITE OPERATOR"* (this section's w23 update, §11.9, roadmap v0.7) **and** the geometric width-lock. ✅ **What each keeps:** the joint dig's failure signature stands (*the write objective reaches loss 0 while retrieval already fails*), and **`sep/w_atom` is a real, sharp d ≥ 4 correlate** worth reporting **as a correlate** (forcing it to 4.90 fails at 0.594 — the correlate is not the cause). ⭐ A **second, additive** contribution is measured the same wave: **localized atom init (N98) is worth +0.051 / +0.082** at d=6 K=64 ⇒ part of the "joint dig" penalty is **atoms starting inside other items' wells**.
> - **Two other numbers in this section move:** ⛔ **"d=8 K=64 fails"** is superseded — the in-run monolithic control is **0.9121 ± 0.0029** (consistent with w24's 0.9067 ± 0.0068), so that cell **is not a wall**; and **sharding fixes d=6 K=64** (0.9251 ± 0.0030 vs 0.8802 ± 0.0075 at identical init/atoms) while **not** fixing the geometry-bound d=4 K=32 (0.89–0.90 with routing removed) ⇒ **`K_total = min(K_addr, Σ_r K_write)`; `K = 32N` is NOT unclamped** (**N105/N106/N107**).
> - ⛔ **New never-quote, and it is the one most likely to be violated: no `K_learned` at `pscale ≠ 1`.** The excursion cells are a **mechanism result at a different task point** (this harness's payload channel has **no read-out noise**). The fair successor — multi-channel payload and/or an annealed read, **payload noise ON** — is w26's heaviest experiment. See **N100** and the w25 blocks on **N92/N96**.

> **⭐⭐ Update (wave-26) — THE WALL MOVED, AND IT MOVED FROM THE READ-OUT SIDE. This section's `K_learned` numbers are now scoped to the SHIPPED read-out code.** The successor experiment ran with **payload read-noise ON**, and both arms work at **d = 4**: an **anisotropic annealed read** (widen only the payload channel, `κ: 3 → 1` over 4 stages, the same 1 200 Verlet steps split across them) takes the w23 *firm* wall `d=4 K=32` from **0.8210 ± 0.0028 to 0.9997 ± 0.0005** (3 seeds, **~110 SE**) at **zero extra parameters, dimensions or steps and no change to the stored format**; a **multi-channel payload code** (Δ = 2/(K−1) held at the 1-channel value, so `log₂K` bits at the same per-axis noise, excursion `max‖a‖` 1.000 → 0.091) takes `K_learned(4)` to **128 = `K_designed(4)`** ⇒ **the learned/designed prefactor tax at d = 4 closes from 1/8 to 1/1 at every noise level measured** (**N109**).
> - ⭐ **Three controls make it a mechanism:** **`readonly`** (anneal the value phase only) reproduces the baseline **to 4 dp** ⇒ **the whole gain is address acquisition** — *this is the explanation of `basin ≡ strict`, which this section has carried unexplained since w23*; **`static`** (widen, never sharpen) reaches **basin 0.9993** and reads the **wrong value**; **`spectator`** (m=4 dims, +43 % parameters, code left long) buys **+0.034** where the code buys **+0.195**.
> - ⭐ **The w25 ban is vindicated by measurement:** the `pscale` free lunch **dies under read noise** (0.9995 → 0.8936 at σ_obs = 0.010 → 0.5894 at 0.020) where the constant-Δ code passes at **0.9980**. ⛔ **New never-quote: any `tol`-metric number at m > 1** — a **blank landscape scores `strict = 1.0000`** there (decode blank: 0.0312 = 1/32, exactly chance) (**N110**).
> - ⚠ **SCOPE, and it is part of the claim: d = 4 ONLY. d = 6 and d = 8 were NOT RUN, no exponent is re-measured, and `K_designed = 4·2^d` is untouched** (128 = 4·2⁴, measured at m = 1, 2 and 4). ⛔ **Never "R2 is unclamped" without it.** ⛔ **And w21's ~1.3-vs-2 bits-per-param is STALE** (measured under this ceiling) — items-per-parameter at the same cell rises ×2.00 (annealed read, zero extra params) to ×5.60, but **nobody has re-derived bits-per-param.**
> - **One lever question closed:** the init × width 2×2 factorial says the two levers are **substitutes** — `atom_init_local` is **+0.0068 ± 0.016 (a null) at the shipped width** and +0.4658 at width 0.15 (**interaction +0.4590, 14.3 SE**) ⇒ **no default changed**, and ⛔ **N98's +0.051 does not cross-replicate on this harness** (**N111**). Full picture: **§11.16 Record 6.**

> **⭐ Update (wave-24) — THE R2 BOUNDARY NOTE. Read this before scoping any capacity experiment or drawing any capacity figure.**
> The w24 Head ruling demotes **masked/static recall** to appendix-only, permanently (§11.11, §11.7 update (1); roadmap v0.7). ⚠ **That demotion does NOT apply to the capacity law in this section.** **R2 — "K = 2^d addressable items" — is a LAW ABOUT THE PRIMITIVE** (how many items are addressable at O(1) read cost), **not a competitive claim against nearest-neighbour**, and is therefore **exempt from the demotion** (Hub-confirmed with the Head, 2026-07-24).
> ⛔ **But its figure must NEVER be framed as beating anything.** The log-linear capacity plot is a *scaling law of the store*, with the designed line and the learned line and the write ceiling on it — not a head-to-head. Any capacity sentence still carries the standing denominators: **capacity-per-parameter is a comparison we lose** (≈1.1–1.6 bits/param vs 2 — **N77**) and **exponential capacity is the modern-Hopfield field's own headline** (our novelty is the *measured constant and mechanism*, **N70/N77** discipline above). ⇒ scope a capacity task as *"how far does the addressable-item law extend, and what breaks it"*, never as *"CLU beats X at recall."*

## 11.7 What a new agent must not say — the positioning guardrails **[external prior art]**

Four claims that a reader of this Part might reasonably form are **retired**, three of them by published work a reviewer will know (full detail: `negative_results.md` **N65**, `claims_matrix.md` **CM-21**):

1. ⛔ **"Symplectic conservation gives a retention guarantee attention has no analogue for."** **HiPPO-LegS has one** — error $O(tL/\sqrt N)$, gradient $\Theta(1/t)$ (polynomial, not exponential). ⚠ **And it is worse than a tie: HiPPO-LegS is *exactly* timescale-equivariant and has NO $\Delta t$ at all** — *a reviewer will ask why a learned mass spectrum beats provable timescale invariance, and we have no answer.* (Contrast Part II, where $M$ is our timescale mechanism, and §11.5, where it has been measurably inert.)
2. ⛔ **"Retrieval is a rollout, not a weighted sum" as OUR novelty.** Published: **Kong, Brewer & Lai, *Nature Communications* 15 (2024)** — location-addressable retrieval of dynamical attractors via an index channel, $N_c \propto K^{1.08 \pm 0.01}$. Ours differs (symplectic, Hamiltonian, mass-structured, address *is* the initial condition rather than an injected bias) — **but the move is theirs and must be cited.** ⭐ Their failure analysis is a free gift: **basin-boundary violation on hand-off** is the predicted failure mode for our multi-particle consolidation and wormhole hand-offs, and it is directly testable.
3. ⛔ **"Continuity is how we escape the NTM/DNC precedent."** **False** — canonical NTM *and* DNC were already fully soft, continuous and end-to-end differentiable (RL-NTM is a separate, later line). **The precedent transfers as a WARNING**: 2 of 3 diagnosed DNC failure modes bite CLU's address scheme, and ⚠ **the one ranked "most importantly" — key/value entanglement — is ARCHITECTURAL here, because $q_0$ is simultaneously the key and the initial condition of the value.** An explicit key/value split (an address embedding distinct from the launch state) is an open first-order design question.
4. ⛔ **"A competitor to the transformer."** Indefensible at 1–1.6 bits/register with no win over a trivial baseline. **Symplectic conservation prevents *decay*, not *capacity*** — and capacity is precisely the axis on which a fixed-size latent is provably weak (Jelassi et al., ICML 2024). ⚠ **Capacity is the program's most exposed flank; frame CLU's contribution as retention control and temporal structure, explicitly NOT capacity, and say so before a reviewer asks.**

✅ **What survives, and should be cited prominently:** **Ramsauer et al. — the modern Hopfield update *is* the attention mechanism**, which makes *"energy-landscape retrieval competes with attention"* a principled claim rather than an analogy. ✅ And the approved framing: *a physics-structured associative memory in which retention is a **designed, per-item property** — permanent (symmetry-protected), finite half-life ($\mu^2$-set), or actively deletable — rather than an emergent consequence of a learned recurrence.* **That claims controllability, which we can evidence, instead of capability, which we cannot.**

> **⛔ Update (wave-21/22) — the guardrail list GREW. Add to the do-not-say list:** the **19×/17×** capacity gap (an un-rescued-GRU artifact; the honest margin is **2.1×**, N78) · **"CLU extrapolates"** outside the autonomous/designed setting (falsified in the trained slot, N79) · **"symplectic ⇒ long-horizon stability"** without citing **UnICORNN** (occupied, with a `(NΔt)³` theorem we lack) · **MQAR as a fair fight** (Arora et al. ICML 2024 Thm 3.1: Ω(N) bits for any fixed-state recurrence — cite the theorem once, move on) · **mass as a storage channel** (it SELECTS; 3× null, §11.10/N76) · **"retrieval cost independent of stored items"** (false for the shipped code; occupied at 128B params where true) · **"we beat SOTA on associative memory"** (a trivial NN pixel floor beats everyone on the standard masked-image protocol, and CIFAR admissibility requires a learned kernel — N81) · a **fiber bit-count without `(σ_rel, N, launches)`** · **"CLU degrades more gracefully than the GRU"** outside contextual MQAR (it is the WORST of four under parametric sequential writes, N75). **New must-cite:** **Titans (NeurIPS 2025)** — test-time gradient descent with momentum on an associative-memory loss = discretized damped second-order dynamics; the nearest published neighbour to CLU's write mechanism.

> **⛔ Update (wave-23) — five more entries for the do-not-say list, and one defence that is now CLOSED.**
> 1. ⛔ **"A designed CLU store beats a trivial feature baseline on associative recall."** **FALSIFIED with a real learned `φ`**: the laundering control (same `φ`, trivial store = kNN-in-φ) **fires on all four (dataset × arm) cells, max CLU margin 0.000** (**N89**). ⭐ **And this closes the standing defence** *"CLU only loses because there is no embedding"* — the embedding now exists in two independent, fair forms and the store still does not win the static benchmark. **Approved replacement wording:** *"a learned read-in makes the CLU register competitive with closed-form modern-Hopfield in feature space and far above its own raw-pixel line, but a trivial kNN in the same feature space is the ceiling on static masked recall."*
> 2. ⛔ **"CLU beats feedforward memories via test-time compute."** The feedforward-NN floor is **flat at 0.99–1.00 and above CLU-gated in every cell (−3.5…−42.2 pp)**. **Say instead:** *"CLU draws a rising, auto-stopping accuracy-vs-compute curve that a saturated feedforward memory structurally cannot draw — while remaining below that floor on this protocol"* (**N90**; CM-23 splits into a surviving *shape* claim and a retracted *dominance* claim).
> 3. ⛔ **Base √2 (1.44) — or `d^1.62` — as the learned capacity exponent** (**N92**, §11.6 update). Quote the two-regime law.
> 4. ⛔ **A single-number controller-rematch verdict.** Per-admitted (**1.000**, best of five) and per-offered (**0.081** fixed / **0.669** sized) are different metrics and **both must travel**; quoting per-admitted alone claims a perfect memory that holds 8% of what it was offered (**N91**).
> 5. ⛔ **Any diagnostic from a `<40`-epoch CAFE fit presented as a property of the shipped model** — including the retired **98.3% ballistic** (trained: ≈77% legacy / ≈50% shipped `dt`) and the retired **0.0000 single-basin collapse** (a ~10-epoch transient) (**N94**). ⛔ Also retired: the w19 audit's **0.4545 persistence "win"** — an L-2 stale-launch artifact; the quotable forecasting number is **0.7705 vs 0.5669, and CLU loses** (**N86 upheld, R19-1 resolved**).
> ✅ **New mandatory control (not a forbidden claim — an instrument):** **every claim made in a learned feature space carries a laundering control** — the same `φ` with the store replaced by the most trivial competent reader (kNN-in-φ). Twice-instantiated now (w18's `q_last`, w23's kNN-in-φ), and both times it fired.
> ✅ **New must-cites from the CL recon:** **SQHN** (Nature Communications 2024, doi:10.1038/s41467-024-46976-4) — *energy-based Hopfield-class store, online-continual, replay-free via parameter isolation*: the nearest published neighbour to CLU-as-a-CL-memory, **cite and differentiate** (continuous designed landscape · fiber payload channel · per-item decay/eviction schedules · relaxation + retry read · learned `φ`); **iCaRL** (the fair-slot precedent); **GDumb** (the pathology check any bounded-memory claim must beat); **Energy-Based Transformers** (arXiv:2507.02092) as the framing-level incumbent for energy-as-verifier test-time compute.

## 11.8 The architectural gap — and the honest summary of where the program stands

⭐ **The reframing that reorganizes everything in this primer (Head, 2026-07-21).** **CLU is a LATENT INFORMATION CARRIER, not a model of the observed system's dynamics.** Symplectic conservation says *latent information is not lost over long horizons* — a **memory** property — **not** that the observed process is conservative. A conservative latent flow carries "this engine degrades at rate X" perfectly well. ⛔ **The Hub's "primitive–task mismatch" hypothesis (*"a symplectic integrator asked to model a ramp wants to orbit"*) is REJECTED and WITHDRAWN — never diagnose "prior mismatch" again.**

⇒ **The failure is in how we WRITE task information into the latent and READ it back out.** Today: read-in $\varphi:\text{data}\to(q_0,p_0)$ is **≈ the IDENTITY** ($q_0$ = raw window, $p_0$ = finite difference); read-out $\psi$ is **HANDCRAFTED** (energy, $\|\nabla V\|$, relaxation residual, $q^*$); so **all representational burden falls on $V_\theta$ alone.** *We have been running the equivalent of attention on raw pixels.* **This single gap predicts most of the program's negatives** — physics scalars at chance ($\psi$ is handcrafted, so there is no reason energy tracks wear), best config = *least* dynamics, and the rollout-below-persistence result (with identity $\varphi$ we tested CLU as a **raw-sensor forecaster**).

⚠ **And the honest correction to that hope: moving to representation space made CLU look WORSE, not better** — feature-space persistence loses at every horizon by 20–56×, on 30/30 features at n=1, because $\psi$ is quadratic in a state that free-streams away. **That is an argument for the design task, not evidence for it.**

⭐ **The missing piece is the CONTROLLER.** Every verb in the architecture — *decide, triage, add, create, trash, select, combine* — is a controller verb, **and none of them is built.** The program has been building the components of one architecture without naming it: the latch (permanent mode), the pseudo-Goldstone (half-life), SSB vacua (symmetry-broken point sets), the governed rollout (stable trajectory), the $\gamma_\varphi$ field (trash — ⚠ **but see the vault theorem: friction *preserves*, so deletion must be re-specified as "overwrite or heat, never friction"**), $M$ as budget allocator, the boost ladder (retry), wormholes, `CLULattice` (multiple particles).

**So the honest one-paragraph summary a new agent should carry:** *the physics of the memory works and is guaranteed — writing, reading, durability, locality and hard read isolation are all demonstrated, under design. What does not work is **indexing** it (gradient address search is dead, N61), **feeding** it (φ is the identity), **reading** it into task space (ψ is handcrafted), and **controlling** it (unbuilt). The negatives are concentrated in the I/O and the controller, not in the primitive.* ⚠ **And the standing tension, which must not be smoothed away:** "start arbitrary and restructure" is in direct conflict with N46/N7/N64/CM-5/CM-16a and with D1/D3 — *this program's strongest negatives say structure does NOT self-organize.* The working resolution (loss-driven restructuring **plus** designed structural scaffolding, since **T3 proves a regularizer can restore diversity but can never choose assignment**) is a **proposal under test, not a settled result.**

**Reading pointers.** `clu-memory-architecture` (the formal object, six propositions, three-regime capacity, the 10-stage feasibility table, the named falsifier — *the derived-address retrieval gate*, pre-registered ≥80% at K=4); `clu-retrieval-demo` (the working loop + the γ-scan); `scout-dynamical-memory-priorart` (prior art and the four retired claims); `negative_results.md` N61–N65 + W2 + §Open reconciliations; `claims_matrix.md` CM-18/19/20/21.

> **⭐ Update (wave-22): the Head reset the scoring, and asked for the plain-language version — both are now canonical onboarding material.**
> **(i) Performance is the deliverable** (verbatim: *"if the property theoretically fails but we still get good task-performance that's still a massive win… Nobody knows why transformers work"*). **The score = external benchmarks won on their own headline metric. Currently ZERO.** Filing rule (binding): property-demo without performance → workshop shorts; performance without explanation → ICLR; performance WITH the explaining law → ICLR/NMI. Do not discard property demos — file them by the performance behind them. Do NOT pre-register the retreat.
> **(ii) The hilly-landscape grounding (use this before any equation with a newcomer):** *CLU = a hilly landscape with a ball. Memory = the shape of the hills; each stored item is a valley. To recall: drop the ball near where the answer should be, let it roll and settle, read where it stopped.* Write = shape a valley at a chosen spot (where · how deep/narrow · permanent-pit vs leaky-valley · the ball's mass). Read = query → start position, mass, friction, resting spot, plus shove-and-retry. ⚠ Precision: **a frozen surface does not decay on its own** — "leaky-valley" decay is either a within-rollout fade or an active edit of well depth.
> **⭐⭐ Update (wave-23) — the honest one-paragraph position, revised: `φ` exists now, and the diagnosis it was supposed to fix did not follow.**
> **(a) The gap above is half-closed and the hoped-for consequence is falsified.** A real learned read-in `φ` was built in two independently-fair arms (frozen PCA-32 and a separately-trained autoencoder, both fit on a **disjoint** pool and **never** through the store). It buys an enormous lift — **raw-space CLU sits at CIFAR chance (0.012) and CLU-in-φ reaches 0.81–0.97**; it rescues closed-form Hopfield from raw chance too; and **CLU-in-φ ≫ closed-form Hopfield-in-φ** at load and noise (CIFAR M=256: **0.973 vs 0.008**). ⛔ **But the designed store still never beats a trivial kNN over the same features** (**N89**). ⇒ *the φ gap was real and worth closing, and it was **not** the explanation for the program's benchmark losses.*
