> **(b) The recurring shape across three independent w23 tasks — record this as the program's position, not as three anecdotes.** In φ-space, in the retry curve, and in the controller rematch alike, **a trivial or strong baseline owns the absolute headline number while CLU owns a mechanism or capability axis that baseline structurally lacks**: a *directed* re-launch no equal-energy kick or k-restart ensemble reproduces (**N90**); admission-with-abstention plus per-item permanence and decay in one store (**N91**); a designed geometric capacity that doubles per dimension up to a write ceiling (**N92**). ⇒ **the contribution is currently mechanistic and capability-shaped, and the open question is which benchmark can express it** — which is exactly what the continual-learning recon independently concluded (*"best replay-free method + per-item control + a retry curve," never "beats replay"*).
> **(c) Two of the program's striking numbers were properties of a barely-trained model.** The w23 reconciliation pass dissolved all three open measurement conflicts into **launch-convention and training-maturity differences — no physics disagreement, no bug**: the persistence sign is carried entirely by the **rollout launch frame** (L-2 vs L-1; γ exactly neutral at n=1) ⇒ **CLU does not beat persistence, N86 upheld and unblocked**; **98.3% ballistic** was a 40-epoch fit (trained: ≈77% legacy / ≈50% shipped `dt`); the **0.0000 single-basin collapse** was a ~10-epoch transient (spread 9.15e-08 at ep10 → 0.31–0.67 at ep150, 100% finite, f32≡f64) and the shipped encode path does not collapse (spread 0.456). ⇒ **standing caveat (N94): a diagnostic computed on a `<40`-epoch CAFE fit is not a property of the shipped model — state the epoch count of every diagnostic.** The only surviving structural observation is the milder one: *the trained `q*` cloud is **low-rank** (participation ratio ≈1 — a near-1-D settled ray), consistent with `q*` ≈ a smoother of the last observation along the degradation direction.*
> **(d) The controller is no longer an empty box** (§11.12), and the primitive's capacity ceiling now has an owner: **the write operator** (§11.6 update, §11.9 update).
>
> **(iii) Where the program stands after the performance wave (w22):** the primitive claim SURVIVED its sharpest test (the K=8 wall is geometry, §11.6); **one performance edge survives** (capacity under item load, 2.1× vs matched GRU/SSM — attention still wins); and **two capabilities have no Hopfield analogue and carry the most promise: RETRY (+46.9pp at ×1.5 compute with a −38.3pp blank guard — an accuracy-vs-compute curve a feedforward memory cannot draw; squarely in the test-time-compute area) and PER-ITEM RETENTION** (permanent + scheduled-decay items in one store). The φ/ψ I/O gap above remains the true bottleneck before any new benchmark.

## 11.9 The write operator: interference is governed by the WRITER, not the gate **[measured, three independent tasks — new wave-21/22]**

**Philosophy.** When a new memory is written by gradient descent on $V_\theta$, every parameter of a global-support potential moves — so writing item B disturbs item A no matter how far apart they sit. The program spent w20–w21 asking *what controls that disturbance*: the function class? an admission gate? dissipation? The answer is **none of these — it is the write operator itself**, i.e. *which parameters the write is allowed to touch*.

**Look-up.** Catastrophic forgetting; parameter isolation methods (masking); rehearsal.

**Math (the measured law).** Interference at a stored item $q^*$ is governed by the **C3 ratio** $\;\|\nabla\delta V(q^*)\| \,/\, \lambda_{\min}(\mathrm{Hess}\,V(q^*))$ — the write's force leak at the item over the well's stiffness — **evaluated at the relaxed fixed point, not the read's launch point**. Measured across 6 function classes and **4.6 decades**, predictive to a factor 1.6 for 5/6 arms; the rival statistic (support radius) falsified (Spearman 0.143). Two independent routes to safety: **shrink the numerator** with a masked/local write (**70×** at identical class/params/budget; drift follows $\exp(-d^2/2s^2)$, two implementations agreeing to 26%) or **inflate the denominator** with stiff wells (13–29×). **Rehearsal from the codebook (anchored write) gives 103× and is the only arm that both preserves AND stores** (strict-A 1.000, strict-B 0.975).

**What the GATE actually does (the Hub's w20 adjudication, corrected):** on the w20 learned geometry the spacing gate **cannot fire** (spacing 1.4142 > $d_{\rm safe}$ 1.10 ⇒ gated ≡ ungated to all digits), and a C3 gate preserves items **only by refusing every write** (strict-B 0.000). ⭐ On a **designed** store the gate IS decisive — item-1 retention **1.000 vs 0.16** at every K — at a measured capacity price (**6.0±0.9 of 16** proposals admitted ≈ the disk-packing bound 6.1). *Refusal is the mechanism; always quote the price with the retention.*

**→ CLU.** ⭐ **The masked-write advantage AMPLIFIES with address dimension: 197× (d=2) → 599× (d=3) → 6332× (d=4)** — a masked atom write is bit-local in parameter space at any $d$, while global-write interference does not fall with $d$. This is currently the program's most robust mechanistic result. ⚠ Context that travels: under sequential parametric writes with a *global* write, CLU is the **worst of four primitives** (K=64 retention 0.16 vs GRU 0.57), and the failure mode is **infeasibility** (the store-without-destroying criterion censors 100% between K=8 and K=32) — the write operator, not more compute, is the lever.

**Status: [measured — law across 4.6 decades; two implementations of the drift law agreeing to 26%].** Sources: `potential-function-class` §0/§3–4, `sequential-write-interference` §0–§3, `designed-mechanism-learned-content` recon 3. Registry: **N74, N75, N82**.

> **⭐⭐ Update (wave-23) — the write operator now owns a SECOND thing: the capacity ceiling.** The dimension-aware re-run isolates a **d-INDEPENDENT ceiling at `K ≈ 32`** on learned capacity, and eliminates the two rival explanations by measurement: it is **not geometry** (the designed mechanism reaches ≥256 in the same cells) and **not parameter count** (d=6 K=64 gets *worse* with twice the atoms — strict 0.855 → 0.809 at 36.9k → 73.7k params — and d=4 K=32 is flat at ~0.83 across a 16× atom sweep). What remains is the **static GLOBAL write's ability to dig many disjoint wells jointly.** ⇒ **learned capacity is capped by the WRITE OPERATOR, not the address geometry** (**N92**; §11.6 update).
> **Why this is a hopeful negative, and what to run next.** The same section of the program already knows the write operator's cure: **masked/local writes are bit-local in parameter space at any `d`** (this wave: **8474× (d=2), 3434× (d=4)**; w22: 197× → 599× → 6332×). **The single highest-value next experiment named by the engineer is therefore whether a masked / curriculum / sequential write breaks the ~32 ceiling** — if it does, capacity becomes `2^d` over a useful range and the designed/learned tax collapses; if it does not, the ceiling belongs to the write *objective*, which is a sharper negative and still publishable. **Untested** (`future_work.md`, w23 fold).
> **Also this wave:** the designed/learned tax **stops widening** (¼ → ⅛ → 1/16 at d=4, then plateaus/narrows to ~1/8 for d ≥ 5), and Prop F1 collects its **fourth** null (per-item mass Δstrict **+0.008 ± 0.011**, coupling 0.145 ± 0.085 — §11.10).

## 11.10 The fiber: what a launch can select, and what a landscape can store **[proven, verified — new wave-21]**

**Philosophy.** If the address is $(m, q_0, p_0)$ and the read is the trajectory, one is tempted to think mass and momentum are extra *storage* channels. They are not — and the reason is a one-line change of variables.

**Math (Prop F1, proven).** In the velocity variable $u = M^{-1}p$: $\dot q = u$, $\dot u = -M^{-1}\nabla V(q) - \Gamma u$. So **every read — any functional of $q(\cdot)$, any γ, any length — depends on $(M, V, p_0)$ only through $\big(G = M^{-1}\nabla V,\; u_0 = M^{-1}p_0\big)$** (verified to 6.2e-16). Consequences:
- **The V↔M degeneracy is an exact gauge for ANY $V$** — anharmonicity does not break it (a rescale of $V$ is also a time-reparameterization at rest launch; verified to 1e-15). What breaks it: **landscape coupling** ($\partial_i\partial_j V \neq 0$ — mass ratios are worth **8.6 bits coupled vs 0.20 separable**), a momentum-carrying launch, or the **relativistic kinetic term** — ⭐ *the governor is what gauge-fixes $M$ against $V$, making inertia an observable* (breaking $\propto(v/c)^2$, slope 1.989).
- **$M$ and $p_0$ are ADDRESS-SIDE: they SELECT, they cannot STORE.** A quantity the reader supplies at launch cannot deliver stored bits. **The fiber's only storing channel is the local jet of $G$.** γ is a spatial field (0 per-item bits); temperature is the noise floor.
- **Composition = slots × bits:** $B_{\rm total} = K_{\rm spatial} \cdot B_{\rm fiber}$ — never items × items (a location has one landscape). And the **parameter ceiling** $B_{\rm total} \le P\,b_\theta$: the shipped `PotentialMLP(d=8)` has 1377 params against ≥36 864 reals needed for 1024 wells with per-well Hessians — **the measured designed capacity and per-well fibers are jointly unreachable by the shipped learned MLP.**
- The fiber is nonetheless large (**129→174 bits/well** at d=2, N=1200, σ_rel=1e-3) but **quote it only dressed with $(\sigma_{\rm rel}, N, \text{launches})$** — the same jet reads 52/87/129 bits across three noise floors — and remember **read length is exponential in bits**: buy capacity with wells/modes/launches, never longer rollouts.

**→ CLU.** This is the corrected home of "mass as an access key" (§11.3 Prop 1, §11.5 Prop 6): a functional mass spectrum ($\tau \propto M^{0.79}$, w20) is a **selection** mechanism — which barriers are crossable, which timescale is read — never a payload. **Third confirmation (w22, pre-registered):** in an address-separable atom landscape (coupling 0.081 < 0.1), per-item mass changes strict retrieval by **−0.005 ± 0.007** — a null that *confirms* the theory.

**Status: [proven, verified to 1e-15/6.2e-16] (structure, both kinetic modes); [measured, d≤2 toys] (rates).** Source: `relaxation-fiber-capacity` §1–§4; `designed-mechanism-learned-content` §2. Registry: **N76**. ⚠ The `clu-autoencoder` task file predates this correction and must be edited before it runs.

## 11.11 The read-in `φ`: what a learned embedding buys, and the laundering control that decides who earned it **[measured, two independent φ arms — new wave-23]**

**Philosophy.** A memory that stores *raw* observations is a memory whose geometry is dictated by the sensor, not by the task. Every physics analogue of a store — a lattice of wells, a landscape of basins — presupposes that "nearby" means *semantically* nearby. The read-in map $\varphi:\text{data}\to(q_0,p_0)$ is what makes that presupposition true. Until wave-23 the program's $\varphi$ was **the identity** (§11.8), which is why "CLU on raw pixels" was the standing diagnosis for most of its benchmark losses.

**Look-up.** Feature maps / kernels; representation learning; the embedding layer that every attention stack has and that CLU did not.

**Math (the object, and the fairness condition that makes it evidence).** Store item $i$ as a Gaussian well centred at $\varphi(\xi_i)$ with width $s$; read a query $x$ by launching at $\varphi(x)$ and settling under damped Verlet; return the payload of the nearest well. **The fairness condition is the whole experiment: $\varphi$ must be fit on a DISJOINT pool with an objective that never sees the store, the wells, or the retrieval loss.** Both w23 arms satisfy it by construction — a frozen PCA-32 (SVD of a disjoint pool) and a 1-hidden-layer autoencoder trained on **reconstruction MSE only** (recon-MSE 0.0170 MNIST / 0.0234 CIFAR). This is the doctrine "**learn around a designed core**": fixed physics + designed wells, learned interface, exactly as attention fixes $\mathrm{softmax}(QK^\top)V$ and learns the projections.

**The measurement, and both halves of it (`phi-read-in`, seed 0, $d_\varphi=32$, $s = 0.3\cdot$median-NN, $M\in\{16,\dots,256\}$, 50%-masked queries):**
- ⭐ **The premise is validated, and the lift is large.** Raw-space CLU on CIFAR is at **chance (0.012 at M=256)**; **CLU-in-φ reaches 0.81–0.97**. Closed-form Hopfield-in-φ also recovers from raw-pixel chance. And **CLU-in-φ ≫ closed-form Hopfield-in-φ** at load and under noise (**CIFAR M=256: 0.973 vs 0.008** for the AE arm; MNIST σ=0.4: 0.852 vs 0.250) — *settling into a basin denoises; one softmax step does not.* ⚠ **Scope it:** on MNIST at high $M$ Hopfield is slightly ahead (0.957 vs 0.871) — the margin is a **CIFAR / hard-feature-geometry** effect, not universal; and **AE-softmax Hopfield re-collapses at high $M$** (0.008), i.e. a learned manifold can re-concentrate inner products the way raw CIFAR did. *φ fixes Hopfield for **localized** reads (CLU, sparsemax), not universally for softmax.*
- ⛔ **And the store does not earn it.** The **laundering control** — same $\varphi$, trivial store (kNN-in-φ) — **fires on all four (dataset × arm) cells: CLU wins 0 of 5 loads in every cell, max CLU margin 0.000.** kNN-in-φ is 1.000 down to $M=128$ and dominates the noise axis too. *"The win is φ's, not ours."* (**N89**.)
- **Why, mechanically:** kNN-in-φ is already a perfect static content-addressable read of the same features, so the settling dynamics can at best tie it — and the fixed rule $s=0.3\cdot$median-NN places the store **exactly at the packing bound** ($\text{spacing}/\Delta_{\rm req} \equiv 1.08$ by construction, since $1/(3.1\times0.3)=1.075$), so as $M$ grows and median-NN shrinks the basins begin to overlap and CLU decays *first*. **There is no slack; a larger `s_frac` would trade capacity for noise, exactly as the $\Delta_{\rm req}$ analysis predicts.**
- ⭐ **What did transfer: the retry trigger.** Confidence = distance-to-nearest-well at settle separates correct from incorrect first-pass reads **in φ-space at AUROC 0.845–0.988** (pre-registered ≥0.65; incorrect reads settle **1.6–8.7×** farther) ⇒ the feature-space extension of §11.13 is **GREEN and unattempted**.

**→ CLU, said plainly.** *A learned read-in makes the CLU register competitive with closed-form modern-Hopfield in feature space and far above its own raw-pixel line — but a trivial nearest-neighbour read of the same features is the ceiling on static masked recall.* **The designed store's value is a capability (retention, decay, admission, retry, sequential-write locality), and a static recall benchmark structurally cannot score it.** ⇒ the redirect to a capability benchmark (continual learning) is not a retreat; it is where the claim is expressible.

**Nomenclature (binding).** **Laundering control** = *the same learned map with the store replaced by the most trivial competent reader*; if the trivial reader ties or wins, the credit belongs to the map. It is now **mandatory on every claim made in a learned space** (fired twice: w18 `q_last` on CAFE, w23 kNN-in-φ). **φ-arm fairness** = fit on a disjoint pool, objective blind to the store.

**Status: [measured, single seed, one φ family per arm, $d_\varphi=32$; the laundering verdict is unambiguous (margin 0.000 everywhere), the accuracies are unswept].** Source: `phi-read-in` §1–§5 + PREREG scorecard. Registry: **N89**; guardrail in §11.7 update; `future_work.md` (φ-space retry, `s_frac` Pareto, the `ψ` half still open).

> **⭐ Update (wave-26) — A φ THAT ACTUALLY WORKS ON CIFAR EXISTS, AND IT RAISES THE LAUNDER BY CONSTRUCTION (`cl-encoder`; N121/N122).** A **task-1-only SSL conv trunk** (`simclr`: 3 conv blocks + GroupNorm → 2×2 spatial map → PCA head to `phi_dim = 256`, L2-normalised cosine addresses; fit unsupervised on ≈4 966 task-1-class images **disjoint from every stream item**, frozen, never trained through the store) moves the Split-CIFAR-10 **kNN-in-φ** ceiling **0.219 ± 0.017 → 0.339 ± 0.015 at 8 000 fit steps → 0.357 ± 0.019 at 20 000** (3 seeds each). **What bought it, isolated:** conv architecture with **no data at all** +0.035 · **reconstruction** fitting **−0.006 (bought nothing — `convae` never beat the untrained trunk)** · **contrastive** fitting +0.041 · more steps and data +0.065 ⇒ *the objective and the fit compute are the two levers, and the second has not saturated* (NT-Xent 5.51 → **3.98** against chance 5.541 and a converged ~2.5–3).
> - ⚠ **The trap this section names is now quantified: the gate metric IS the laundering control**, so this work **raised the launder** and **nothing in it is a store result** (no settle was run at the new φ). ⛔ Any wording that reads *"the encoder improved CLU"* is wrong.
> - ⭐ **The strict-φ cost finally bites, and it is decision-changing.** `generic_frozen − task1_only`, fit-pool-matched: **+0.003 at PCA** (both arms broken) vs **+0.020 ± 0.010 at the SSL arm** (3/3 seeds positive) — and *exactly across the decision boundary*: the leaky arm **clears** the 0.35 gate (0.358) and the defensible arm **misses** it (0.339). ⇒ `PREREG_CL_PHI` §7's *"strictness costs essentially nothing"* is **MNIST/PCA-specific.**
> - ⛔ **Never "the gate was cleared" without the fit budget** (8 k = MISS; 20 k = CLEAR by **+0.007**, less than the ±0.019 seed spread). ⛔ **"24.5× fewer floats" is MNIST-specific** — an address here is **256 floats vs a raw exemplar's 3 072** ⇒ **12×** (24× at `phi_dim = 128`, which costs only 0.004 of the gate).
> - ⭐ **And this section's crowding mechanism is CONFIRMED at the new arm, not fixed by it:** median-NN 0.975, `s` 0.192, **σ_q 0.946 ≈ median-NN**, corrected packing slack **0.332** — indistinguishable from the PCA arm's 0.337–0.345. **A better φ does not de-crowd the store**, because crowding is intrinsic to a *classification* stream (the query is a different image, not a corrupted copy). ⛔ Never the retracted 1.08.

> **⛔⛔ Update (C2W8 pass 1, 2026-08-06) — ONE CLAUSE OF THE BLOCK ABOVE IS A READ-OUT ARTIFACT, AND THE STORE HAS NOW ACTUALLY BEEN RUN AT THIS φ.** The block above reports the arm isolation as *"reconstruction fitting **−0.006 (bought nothing — `convae` never beat the untrained trunk)**"*. ⛔ **That comparison was measured at `cl-encoder`'s pre-sweep read-out defaults — the corner its own read-out sweep then measured as the WORST of 28** (whitened PCA · no L2 · `d = 256`: 0.2676, against the chosen `keep`-spatial · plain PCA · L2 · `d = 256`: 0.3496). At the **measured** read-out, `convae` reads **CLU 0.267 ± 0.006** and kNN-ring **0.277 ± 0.003** against `randconv`'s **0.213 ± 0.006** / 0.235 ± 0.005 ⇒ ⭐ **+0.054, and it CLEARS the +0.10 lift threshold that had been pre-registered as a MISS.** ⛔⛔ **NEVER-QUOTE *"reconstruction bought nothing"* — and ⛔ do NOT invert it to *"reconstruction helps"*: ✅ the only approved form is *the SIGN of the objective comparison depends on the read-out, and the banked table's read-out was not the measured one.*** ⇒ ⛔ **`cl-encoder` §3's objective/architecture decomposition is not safe to quote at all**, and a defensible *"the objective matters"* sentence requires re-running that isolation table at the measured read-out (it does not exist). ⭐ **What is NEW and does not amend anything above: the CLU settle was finally run at this φ** — `simclr` **CLU 0.319 ± 0.005** (5 seeds), **+0.1588 ± 0.0123 paired** over the PCA row, ⛔ **and still −0.014 ± 0.003 below its own kNN launder (0/5 seeds) — with the settle equal to its own SAME-KEYS kNN to +0.0002 ± 0.0004.** ⇒ ⭐⭐ **the section's own warning is now measured rather than argued: the encoder improved the ADDRESS SPACE and improved the launder by the same amount.** ⚠ **The strict-φ cost above (+0.020 ± 0.010) is UNAMENDED — `generic_frozen` is a declared NOT-RUN at C2W8.** **Registry N258 / N255 / N256; Record 30 below; ledger ⟲ C2W8 pass-1.**

## 11.12 The controller (MVC-0): admission, placement, eviction, decay — and why one number cannot score it **[built + measured, 5 seeds — new wave-23]**

**Philosophy.** A landscape with wells is a *store*; what makes it a *memory system* is the policy that decides **what to write, where, at what depth, and what to forget**. §11.8 called this the architecture's single largest hole. Wave-23 built the minimal version — **hand-coded, no learning anywhere in the loop** — so that the policy's contribution is separable from any training effect.

**Look-up.** Cache admission/eviction policies (LRU); collision resolution in hash tables; garbage collection; the "trash region" of Part VII, now with a scheduler.

**The four verbs, as implemented (`chlu/core/controller.py`, on a designed `AtomStorePotential`).**

| verb | rule | physical content |
|---|---|---|
| **admission** | admit iff $\min_j d(q_{\rm new}, q_j) \ge d_{\rm safe} = 4.4\,s$, else **refuse-and-relocate** (400 candidate draws) | a *certificate of isolation*: the write is allowed only where Prop 2's protection is available |
| **placement** | the address **is** the admitted/relocated site; the writer records where it wrote | routes around the dead address gradient (Prop 5 / **N61**) — nothing is searched |
| **eviction** | when full: LRU (staleness) or shallowest (depth), **permanent never evicted**; an all-permanent full store raises a **capacity alarm** | a bounded store that refuses rather than silently overwrites |
| **decay** | `tick()`: leaky wells $a \mapsto a\,e^{-\lambda}$, self-evict below floor; permanent $\Leftrightarrow \lambda = 0$ | per-item retention as **amplitude decay** — permanent and forgettable content coexisting in ONE landscape |

**Measurement — and the reason the verdict is two-part (`controller-mvp`, 5 paired seeds, $d=3$, $s=0.35$, $d_{\rm safe}=1.54$).**
- ⭐ **The gate DEMONSTRABLY FIRES here** — live-site min-spacing **1.61 ≥ 1.54**, intervention rate **0.20 → 0.97** across $K$ — in direct contrast to **N74**, where on the w20 ring the same gate was arithmetically vacuous. *Geometry chosen so the decision is decidable is a prerequisite for measuring a policy at all.*
- **Controller OFF** reproduces w21 exactly and then dies: $1.000 \to \mathbf{0.110}$ (K=16, = designed-ungated) $\to 0.009 \to \mathbf{0.000}$ (K=64).
- ⭐ **Controller ON, retention-per-ADMITTED = 1.000 at every $K$ to 64 — best of five** (vs gru 0.57 · mlp 0.43 · attn 0.34 · learned-CLU 0.16). *The gate never stores an item it will corrupt.*
- ⛔ **Controller ON, retention-per-OFFERED is geometry-conditional:** on a **fixed** radius-2 disk it is $5.2/K$ — **0.081 at K=64, LAST of seven**, because the packing bound is $N_{\rm pack} = \pi R^2/(\tfrac{\sqrt3}{2}d_{\rm safe}^2) = 6.12$ and the controller refuses everything past it. On a **sized** disk ($R = 0.808\sqrt K$, so $N_{\rm pack} \ge K$) it is **0.669 — beating all four primitives** (**N91**).
- **Costs and limits, stated before a referee does:** the gate itself is **0.006 ms**; **refuse-and-relocate is 3.1 ms at $n_{\rm stored}=64 \approx 4\times$ one 0.70 ms read** (≪ a gradient write, ≠ free vs a read). Sized geometry stops at 0.669 because *random* disk proposals achieve **42.8/64** packing — the `d_eff < d` shell-concentration effect, a **packing-efficiency** limit, not a controller limit (**N93**). And the decay demo confirms the physical retention law exactly: leaky amplitude $e^{-0.35t}$ (measured 0.705 = $e^{-0.35}$), half-life 1.98, `decayed_out = 6/6`, permanent item retention 1.000 throughout.

**→ CLU, said plainly.** **The address space, not the controller, is the binding constraint.** Two numbers therefore travel together, always: *per-admitted measures the purity of what the system chose to keep; per-offered charges every refusal as a miss.* Quoting the first alone claims "a perfect memory" — true only of the 8% it agreed to hold. **The design rule this licenses for any bounded-store benchmark: either size the address space to the load ($N_{\rm pack} \ge K$), or accept abstention and report per-admitted *with the admitted fraction*.**

**What the controller CANNOT fix (from the spec, restated):** it cannot manufacture a $\tau=\infty$ item on an unconstrained *learned* $V$ (permanence here is the **designed** $\lambda=0$ coset, not a training outcome); it cannot beat the packing bound; and its spacing certificate is **meaningless for a global-support learned write** (**N75**) — the controller-ON arm is designed-store-only by construction.

> **⚠ Update (wave-25) — one number in the decay demo above is a BEST CASE, and the controller has now run on a real stream.** **(a) The correction (`mia-decay-measurement` §6 / defect D2).** The decay-demo line *"leaky item retention 1.00 through 8 ticks"* was produced by an instrument with two blind spots: `item_decay_demo.retention_of` passes a **1-element `sites` array** to `evaluate_items`, so `basin_of`'s `argmin` is always 0 and **the basin criterion is vacuous** (only the value test runs), and its `leaky_id = 1` happens to carry payload **+0.1429**, the codebook's **most decay-robust** value. Re-measured with the full live-site basin test over all 8 codebook values at the same amplitude ($A = 0.0608$): **retention = 0.886 ± 0.19, and 0.500 ± 0.11 for the item whose payload is $+1$** (that same item-1 still scores 0.999, agreeing with the original). ✅ **The permanent-item row is unaffected — 1.000 at every tick — and the amplitude law is unaffected** ($e^{-0.35t}$, 0.7047 at $t=1$, self-eviction at tick 9, reproduced independently). ⛔ **Corrected wording: *"a permanent item retains 1.000 while leaky wells decay and self-evict; a decayed leaky item's retention is payload-dependent (0.886 mean, 0.500 at $a=+1$)"* — never *"decayed items answer perfectly right up to the floor."*** The **cause** of the payload dependence is a design property, not a bug in the demo: the $q_2(0)=0$ guard makes each site a payload **hill** of height $\tfrac12\kappa a_i^2$ (up to 0.5) competing with an address well of depth $A$ (0.05 at the floor) ⇒ **two items written with the same `leak` do not have the same effective lifetime** ($r = -0.846$ with $a_i^2$); a **theorist ruling** is owed before the potential is changed (defect **D3**; **N108**). **(b) The controller on a live 10 000-offer stream (`cl-entry-build`, Split-MNIST, 3 seeds).** Admitted fractions per task **0.57–0.95**, `n_live` saturating at the 200-item budget **from task 0 on** ⇒ **budget-bound, not gate-bound**; setting `d_safe_mult · clu_s_frac = 0.88 ≤ 1` makes the spacing gate self-consistent (it refuses 5–45 % per task instead of refusing everything — exactly the N91 failure mode this section warns about). ⭐ **The two causes of forgetting are separated and never conflated:** in a 200-item store fed 10 000 offers, **budget pressure removes 6 492 items and the schedule removes 71 (≈91×)** ⇒ the honest framing is *"the schedule is a per-item control operating inside a capacity-bound store"*, **not** *"the store forgets on schedule."* And the scheduled law holds to numerical precision on the live stream (max abs error **6.8e-8** per item, permanent items at retention **1.000** through all five tasks) at a measured price of **≈0.01 ACC**.

**Status: [built + measured, 5 seeds, one designed-store family, Newtonian, $p_0=0$, scalar payload; the eviction verb is stressed only in the decay demo + unit tests, and the primitive baselines are reused from w21 rather than re-run].** ⚠ **w25: the decay-demo retention row is corrected (best case ⇒ 0.886 / 0.500), and the four verbs have now run inside an external benchmark stream.** Source: `controller-mvp` §0–§5; `mia-decay-measurement` §6; `cl-entry-build` Item 4. Registry: **N91 (w25 correction), N93, N108**; the unbuilt remainder (learned policy, learned landscape, combine/triage) is in `future_work.md`.

## 11.13 Test-time compute on a memory read: the curve, the gate, and the three controls that make it evidence **[measured, 8 cells, all controls — new wave-23]**

**Philosophy.** Part VI derived what a boost *costs*; the question a reviewer asks is what it *buys*, and — the harder half — **whether anything cheaper buys the same thing.** A method that improves when you spend more compute is only interesting if the improvement is attributable to *its* mechanism rather than to perturbation-in-general or to "try again $k$ times."

**Look-up.** Test-time compute / System-2 scaling; iterative refinement; anytime algorithms; Energy-Based Transformers (energy-as-verifier) as the framing-level incumbent.

**The construction.** First pass = one damped settle per query (**1 compute unit**). Then, per round, take the **lowest-confidence fraction** of queries (confidence = cosine to the nearest stored well), re-launch them with a **directed momentum boost** ($\times1.5$), and **lock** any query once retried. Ladder $k \in \{0,1,2,4,8\}$; gate threshold $\tau$ swept. Compute is measured in **total relaxation steps normalised by the first-pass budget**, so the gated arm's cost is *measured*, not assumed — and because the low-confidence tail is finite, the arm **auto-stops**.

**The result, and the controls that make it evidence (`retry-compute-study`, MNIST, seed 0, 8 cells = 2 loads × 2 degradation levels × {mask, noise}).**
- ⭐ **The curve is real and monotone:** flagship cell (mask, $M$=256, $p$=0.5) **0.359@1.0× → 0.461@1.1× → 0.562@1.2× → 0.738@1.4× → 0.961@1.64×**; across cells **+6.6 … +76.2 pp**, saturating at **×1.2–1.8**.
- ⭐⭐ **The mechanism survives every control.** **Equal-energy random-kick** (same energy, random direction) and **ensemble-of-$k$** ($k$ independent random-momentum restarts, best-confidence answer) are **DEAD FLAT in all 8 cells** (flagship: kick 0.359→0.348; ensemble 0.359 at every $k$, up to 9× compute). ⇒ **the lift is the *directed* symplectic re-launch — not stochastic restart, not "just $k$ tries."** Hopfield-$k$-steps *declines* with iteration.
- ⭐ **The confidence gate is load-bearing and self-limiting.** Ungated retry-all **collapses 0.96 → 0.004** at 9× compute (it corrupts already-correct reads — the w22 −38pp blank guard, now a continuous curve); the τ-sweep has a clean optimum (**τ=0.99: 0.961@1.64×** vs no-gate 0.828@**1.81×** — *lower accuracy at higher compute*).
- ⛔ **And it does not win the benchmark:** the **feedforward-NN floor is flat at ceiling 0.99–1.00 and above CLU-gated in every cell (−3.5 … −42.2 pp)** — on masked-pixel retrieval the surviving pixels already name the pattern (**N90**, the same floor as **N81**). ⇒ **CM-23 splits: the *shape* claim survives (a saturated feedforward memory cannot rise); the *absolute-dominance* claim is retracted.**
- ◐ **Protocol-dependence is a finding, not noise:** the boost recovers **structured erasure** (mask, +36…+76 pp) far better than **full-field Gaussian noise** (+6.6…+34.8 pp), and past σ≈0.4 every arm is at chance — *a query that has left every basin cannot be retried back into one.* Mechanistic lead worth a theory pass: with a mask the observed pixels still pull toward the true basin; with full-field noise they do not.
- **Design note (recorded so nobody re-derives it):** post-settle, cosine-to-nearest-well is a good **ranking** signal (correct 0.998 vs wrong 0.949) but a **useless acceptance** signal — a boost into the *right* well does not raise it above the wrong well it left. **Gate + lock-on-retry; never a per-item accept rule.**

**→ CLU, said plainly.** **A memory read with a knob: spend more relaxation steps only on the queries the system is unsure about, and accuracy rises until the uncertain tail is spent.** That is a capability a single-shot associative memory structurally lacks — and the three controls say it is *the physics*, not a heuristic restart. It is **not** a leaderboard result on this protocol. The created benchmark (**RUD-C** — *retrieval-under-degradation at a compute budget*, anchored verbatim to the U-Hop harness, with the four controls **mandatory for a valid submission**) is the vehicle that would make it one, and the honest scoring rule is written into the spec: *a method passes the mechanism bar only if it dominates the stochastic-restart and equal-energy controls; it wins only if it also dominates the feedforward floor.*

> **⭐ Update (wave-24) — the "no headroom" excuse is CLOSED, and the mechanism is confirmed at 5 seeds.** Two independent w24 results tighten this section. **(a) `headroom-retry-benchmark` (N95):** the leaderboard NO was re-tested *with headroom present* — two **ambiguity** regimes (block occlusion + contracted crowded store, 3 seeds, first-pass **0.26–0.93**, off ceiling) built precisely so the feedforward line is not saturated. CLU-gated retry **still** does not beat the **ML-optimal `feedforward_nn_masked` oracle** (=**1.000** in all 8 cells) — loses **−3.5…−38 pp** everywhere — so **the retraction cannot be blamed on saturation.** The lesson generalises: *on a designed store, identity retrieval under KNOWN erasure is information-theoretically trivial (the masked oracle is 1.000 regardless of ambiguity), so "ambiguity" only bites methods that ignore the mask.* RUD-C's mandatory baseline #1 is therefore the **masked observed-dims oracle**, not the naïve NN. **(b) `multi-seed-w23` (N90 confirmation):** the mechanism is decisive at 5 seeds — random-kick and ensemble violate the +3pp bar in **0 of 40** (seed,cell) pairs each; gated lift restated **+0…+79.7 pp**, saturation **mean ×1.53**. ⚠ **The NN gap moves a THIRD time — quote only the 5-seed range: mask −3.9…−20.7 pp, noise −9.7…−48.2 pp** (both prior wordings, incl. the seed-0 "−3.5…−42.2 pp", are too narrow). ⭐ **Nuance:** under geometric crowding the gated−kick peak margin shrinks to +2.3…+5.5 pp — the boost's *direction* buys less as the store gets more ambiguous.

> **⭐ Update (wave-25) — the untested route was taken, and the anytime read stopped losing: it TIES (`cl-entry-build` Item 3, N103).** The w24 lesson said the one place a leaderboard result could exist is *a query in a space the store is not metric-native to*. The CL entry supplies it for free: the corruption is applied in **pixel space** while the store is addressed in **φ**, so there is **no "known-erased" coordinate subset of the store's space** and the masked-NN oracle that beat CLU in 8/8 w24 cells **cannot be constructed**. The honest floor becomes 1-shot **kNN-in-φ** and its matched-compute version **feedforward-in-φ**. Measured at the hardest level (end-of-stream, 199–200 wells, $p=0.8$, **6 seeds**): first pass **0.839 ± 0.025** → **gated 0.885 ± 0.018 @ 1.40 ± 0.20×**; floors **kNN 0.874 ± 0.025 / feedforward 0.877 ± 0.025** ⇒ **gated − floor = +0.8 ± 1.6 pp (3/6 seeds positive) — a TIE**, and **gated − random-kick = +4.6 ± 2.2 pp in 6/6 seeds** with ensemble +4.4 and **ungated collapsing to 0.000**. ⭐ **This is the first regime in the program's history where the anytime read is not *below* the correct floor** — and it is a tie **because the author pre-registered that a win here must be re-run**, ran seeds 3–5, and reported what they gave. ⛔ **Say "ties the honest floor in the one regime with no constructible oracle" — never "wins."** ⚠ **Two scope items travel:** (i) one dataset, one corruption level (at $p=0.5$ the cell is saturated; on CIFAR the kNN floor is 1.000 and gated − kNN = −0.055 ± 0.019 — *a saturated floor is unbeatable*); (ii) ⚠ **$\tau$ is NOT the binding element of the gate in φ-space** — all four thresholds {0.99, 0.999, 0.9999, 1.0} give **identical** curves, because after a damped settle in a crowded φ store essentially every read sits below cosine 0.99, so what produces the curve is **the ranking + the 10 %-per-round budget + the lock**. That is a **behavioural difference from w24** (where $\tau=1.0$ demonstrably over-retried) and it is **1 seed** ⇒ the threshold clause must not be quoted as load-bearing in φ-space until multi-seeded. ⚠ **A control had to be rebuilt to get an honest floor at all:** `_feedforward_ladder`'s `clip(|q+\text{noise}|,0,1)` TTA is correct for pixels in $[0,1]$ and **destructive for a signed zero-mean φ vector** (the line collapsed to ~0.005 with $k$ — it was never a floor). **Any future φ-space use of `exp_retry_compute` must use `_feedforward_ladder_phi`.** ⭐ Free companion that **inverts an assumption in this section's framing**: there is **no forgetting-by-age** in the store — the task-age spread is 6.2 pp and the **newest** items are hardest (0.796), not the oldest (0.846), because they were admitted last into an already-crowded store. ⛔ Do not write *"retrieval degrades as the store crowds with age."*

> **⚠⭐ Update (wave-26) — the τ clause is NARROWED to a RULE, and the dial acquires a physical home (`carried-remeasurements` §1, N117; `readout-channel-theory` §3, N119).** **(a) The w25 τ caveat replicates *exactly* — and is too general.** At **p = 0.8** all four thresholds give **bit-identical** ladders (`max|Δacc| = max|Δcompute| = 0.000e+00`, **3/3 seeds × 2 store snapshots = 6/6 cells**, asserted with `==` on floats — *structural, not stochastic*). At **p = 0.5, in the same φ store, minutes apart**, the identity is **false in 6/6 cells** and **τ = 1.0 costs −0.485 ± 0.064 accuracy** (−0.546 ± 0.023 end-of-stream; e.g. 0.9950 → 0.4171) at **1.80×** compute instead of 1.12–1.30×. ⇒ **The rule, and it is exact arithmetic:** reads retried by round `k` = `min(k·step_n, #{cos₀ < τ})`, because an unretried read's cosine is frozen at its first-pass value ⇒ **τ is load-bearing iff `#{cos₀ < τ} < k·step_n`** (pool ≥ 160/200 at p = 0.8; **24–60/200** at p = 0.5). ⛔ **The "φ-space vs pixel-space" framing was the analyst's own pre-registered hypothesis and it is FALSIFIED** — *both behaviours occur inside φ-space.* **w24 and w25 measured the two ends of one axis; neither is retracted.** ⛔ **Every threshold statement names the corruption level.** The **tie is unchanged** by the remeasurement (gated 0.8631 vs the matched-compute kNN floor 0.8698 at p = 0.8, −0.007 ± 0.010). ⚠ Two harness facts that let this contradiction survive a wave: the retry cell saves `mean_confidence` but **not `#{cos₀ < τ}`** — the quantity that decides the question — and the τ-sweep runs **only on the last corruption level**. **(b) ⭐ A physical home for the dial.** The read-out theory's **trilemma** (*value fidelity · amplitude-independent hold · amplitude-independent latency — pick two*) identifies the third corner as **this dial**: a store that keeps the value exact and the hold amplitude-independent must let the read **take longer as the item fades** (`τ_y = η/(κ(g₀+A))`; 4× read steps buys payload-independence down to `A = 0.06`). ⇒ ***"a faded memory costs more integration steps to read"* is a physical, measurable statement that a timestamped row cannot make** — the first time two of the four dials are shown to be the same trade-off seen from different sides (**N119**).

**Status: [measured, 8 cells, single seed, MNIST, everything designed/closed-form on all six lines — nothing learned; w24: mechanism 5-seed-clean, leaderboard NO confirmed WITH headroom; w25: the R3-NATIVE cell is a 6-seed TIE — the first non-losing result, mechanism margins intact; w26: the τ clause narrowed to a measurable rule (`#{cos₀<τ}` vs `k·step_n`), the tie unchanged].** Sources: `retry-compute-study` §1–§6; `headroom-retry-benchmark` (N95); `multi-seed-w23` (N90 5-seed); `cl-entry-build` Item 3 (N103); the φ-space gate transfer is `phi-read-in` Item 4 (AUROC 0.845–0.988). Registry: **N90 · N95 · N103**; the w22 precursor is **N81**.

## 11.14 The one-slide primitive, in the Head's words: **atoms**, and **the two gates** **[plain-language records — new wave-24]**

**Why this section exists, and why its wording is protected.** The program's ambition (Head, 2026-07-24) is the transformer's *adoption* trajectory, not a leaderboard: *"propose the primitive so compellingly that thousands of researchers adopt, adapt and optimize it."* **Adoption is gated on legibility**, and the primitive fits on one slide:

> ### ***write = dig a valley · read = drop a ball · forget = let it fill in.***

The two records below were written by the Head and are reproduced **verbatim** (the blockquotes). **Do not rewrite them into technical prose — the imagery is the deliverable.** Everything outside the blockquotes is curator annotation: the measured numbers behind each image, with sources, so an onboarding reader can go from picture to evidence in one step. ⚠ **The imagery describes mechanisms, not wins.** Nothing here licenses a performance claim; the masked-recall demotion (§11.11, roadmap v0.7) is unaffected by how well the picture reads.

### Record 1 — **Atoms** (the terrain, and what actually caps it)

> **Atoms.** The landscape `V` is built from thousands of small Gaussian bumps ("atoms") — terrain sculpted from little mounds of clay. A stored item = a valley dug by arranging many atoms. The **designed write** places the clay by formula, one valley at a time. The **learned write** hands all atoms to one gradient optimization and asks it to dig all K valleys **jointly** — and that joint dig is what caps at ~32: different valleys fight over shared clay, and the write objective declares success (loss 0) while retrieval already fails. Not the terrain (designed digs ≥256), not parameters, not dimension, and — pending the ablation in point 3 — not numerics.
>
> — Head, `head-advisor-w23-direction.md` §"Plain-language records"

**The same thing in symbols.** The store is an atom dictionary $V(q) = \sum_{a=1}^{N_{\rm atoms}} w_a \exp\!\big(-\|q-c_a\|^2/2\sigma^2\big)$. *Designed write:* choose $(w_a, c_a)$ **per valley, in closed form** — item $i$ gets its own clay, so writes are bit-local and additively separable (§11.2, §11.9). *Learned write:* one gradient optimization over **all** $(w_a, c_a)$ simultaneously against a write loss for all $K$ items — the atoms are **shared**, so the wells are coupled through the optimizer even when they are far apart in $q$-space (§11.9's global-write regime).

**The Head's closing enumeration, with the measurement behind each clause** (`dimension-aware-budget` §1–§3, via **N92**; §11.6/§11.9 updates):

| the clause | the evidence it rests on | verdict |
|---|---|---|
| **not the terrain** | the *designed* mechanism reaches **≥256** items in the same cells where the learned write stalls at ~32 (designed line censored at `k_cap` 256) | ✅ settled |
| **not parameters** | d=6, K=64 gets **worse** with twice the atoms — strict **0.855 → 0.809** at **36.9k → 73.7k** params; d=4 K=32 flat at **~0.83** across a **16×** atom sweep | ✅ settled |
| **not dimension** | the ceiling is **d-independent**: K=64 is unwritable at **d=6 AND d=8** despite 4× different geometric room (site sep 0.795 vs 0.908) | ✅ settled |
| ✅ **not numerics (scale-DILUTION half only)** | **MEASURED (`write-ceiling-break`, w24).** The Head's **scale-invariance ablation** (a size-independent per-item write signal) moves the frontier cell d=6 K=64 by **\|Δ\|=0.010** (0.858 → 0.848, within seed noise) ⇒ the ceiling is **NOT** quantization / normalization / signal dilution. | ✅ **scale-dilution ruled out by measurement** |

> **Update (wave-24) — the record's fourth clause resolves HALF-way, and the *mechanism* is now CONTESTED (Hub ruling; do not quote as settled).** `write-ceiling-break` closed the numerics hypothesis in the sense the Head meant it — **scale-dilution is ruled out by measurement (\|Δ\|=0.010)** — and confirmed that **no write operator raises the ceiling** (masked/sequential writes *backfire*, collapsing capacity below 32; crowding-aware and combo also fail). But **what the ceiling *IS* is now under contest** and must NOT be written as settled either way:
> - `write-ceiling-break` reads it as *"optimization interference / a representational limit of the static write"* (a write-side **addressing** failure — basin ≈ strict at every failing cell).
> - `lattice-capacity-theory` §4.2 offers a competing **geometric width-lock** account: the critical separation is `sep_crit ≈ 2.4–3.0 × well width` at **every** designed AND learned wall d=2…8, which — via `minsep ∝ K^{-1/d}` flattening in high d — **predicts a d-independent-looking ceiling without any operator ceiling**, and reproduces the learned/designed tax as `2^{-d}` from the shipped `well_width 0.15` vs `atom_init_width 0.30`.
> - Crucially, the scale-invariance arm rescaled **depths/margins/barrier, NOT width+radius**, so it does **not** test the geometric account. **The decisive check is free:** dump the trained `log_width` on the w23 checkpoints (`lattice §5.0`); the one-flag `atom_init_width 0.30→0.15` run (`lattice §5.2`, predicted to move `K_learned` by ~`2^d`) is the other adjudicator. See **N92 (wave-24 update)** and **N96**.
> ⇒ **Approved phrasing for the fourth clause: "scale-dilution ruled out by measurement (\|Δ\|=0.010); the ceiling's mechanism is CONTESTED (a static-write optimization/representational limit vs a geometric fixed-width lock is under test) — never write 'the ceiling is the write operator' as settled."**

⭐ **The most quotable half of the record is the failure signature, and it is a real measurement:** *the write objective declares success (loss 0) while retrieval already fails.* That is what puts an **objective-side fix** (a crowding-aware margin) on the table alongside the write-operator fix (masked/local writes: **8474×** at d=2, **3434×** at d=4; w22's 197× → 599× → 6332×). ⚠ Note the crowding-aware margin was *tried* at w24 and **falls short** (0.792 < 0.9, actually *below* baseline) — it does not cross the ceiling either (N96).

> **✅⭐⭐ Update (wave-25) — the adjudicator RAN, and the record's closing sentence resolves: the cap is a REACH condition, and neither contestant was right (`r2-geometry-revival`, N100).** The free trained-`log_width` dump was performed, the one-flag experiment it authorised was run at 3 seeds, and the mechanism probes that followed found a third thing.
> - **What the dump found (the geometric account passes its registered test):** trained widths **0.215–0.364** (never near the 0.18 falsifier); the critical `sep/w_atom` ratio varies only **1.36×** across d; and at **d ≥ 4** a single threshold $t^\* \in (2.30, 2.49)$ separates **all seven cells with zero misclassifications**, *including the out-of-sample d=8 K=64 crack cell*. A d-independent operator ceiling has no business producing a correlate that sharp.
> - **What the experiment it authorised found (the same account's causal claim is dead):** `atom_init_width 0.30 → 0.15` does **not** raise `K_learned(4)` from 16 toward $2^d$ — it takes it **below 16** (**0.9368 ± 0.0133 → 0.5944 ± 0.0164**, 3 seeds, **28 SE**, flat under a 2× atom re-check). And the failing cell sits at `sep/width = 4.90`, *above every ratio at which the ladder ever passed* — **the variable that classifies the unmanipulated cells perfectly is useless the moment it is manipulated. That is the signature of a correlate, not a cause.**
> - **What actually binds (measured per-item, then confirmed by intervention):** the read launches every query on the **payload = 0 manifold**, a distance $|a_i|$ from the stored target along the payload channel; a Gaussian well of width $s$ exerts force $\propto D\,r/s^2 e^{-r^2/2s^2}$, which **collapses super-exponentially in $1/s$**. So a narrow well is **deep, sharp, and invisible from the launch point**. Measured: at width 0.15, items with **small** $|a_i|$ retrieve at **1.000** and items with **large** $|a_i|$ at **0.172** (corr $-0.887$), and the force at the launch manifold **collapses 5.1×** (0.881 → 0.171). Halving the excursion — with the tolerance halved so the *relative* precision is unchanged — takes the narrow-well store **0.586 → 1.000** (payload error 0.347 → 6.4e-5).
> ⇒ **Approved replacement for the record's closing enumeration** (the imagery above is untouched; only the *cause* clause changes): ***"not the terrain, not parameters, not dimension, not numerics — and not, as we first read it, the joint dig itself: the cap is a READ-OUT REACH condition. The well width is squeezed from below by the query noise ($s \gtrsim \sigma_q$) and from above-usefully by how far the read has to reach along the payload channel ($s \gtrsim |a|_{\max}/\kappa$, $\kappa \approx 3$). The shipped 0.30 sits at that second bound, and the write pushes any narrower init back toward it."*** ⛔ **Both prior attributions are retired as causes** — *"the ceiling is the write operator"* **and** *"a fixed-width geometric lock"*. ✅ **What survives from each:** the joint dig's failure signature is still real and still the most quotable half (*the write objective declares success while retrieval already fails*), and `sep/width` is still a **sharp d ≥ 4 correlate** worth reporting as one. ⭐ **A second, additive contribution was measured the same wave:** atoms initialised inside *other* items' wells cost **+0.051 (monolithic) / +0.082 (sharded)** at d=6 K=64 when repaired (**N98** w25 block) — some of what read as "valleys fighting over shared clay" is **clay that started in the wrong valley**. ⚠ **The picture's fair-warning clause:** the excursion intervention is a **mechanism result at a different task point, not a capacity number** — this harness's payload channel carries **no read-out noise**, so shrinking the excursion is free *here*. ⛔ **No `K_learned` may be quoted at `pscale ≠ 1`.** The fair successor (multi-channel payload + an annealed read, **payload noise ON**) is w26's heaviest experiment.

**Status: [imagery = Head-authored record, binding wording] · [three of four clauses settled — N92] · ~~[the fourth clause: scale-dilution RULED OUT by measurement (\|Δ\|=0.010, N96), but the ceiling's mechanism CONTESTED — geometric vs operator, adjudicator = the free trained-`log_width` dump]~~ → ✅ **RESOLVED (w25): scale-dilution ruled out by measurement AND the mechanism identified — a read-out REACH condition (N100); both prior attributions retired as causes; `sep/width` retained as a d ≥ 4 correlate**].** **Reading pointers:** §11.6 (the pinned law `K_learned(d)=min(2^d, ≈32)`), §11.9 (the write operator owns interference; the ceiling's mechanism is contested), `dimension-aware-budget` §0–§3, `write-ceiling-break`, `lattice-capacity-theory` §4.2/§5.0, registry **N92 (w24 update) / N96**.

### Record 2 — **The two gates** (why a policy, not just a landscape)

> **Admission gate (writing):** before storing, measure the distance from the proposed valley site to every existing valley; if closer than the safety distance (≈4.4× valley width — below which digging deforms a neighbour), relocate or refuse. A parking attendant who won't let you park close enough to dent the next car. The check is nearly free; hunting for a free spot in a crowded lot is what costs. This is why retention-per-admitted is a flat 1.000: nothing is stored that the store will later corrupt.
>
> **Confidence gate (reading):** after the ball settles, measure how close it stopped to a known valley centre. Dead-centre = confident, accept. In the no-man's-land between valleys = suspicious, boost and re-roll. Never re-kick a confidently settled ball — gateless retrying knocks correct answers into wrong valleys (measured: worse accuracy at more cost). The gate also makes compute self-limiting: when no low-confidence reads remain, spending stops on its own (~1.6×, not a fixed 9×).
>
> — Head, `head-advisor-w23-direction.md` §"Plain-language records"

**The admission gate, in symbols and numbers** (§11.12; `controller-mvp`, 5 paired seeds, d=3, s=0.35 ⇒ $d_{\rm safe}=1.54$; **N91, N93**):
- The rule is **admit iff $\min_j d(q_{\rm new},q_j)\ge d_{\rm safe}=4.4\,s$, else refuse-and-relocate** (400 candidate draws). The "≈4.4× valley width" in the Head's text is exactly this constant, and it is a *certificate of isolation*: the write is allowed only where Prop 2's protection (§11.3) is available.
- **"The check is nearly free; hunting for a free spot is what costs"** is measured: the gate itself is **0.006 ms**, while **refuse-and-relocate is 3.1 ms at $n_{\rm stored}=64$ ≈ 4× one 0.70 ms read** (≪ a gradient write, ≠ free vs a read).
- **"This is why retention-per-admitted is a flat 1.000"** is measured: **1.000 at every K to 64 — best of five** (gru 0.57 · mlp 0.43 · attn 0.34 · learned-CLU 0.16); controller OFF dies 1.000 → 0.110 → 0.009 → **0.000**.
- ⚠ **The price the image does not show, and which must travel with it:** per-**offered** retention is geometry-conditional — **0.081 at K=64 on a fixed radius-2 disk (last of seven)**, because the packing bound is 6.12 and the attendant refuses everything past it; **0.669 on a sized disk ($R=0.808\sqrt K$), which beats all four primitives**. *A parking attendant who turns cars away is only impressive if you also report how many were turned away.* **Both metrics always travel together** (forbidden claim, §11.7 update (4)).

**The confidence gate, in symbols and numbers** (§11.13; `retry-compute-study`, 8 cells, seed 0; **N90**):
- Confidence = **cosine to the nearest stored well after settling**; the lowest-confidence fraction is re-launched with a **directed momentum boost (×1.5)** and **locked** after retry.
- **"Never re-kick a confidently settled ball"** is measured: ungated retry-all **collapses 0.96 → 0.004 at 9× compute**, and the τ-sweep has a clean optimum — **τ=0.99: 0.961 @ ×1.64** vs no-gate **0.828 @ ×1.81** (*lower accuracy at higher cost*, exactly the Head's phrasing).
- **"Compute is self-limiting (~1.6×, not a fixed 9×)"** is measured: the curve is monotone and saturates at **×1.2–1.8** (flagship cell 0.359@1.0× → 0.961@1.64×) because the uncertain tail is finite.
- ⚠ **Design detail the image compresses:** cosine-to-nearest-well is a good **ranking** signal (correct 0.998 vs wrong 0.949) but a **useless acceptance** signal ⇒ **gate + lock-on-retry, never a per-item accept rule.** And the boost recovers *structured erasure* (mask) far better than *full-field noise* — past the basin cliff there is nothing to re-roll toward, which is why the w24 headroom benchmark must be built from **ambiguity, not destruction**.

**→ CLU, said plainly (the frame these two records serve).** The store's value is **not static lookup** — that is ceiling-capped by the very operation settling approximates (§11.11) — but **control over memory in time: admission · lifetimes · isolation under sequential writes · compute-adaptive reads.** The admission gate is dial 1, per-item decay (§11.12's leaky wells, `exp(−leak·t)`) is dial 2, write locality (§11.9) is dial 3, and the confidence gate is dial 4. ⭐ **Every future claim should be a claim about one of those four dials** (Head+Advisor, 2026-07-24 — binding; the roadmap v0.7 spine).

**Status: [imagery = Head-authored records, wording binding] · [every annotated number measured: `controller-mvp` 5 seeds, `retry-compute-study` 8 cells seed-0, `dimension-aware-budget` 2–3 seeds] · [nothing here is a performance claim].** **Reading pointers:** §11.9 (write operator), §11.12 (MVC-0 controller — the admission gate as built), §11.13 (test-time compute — the confidence gate as built), §11.6 (the capacity law), §11.11 (why static recall is the wrong axis); source `head-advisor-w23-direction.md` Parts 1–4; `research_roadmap.md` **v0.7**.

## 11.15 Three pictures from wave-25: **reach**, **the parking rule**, and **answering vs leaking** **[plain-language records — new wave-25]**

**Why this section exists.** §11.14 established the house style for the program's one-slide legibility: a picture first, then the measurement behind every clause, then the boundary. Wave-25 produced three mechanisms that each need one — the **reach condition** (the most important scientific result the program has produced, per the Head and two advisors), **PGCP** (the placement rule that makes deletion exact), and the **retention-vs-leakage separation** (the lifetimes dial's adversarial accounting). ⚠ **As in §11.14, the imagery describes mechanisms, not wins.** Nothing here licenses a performance claim.

### Record 3 — **Reach** (why a deeper, sharper well is a *worse* memory)

> **Reach.** A stored item is a valley in the landscape, and the read is a ball dropped from a fixed starting line — the **payload = 0 line**, the same line for every item, because the reader is not allowed to start by assuming the answer. An item whose value is large sits **far** from that starting line. Now make the valley narrower: it gets deeper and sharper, but its *pull* falls away faster than its depth grows — a narrow deep hole exerts almost no force on a ball rolling past at distance. **From far away, a narrow valley is invisible.** So the width cannot shrink: it is squeezed from below by the wobble in the query (the valley must be wide enough to catch a jittered ball) and from above-usefully by the distance the read has to travel (the valley must be visible from the starting line). The shipped width already sits at that second bound — and if you initialise it narrower, the training pushes it back out.

**The same thing in symbols.** A stored item is $z_i = (c_i, a_i)$: an address $c_i$ and a payload $a_i \in \mathrm{linspace}(-1,1,K)$. The read launches at $q_2(0) = 0$ (the anti-decoration guard, §11.2) — a distance $|a_i|$ along the payload channel from the target. A Gaussian well of width $s$ and depth $D$ exerts force
$$\|\nabla V\| \;=\; D\,\frac{r}{s^{2}}\exp\!\Big(-\frac{r^{2}}{2s^{2}}\Big),$$
which for $r \gg s$ **collapses super-exponentially in $1/s$**. Hence the two-sided condition — **the ceiling-side half is the one nobody had written down**:
$$\boxed{\;s \;\gtrsim\; \sigma_q \quad\text{(catch the jittered query)}\qquad\text{and}\qquad s \;\gtrsim\; |a|_{\max}/\kappa,\;\; \kappa = O(3)\quad\text{(be visible from the launch manifold)}\;}$$
With $|a|_{\max} = 1$ the second binds at $s \approx 0.3$ — **exactly the shipped `atom_init_width = 0.30`.**

**Every clause, with its measurement** (`r2-geometry-revival` §1/§2/§4; **N100**; d=4, 8192 atoms, value-blank controlled where stated):

| the clause | the evidence | verdict |
|---|---|---|
| *"make the valley narrower and it gets worse"* | `atom_init_width` **0.30 → 0.15**: strict **0.9368 ± 0.0133 → 0.5944 ± 0.0164** (3 seeds, **28 SE**); **flat at 2× atoms** (0.5859 → 0.6094) ⇒ a real collapse, not starvation | ✅ measured |
| *"the pull falls away faster than the depth grows"* | median $\|\nabla V\|$ at the launch manifold **0.881 → 0.171** — a **5.1× collapse** for a **1.63× narrowing** | ✅ measured |
| *"items far from the starting line are the ones that fail"* | per-item split at the median $\|a_i\|$: small-$\|a\|$ **1.000**, large-$\|a\|$ **0.172**; corr(strict, $\|a_i\|$) = **−0.887** (at width 0.30: 1.000 / 0.895, corr −0.508) | ✅ measured |
| *"it is reach, not packing"* | the failing cell sits at `sep/width = **4.90**` — *above every ratio at which the ladder ever passed* (max PASS 4.23) — **and fails at 0.594**; and capacity is **monotone increasing** in width (0.600 → 0.776 → 0.824 → 0.832 over 0.15 → 0.40) | ✅ the correlate is not the cause |
| *"shorten the reach and the wall moves"* | halve the excursion **with the tolerance halved**: d=4 K=16 **0.586 → 1.000** (payload error 0.347 → **6.4e-5**); **d=4 K=32 — w23's firm wall, flat across a 16× atom sweep — 0.824 → 1.000**; d=4 K=64 → **0.9922**, *at both widths* | ⚠ **mechanism only** (see boundary) |
| *"training pushes a narrow init back out"* | init 0.15 → trained **0.184–0.185 (+23 %)**; init 0.30 → trained 0.301–0.313 (+2 %) | ✅ measured |

⛔ **The boundary, and it is not negotiable.** The excursion cells are a **mechanism result at a different task point, not a capacity number**: this harness's payload channel carries **no read-out noise** (queries launch at exactly payload 0), so shrinking the excursion is free *here* in a way it would not be under a payload-noise model. **No `K_learned` may be quoted at `pscale ≠ 1`.** The fair successor — **more payload channels of smaller excursion carrying the same total precision**, and/or an **annealed read** — is w26's heaviest experiment, and **payload-noise-ON is its crux**.

**→ CLU, said plainly.** **The store's capacity ceiling was never a property of how tightly items can be packed; it was a property of how far the reader has to reach to collect the answer.** That is a statement about the **read-out interface**, which is a part of the primitive nobody had been treating as a design parameter — and it re-opens R2 on an axis (the output code) rather than on a knob.

**Status: [Stage-1 refutation: 3 seeds, 28 SE, budget-adequacy re-checked] · [Stage-0 width dump and the excursion cells: 1 seed each, declared] · [mechanism confirmed by intervention, not inferred from correlation] · [no capacity claim, no comparison to anything — R2 exemption, CM-23(m)].** **Reading pointers:** §11.6 (the capacity law), §11.9 (the write operator owns interference), §11.14 Record 1 (the atoms picture, whose fourth clause this resolves), `r2-geometry-revival` §1/§2/§4/§8, registry **N100** (+ the w25 blocks on **N92/N96**).

The **isotropic** blur — one global width, exactly what this record's picture would suggest — **fails** (0.824 → 0.238), *and the designed register collapses on the identical schedule* (1.000 → 0.344), which is the proof that the damage is geometric. ⚠ **All of it at d = 4 only.**

### Record 4 — **The parking rule** (how to make deletion exact by never having a "conflict" in the first place)

> **The parking rule.** The old admission gate is a parking attendant: cars arrive, and if a spot is too close to an existing car the attendant hunts for another. That works — but *where your car ends up depends on who arrived before you*, so removing a car does **not** leave the lot as it would have been if that car had never come. **The new rule paints permanent bays on the lot and gives every car a fixed ticket number.** Cars are parked in ticket order — each takes the first free bay on its own preference list — no matter what order they actually drove in. Now the lot's layout is a **function of which cars are present, and nothing else**. Remove one, re-park only the lower-numbered cars behind it, and you get **exactly** the lot that would have existed had that car never arrived — bit for bit. Bonus: painted bays pack **better** than hunting for gaps.

**The same thing in symbols.** Fix the store geometry $G$ (address disk of radius $R$, hex lattice $\Lambda$ of spacing $d_{\rm safe}$). Each record carries three deterministic per-key functions: a hash point $g(\kappa)$, a 64-bit priority $\mathrm{prio}(\kappa)$ (a strict total order), and a probe order $\pi(\kappa)$ (all cells sorted by distance to $g(\kappa)$). **Canonical placement** $\mathrm{pos}_S$: process live keys in **descending priority**; each takes the first cell of its own probe order not already taken by a higher-priority key. Then:
- **Thm 1 — $\mathrm{Store}(S)$ is a set function** (strong induction over descending priority; *no arrival order appears in the definition*).
- **Thm 2 — $\mathrm{delete}(\mathrm{Store}(S), i) = \mathrm{Store}(S \setminus \{i\})$ bit-identically** (via suffix stability: every key $\succ i$ is unmoved).
- **Thm 3 — the spacing certificate is free**: distinct lattice cells are $\ge d_{\rm safe}$ apart *by construction*, so the admission test is absorbed into the geometry (neighbour gradient factor $e^{-4.4^2/2} = 6.3\mathrm{e}{-5}$ — §11.12's "5 orders").
- **Thm 4 — decay and permanence COMMUTE with deletion**: amps factorise per record ($a_k(t) = \mathrm{base}_k e^{-\lambda_k (t - \mathrm{born}_k)}$), so deleting a *decaying* item at any point of its schedule leaves every survivor bit-identical to the never-written history.

**Every clause, with its measurement** (`order-independent-placement` §2/§3; **N99** w25 block; pure-numpy reference + a JAX check on the shipped PyTree):

| the clause | the evidence |
|---|---|
| *"same cars, same spots, whatever the arrival order"* | **24/24** exhaustive orders (n=4) · **200 random orders** at n ∈ {8,16,40,64} · **100 write/delete interleavings** · mid-decay deletes — **all bit-identical** (`tobytes()` equality, **0 tolerance**); min spacing exactly **1.540000** at every n |
| *"the lot is the same as if the car had never come"* | write-then-delete ≡ never-written, **bit-identical**; on the **shipped** `AtomStorePotential`: `centers/payloads/amps/active` **byte-equal** and $V(q)$ byte-equal on 64 random queries (**max $|\Delta V| = 0.0$**) ⇒ **zero production-code change to the store class** |
| *"painted bays pack better"* | sized K=64: **61/64 = 0.953** admitted (deterministic) vs refuse-and-relocate **43.0 ± 1.7** vs no-collision-handling 23.8 ± 1.9; fixed R=2: **7 cells vs 6.0 ± 0.7** ⇒ **the price of exactness is NEGATIVE** |
| *"re-park the cars behind it"* (the real cost) | **2.84 moves/delete at full load** (median 3 — a declared, mild PREREG miss; max 7), **0.22 at half load**; each move is bookkeeping-cheap (`evict` + `with_item`, no physics run) — **but deletion legitimately relocates lower-priority survivors**, so reads must use the record's current centre |
| *"decay doesn't break it"* | permanent item amp **1.0** after 5 ticks; leaky survivors at $e^{-1.75} = 0.173774$ **exactly**, with the victim deleted mid-schedule |

⛔ **The boundary, stated as the theorist stated it.** **Below capacity ⇒ full exactness.** Overflow under this rule is **priority eviction — itself a set-function policy** (insert-only streams stay order-independent; full counterfactual exactness over the *offered* stream needs the specified-but-unbuilt waitlist). ⛔ **LRU/staleness eviction is intrinsically historical** (`last_used` is query history) and is **permanently excluded** from the deletion claim. ⛔ **The discrete skeleton is prior art** — strongly-history-independent (uniquely-represented) hashing — so the novelty is the **continuous-landscape composition** (a physical decay law + permanence + a spacing certificate + the packing economics), **never exactness per se**; those citations are **not yet verified from primaries** and gate the wording. ⛔ **"Certified" stays forbidden** — nothing here supplies $(\varepsilon,\delta)$. ⛔ **Never quote "0.953" as measured retention** until the rematch cell runs under the **real two-phase read**. ⛔ And the rule **has no code path yet**: `Controller.delete` does not exist.

**→ CLU, said plainly.** **The reason the store could not claim deletion was never physics — it was the parking attendant.** Replace a stochastic, history-dependent placement search with a deterministic canonical assignment and *deletion becomes set subtraction*, with the isolation certificate thrown in free and a **better** packing than the rule it replaces. The claim now has **a code task, a citation task, and a number to hit** (drive the post-evict hole-detection AUC from **0.99985 to ~0.5**) — which is a far better position than "blocked by a technical gap."

Defensible novelty = the **four-part composition** (packing certificate · decaying content + commutation proof · canonical **energy function** · a **negative** SHI price against Buchbinder–Petrank's proven exponential cost). ⛔ New bans: **"deletion-compliant"** (EUROCRYPT'20 term of art whose dependency clause we fail) and **"our fix-up cascade"**; Hartline may be cited only for the definitional equivalence (his Thm 1 needs a **reversible** structure — our amplitude layer is not). **(f) ⚠ Cascade-cost wording:** *2.84 moves/delete* is **one key-set realization** (reproduced to 3 dp); over 200 key sets the same lattice gives **3.865** ⇒ quote ***"~3–4 at full lattice load, ~0.2–0.4 at half, ~0 below half."*** (**N99 w26 block, N118**.)

### Record 5 — **Answering vs leaking** (the store stops answering before it stops leaking)

> **Answering vs leaking.** Let an item fade and two different things happen at two different rates. **Answering** is whether the ball still rolls into the right valley when a query arrives — that degrades early, because a shallow valley catches a smaller and smaller neighbourhood. **Leaking** is whether someone who can *inspect the landscape* can still tell the item was ever written — and that survives much longer, because a faint dent is still a dent. Measured on one axis, the two curves **never cross**: at the last moment before the item self-evicts, the store answers **83 %** of its queries while an observer still identifies membership at **AUC 0.98**. ⇒ ***the store stops answering before it stops leaking.*** And against an observer with **exact arithmetic**, fading buys nothing at all: the dent shrinks in proportion to the amplitude, but "is there a dent?" stays perfectly answerable at every stage of the item's life. What fading *does* buy — with no observer in the picture at all — is a **shrinking catchment**: the radius from which a query is still pulled home contracts by 1.5× as the item fades, which a row with a timestamp cannot express.

**Every clause, with its measurement** (`mia-decay-measurement` §0–§3; **N108**; designed store, 8 targets × 3 seeds, **128 paired IN/OUT worlds** per target, 18 amplitude levels, per-example U-LiRA-style; **no learning anywhere**):

| the clause | the evidence |
|---|---|
| *"the curves never cross"* | at the last amplitude before self-eviction ($A = 0.051$, $\tau = 2.976$): **retention 0.832** vs **query-MIA AUC 0.983** (TPR **0.858 @ FPR 1 %**) vs **white-box 1.000**; the gap opens monotonically from 0.000 to **+0.151**, then to +0.500/+1.000 after eviction |
| ⛔ *"against exact arithmetic, fading buys nothing"* | **white-box AUC = 1.000 at ALL 18 amplitude levels**; the depth gap is **$0.3935\cdot A$ to four decimals at every level** (the closed form $1 - e^{-1/2}$) and $d'$ falls **1560 → 79.6** — *the effect size decays, the attack's success does not*. **Pre-registered as the expected outcome, and the falsifier fired as registered.** |
| ✅ *"against a blurry observer it is genuinely graded"* | $A_{75} = 2.57\,\sigma_{\rm obs}$: measured **0.0780 / 0.2627 / 0.7537** at $\sigma_{\rm obs} = 0.03/0.1/0.3$ vs predicted 0.0771 / 0.2570 / 0.7710 (**+1.2 %, +2.2 %, −2.2 %**) |
| ⛔ *"a boolean flag would do just as well — sometimes"* | vs a CLU-with-a-TTL-flag control at $A = 0.06$: exact white-box **1.000 vs 1.000 (separation 0.000 — no differentiator)**; at $\sigma_{\rm obs} = 0.1$, **0.559 vs 0.996 (separation 0.437)** ⇒ **the differentiator exists only relative to a stated observer resolution** |
| ⭐ *"the catchment shrinks"* (**the caveat-free differentiator**) | $R_{50}$ (read radius at which retention halves) **1.146 → 1.083 → 0.979 → 0.874 → 0.752** as $A: 1 \to 0.06$ — a **1.52×** contraction, matching the pre-registered saddle calculation at all five points — where a **TTL vector-store's lookup radius is a constant hard step at ≈0.77, independent of age** |
| ⛔ *"and eviction does not make the item gone"* | with **placement held fixed**, every post-evict statistic is **0.5000 ± 0.0000** (the two stores are bit-identical) — but under the **shipped** history-dependent placement, distance-to-nearest-live-site alone gives **AUC 0.99985 ± 0.0007, TPR 1.000 @ FPR 1 %**, *after the item is gone from $V$*; and the raw arrays still hold the address and value verbatim (max err 5.6e−8 / 0.0) |

⛔ **The boundary.** **Store-level only** — no φ, no learned $V$, no payload encoder; **no $(\varepsilon,\delta)$ claim**, and the words *certified / unlearning / privacy guarantee* are not used. **$\sigma_{\rm obs}$ is our modelling choice, not a property of CLU** ⇒ its defensible use is **mechanistic** (*"the leak signal is $0.3935\cdot A$"*), **never protective**. The **0.99985** was measured at **7/8 occupancy — the most favourable case for a hole statistic** and must be re-measured at low occupancy before it is quoted as general. ⚠ And the dial has a measured defect: **effective lifetime is payload-dependent** ($r = -0.846$ with $a_i^2$ — retention at the floor is 0.999 for $a = \pm 0.1429$ but **0.500 for $a = +1$**), because the $q_2(0) = 0$ guard makes each site a payload **hill** of height $\tfrac12\kappa a_i^2$ against a well of depth $A$. *Only the user loses — the query-MIA is flat in $a_i$.*

**→ CLU, said plainly.** **Retention and detectability are different physical quantities, and the store's dial moves one of them.** The honest sentence is *"the store stops answering before it stops leaking"* — an asymmetry, measured, that degrades the **user** before the **attacker**. The claim to lead with is therefore **not** privacy but **retrieval geometry**: *decay contracts an item's addressing tolerance, which a timestamped row cannot express.* Everything privacy-flavoured is **observer-relative and must say so.**

**Status: [measured — 24 per-example values × 128 paired worlds × 18 levels, 3 seeds, designed store, no learning] · [5 of 10 pre-registrations confirmed, 1 partial, 4 falsified — the two decision-relevant falsifications both point the same way: the store leaks through channels the amplitude dial does not control] · [no privacy claim; every adversary number is relative to a stated threat model].** **Reading pointers:** §11.12 (the decay verb and its corrected retention row), Record 4 above (the placement rule that must drive the 0.99985 to 0.5), `mia-decay-measurement` §0–§8, registry **N108** and **N99 (w25 update)**.

> **⭐ Update (wave-26) — the leak is a CURVE, the eviction hole is closed, and the payload-dependence is RULED to be a chosen corner, not a bug.** **(a) ⛔ "0.99985" was the top of a curve.** Post-evict `AUC(z_hole)` is **0.6715 ± 0.0405 / 0.9165 ± 0.0265 / 0.9961 ± 0.0040 / 0.99985 ± 0.00070** and `TPR@FPR 1 %` **0.029 / 0.118 / 0.924 / 1.000** at **2 / 4 / 6 / 8** offers into a capacity-8 store. **Mechanism, measured:** the **IN-world minimum `z_hole` equals `d_safe` = 1.5400 to 4 dp at every load** — the write reserves a hard exclusion disk that eviction does not give back; **what changes with load is how tightly the background packs in the OUT world** (mean 1.9575 → 0.7128) ⇒ *the attacker's separation comes from the background getting closer, not from the hole getting deeper.* The paired-placement control is **0.5000 ± 0.0000 in 24/24** ⇒ **placement is the entire post-evict leak.** ⭐ **Standing rule, adopted program-wide: "quote the curve, not the endpoint."** **(b) The hole is closed at the source** (Record 4 (b)): under canonical placement every post-evict statistic is **0.5000 ± 0.0000** with byte-identical stores — **inside the below-capacity scope.** And the D1 defect is **fixed**: `evict` now scrubs `centers`/`payloads`, verified neutral (`max |ΔV| = 0.0`; the whole relocate arm reproduces its published numbers to 4–5 dp). **(c) ⭐ The payload-dependence is RULED — and it is a TRILEMMA, not a defect.** *Value fidelity · amplitude-independent hold · amplitude-independent latency — **pick two***: exact value ⇒ the launch point is a hill of height `½κ_eff a_i²`; making the hold amplitude-independent needs `κ_eff ∝ A_i`; but then the payload settling time `τ_y = η/κ_eff ∝ 1/A_i`. **The shipped store simply chose a corner.** ⛔ **Both fixes this record's boundary floated are REFUTED:** `payloads*amps` kills the value criterion at `A = 1 − tol/|a_i|` (measured death amplitudes 0.90/0.80/0.70/0.30 vs the formula's 0.900/0.860/0.767/0.300 — exact), and launching at `q₂ = S(q_addr)` **breaks the anti-decoration guard** — *the trivial substitute (return `S` at the launch point, run **no dynamics at all**) already passes the shipped value criterion on **0.672** of queries, amplitude-independently.* ✅ Recommended instead: a **gated-stiffness** payload channel (**retention 1.000, payload-independent, at every amplitude to the floor** at `g₀ = amp_floor`) — at the price that the on-site curve becomes a **step** (the TTL shape), while the *graded* configuration needs `g₀ ≪ amp_floor` **plus a longer read as the item fades** ⇒ ⭐ **the compute-adaptive-read dial.** ⚠ **`R₅₀` — this record's caveat-free differentiator — was NOT measured under the gate and must be re-measured before the claim is re-used.** ⚠ **And every retention magnitude here is placement-dependent:** on a max-radius ring the same store gives **0.500 at A = 0.06** where the controller-placed disk gives **0.886** ⇒ state the `|c|` distribution with any retention number (**N119**, N108 w26 block).
