## 11.16 Three pictures from wave-26: **the shorter reach**, **the saddle**, and **the launder that deletes the physics** **[plain-language records — new wave-26]**

**Why this section exists.** §11.14 and §11.15 established the house style. Wave-26 produced three things that need one: the **intervention that finally moved a standing capacity wall** (and did it from the *read-out* side, which nobody had treated as a design surface), the **criterion that replaced the reach bound one wave after it was written**, and the **control that measured the program's own dynamics as a net negative** — the least comfortable and most important picture in the file. ⚠ **As in §11.14–§11.15, the imagery describes mechanisms, not wins.** Nothing here licenses a performance claim; **Record 8 is explicitly a negative.**

### Record 6 — **The shorter reach** (move the answer closer, or lend the reader a torch)

> **The shorter reach.** Record 3 said the reader is a ball dropped from the same starting line for every item, and that an item whose *value* is large sits far from that line — so a narrow valley, however deep, cannot be felt from there. There are only two honest ways out, and w26 built both. **(1) Put the answers nearer the line.** Instead of writing one number along one axis from −1 to +1, write it as a short address in **several** channels — the same number of distinct answers, the same spacing between neighbouring answers, so nothing is made easier to tell apart — but the *furthest* answer is now a tenth as far away. **(2) Lend the reader a torch, and take it back.** Leave the stored landscape exactly as it is, but during the phase where the ball is still deciding *which* valley it belongs to, temporarily blur **only the payload direction** — so the far valleys become visible — then sharpen back to the true landscape before the value is read off. The torch costs nothing: no extra numbers stored, no extra dimensions, not even extra steps (the same total integration, split across the schedule). ⚠ **And the third way — blurring everything at once — does not work, which is the proof that this is geometry and not tuning:** the *designed* store, which has no learning in it at all, collapses on exactly the same schedule.

**The same thing in symbols.** The read must satisfy two inequalities at the same time: **reach**, $s \gtrsim |a|_{\max}/\kappa$ (be visible from the launch manifold), and **packing**, $s \lesssim \mathrm{sep}/2.4$ (keep neighbouring wells apart). At $d=4$, $K=32$ these are $s \gtrsim 0.33$ and $s \lesssim 0.30$: **the windows do not overlap.** But they live on **different axes** — reach along the payload channel, packing along the address axes — so:
- **arm (a), the code:** put the $K$ codewords on the $\Delta$-spaced lattice in $\mathbb{R}^m$ with $\Delta = 2/(K-1)$ (the 1-channel spacing) and keep the $K$ smallest-norm points ⇒ **same $\log_2 K$ bits, same per-axis distinguishability, excursion $\max\|a\|$ down from 1.000 to 0.091** at $m=4$, $K=32$;
- **arm (b), the torch:** $\text{width}_{\text{payload}}(\ell) = \kappa(\ell)\, s_j$ with $\kappa: \kappa_0 \to 1$ over $L$ stages, **address widths untouched**, `address_steps`/`read_steps` split across stages so the step count is identical.

**Every clause, with its measurement** (`r2-excursion-reach` §3/§4/§5/§6; **N109–N111**; $d=4$, 8192 atoms, global write, `pscale = 1`, **payload read-noise ON** ($\sigma_{\rm launch}=0.05$), nearest-codeword **decode**, value-blank ✅ on every PASS):

| the clause | the evidence | verdict |
|---|---|---|
| *"lend the reader a torch and take it back"* | $d{=}4$, $K{=}32$ — the w23 **firm** wall (flat at 0.825–0.840 over a 16× atom sweep): **0.8210 ± 0.0028 → 0.9997 ± 0.0005** ($\kappa_0{=}3$, $L{=}4$, **3 seeds**), $\Delta = +0.1787$ at SE 0.0016 ⇒ **~110 SE**; under noise **0.8190 → 0.9980 ± 0.0000** at $\sigma_{\rm obs}=0.010$, where the designed reference still passes (0.9990) | ✅ measured |
| *"the torch costs nothing"* | **0 extra parameters, 0 extra latent dimensions, 0 extra Verlet steps, no change to the stored format** (the schedule is a tuple of Python floats, provably outside the write's `trainable_filter` — asserted in a test) | ✅ by construction + test |
| *"put the answers nearer the line"* | $m{=}1 \to m{=}4$ at $K{=}32$: **0.8047 ± 0.0060 → 1.0000** ($\sigma_{\rm obs}=0$ and 0.005) and **0.9980 ± 0.0008** at 0.010; $\max\|a\|$ **1.0000 → 0.0912** at **identical** code $\Delta = 0.0645$ | ✅ measured |
| ⭐ *"the wall is now the designed wall"* | at $\sigma_{\rm obs} = 0/0.005/0.010$: designed **128 / 64 / 32**, shipped learned **16 / 16 / 16**, + torch **32 / 32 / 32**, + code **128 / 64 / 32** ⇒ **the learned/designed prefactor tax goes 1/8 → 1/1 at every noise level measured** | ✅ measured, bracketed by a PASS *and* a FAIL on both arms |
| ⭐ *"it is address-time blindness"* (**the control that names the mechanism**) | **`readonly`** — anneal the *value* phase only — reproduces the baseline **to four decimals** (0.8210 vs 0.8210; 0.9368 vs 0.9368 at $K{=}16$) ⇒ the decision is made while the address is being chosen; **this is why `basin ≡ strict` at every failing cell since w23** | ✅ the mechanism, isolated |
| ⭐ *"and you must take the torch back"* | **`static`** — widen and never sharpen — reaches **basin 0.9993** and reads the **wrong value** (0.6250 ± 0.0255 on `tol`; **0.2389 ± 0.0150** under decode) ⇒ *"just use wider wells" is a **different, failing** result*; the **continuation** is what buys fidelity | ✅ the counterfactual |
| ⭐ *"it is the code, not the bytes"* | **`spectator`** — same $m{=}4$ latent geometry, same **+43 %** parameters, code left at excursion 1.0 — buys **+0.034**; the code buys **+0.195** | ✅ the fairness control |
| ⛔ *"blurring everything at once fails"* | isotropic blur at $s_{\rm extra}=0.30$: learned **0.8242 → 0.2383**; **designed 1.000 → 0.3438 on the identical schedule**; mass-preserving mode 0.3086 | ✅ refuted, **and the refutation is the mechanism** |
| *"the laundering line never moved"* | `K_designed(4) = **128** at m = 1, 2 and 4`; the designed arm also **defines the format's noise ceiling** (it fails $K{=}128$ at $\sigma_{\rm obs}=0.005$, $K{=}64$ at 0.010) and **no learned PASS is claimed above it** | ✅ control intact |
| *"and it is not starvation"* | at arm (b)'s first-fail cell ($K{=}64$), doubling the atoms 8192 → 16 384 gives 0.8506 → 0.8027 — **flat-to-worse** | ✅ N92 protocol |

⛔ **The boundary, and it is part of the claim.** **$d = 4$ ONLY — $d=6$ and $d=8$ were NOT RUN** (compute; declared in the PREREG's priority order), so **no exponent is re-measured** and $K_{\rm designed} = 4\cdot 2^d$ is untouched (128 = $4\cdot 2^4$, measured). $K{=}128$ is **2 seeds**; arm (b)'s $K{=}64$ FAIL rests on **1 seed + the atom re-check**; **the two arms are untested in combination**. ⛔ **Never quote a `tol`-metric number at $m>1$** — a **blank landscape scores 1.0000** there (decode blank: 0.0312 = 1/32, exactly chance). ⛔ **Never quote `K_learned` without its $\sigma_{\rm obs}$ and the designed-arm reference**, and **never at `pscale ≠ 1`** — the w25 free lunch **dies** under read noise (0.9995 → 0.5894) where the constant-$\Delta$ code passes (0.9980). ⛔ **w21's bits-per-param is STALE**; items-per-parameter rises ×2.00–5.60 at the same cell, but **nobody has re-derived bits-per-param.**

**→ CLU, said plainly.** **The store's capacity ceiling was a property of the *interface*, not of the physics — and interfaces are designable.** Two changes on the read-out side, neither of which touches the write objective, the atom budget or the potential, take the learned capacity at $d=4$ to the designed capacity. That is the first time in this program that a standing wall has moved, and the reason it is credible is not the headline number but the **three controls**: one that reproduces the baseline exactly, one that gets the address right and the value wrong, and one that pays the bytes without buying the gain.

**Status: [measured — 3 seeds at the decisive cells (~110 SE), payload read-noise ON, value-blank at chance on every PASS, budget-adequacy re-checked] · [d = 4 ONLY; d=6/d=8 declared NOT RUN; K=128 is 2 seeds] · [a law about the primitive — R2 exemption, CM-23(m): its figure is never framed as beating anything].** **Reading pointers:** §11.6 (the capacity law + its w26 update), §11.15 Record 3 (the reach picture this dissolves), Record 7 below (the criterion that replaced its formula), `r2-excursion-reach` §1–§10, registry **N109 / N110 / N111** and the w26 blocks on **N100 / N98**.

### Record 7 — **The saddle** (why no amount of digging buys reach, and why a ratio that predicts perfectly is still not a cause)

> **The saddle.** Two forces act on the rolling ball: the valley's pull, which is huge nearby and dies off super-exponentially with distance, and the bowl's own inward pull toward the centre, which is gentle and **constant**. Whether the ball ever reaches its valley is not a question of the valley's depth in isolation — it is whether, somewhere between the ball and the valley, those two forces balance and create a **ridge**. If the ridge exists between them, the ball is trapped on the wrong side and rolls to the middle of the bowl instead; if not, it always arrives. **So the failure is not "the ball misreads the value" — it is "the ball never gets there", i.e. an addressing failure**, and that is why a failed read always lands on whichever item happens to sit nearest the bowl's centre. Now the discouraging part: the position of that ridge depends on the valley's depth only through a **logarithm**. Digging ten times deeper moves the reachable distance by a few per cent. **You cannot buy reach by digging.** And the pretty ratio everyone quotes — separation divided by width — turns out to predict well only because the *width* barely varies; the **separation alone** predicts better.

**The same thing in symbols.** For a well of depth $D$, width $s$ at $z_i = (c_i, a_i)$ in a confinement $\alpha|q|^2$, **every** stationary point lies on the ray through $z_i$, at distance $R$ from it solving
$$\boxed{\;\Big(\tfrac{D R}{s^{2}}\Big)e^{-R^{2}/2s^{2}} \;=\; 2\alpha\,(L-R),\qquad L \equiv |z_i| = \sqrt{|c_i|^{2}+a_i^{2}}\;}\tag{$\star$}$$
With $v = R/s$, $A = L/s$, $\beta = D/(2\alpha s^2)$ this is $h(v) \equiv v\,(1+\beta e^{-v^2/2}) = A$: for $\beta \le e^{3/2}/2 = 2.2408$ there is **one** root and **no spurious minimum can ever exist**; for $A > \kappa_{\rm stat}(\beta)$ there are three, and the middle one $R_2$ is a **saddle**. ⇒ **(U) the query is captured by item $i$ iff $L/s < \kappa_{\rm stat}(\beta)$ or $|a_i| < R_2$.** Every closed form for the ceiling is a **square root of a logarithm** ($\kappa_{\rm stat}\approx\sqrt{2\ln\beta}$; the conservative address-hold form is $\kappa_R = \sqrt{2\ln(D/(2\alpha|c|s))-1}$).

**Every clause, with its measurement** (`readout-channel-theory` §1–§2; **N120**; the learned cell reproduces `r2-geometry-revival`'s to 4 dp — $w_{\rm atom}$ 0.3013 vs 0.30129, strict 0.9473 vs 0.947266):

| the clause | the evidence |
|---|---|
| *"($\star$) is the criterion"* — on the **shipped integrator** | measured/predicted capture-threshold ratio **1.013 ± 0.025 (2.4 % rel. sd) over 7 variants**; the transition is a **saddle-node bifurcation** — capture goes 1.000 → 0.000 in **one 0.1 grid step** at every variant. The PREREG's closed form $\Psi$ is **1.357 ± 0.064 too tight**; $\kappa_{\rm stat}\!\cdot\!s$ is **0.871 ± 0.036 too loose**; neither is the threshold |
| ⭐ *"and it holds on the real, trained landscape with nothing fitted to it"* | radial-profile fits at all 32 sites ($R^2 \ge 0.9938$) feed measured $(D_i, s_i, |c_i|)$ into ($\star$): **(U) classifies 15/16 at width 0.30 and 16/16 at width 0.15 ⇒ 31/32, ZERO free parameters**; the unpinned flow test scores 16/16 and 15/16. ⚠ **Do not pin the payload channel while descending** — the pinned variant is the conservative bound and scores only 10/16, 13/16 |
| ⭐ *"the failure is an ADDRESS failure"* | of the three candidate mechanisms, **spurious-minimum-on-the-payload-ray fires 0/32** (never), the payload-descent time budget is **slack by ≥10×**, and the address-hold criterion is the binding one ⇒ **`basin ≡ strict` because they are the same event** |
| *"a failed read lands on whatever sits nearest the centre"* | every failed flow at width 0.15 ends at $|x_{\rm end}| = 0.064$, $y_{\rm end} = 0.067$ — **item 13, the single site the farthest-point sampler placed at the ball centre**; 6/7 failures at width 0.15 and the one failure at width 0.30 |
| ⛔ *"you cannot buy reach by digging"* | $\kappa_{\rm stat} =$ **3.33 / 4.08 / 4.67 / 6.06** at $\beta = 10/10^2/10^3/10^6$ ⇒ raising $\kappa$ **4 → 5 needs $\beta\times55$** ($D$ 0.69 → 37.9 at $s=0.3$) and **4 → 6 needs $\times9200$**. The levers with leverage are $s$, $|a|_{\max}$, $\alpha$ and $|c|$ — all four enter ($\star$) algebraically |
| ⚠ *"the shipped store sits on the boundary"* | $a_U(0.302,\,0.91,\,0.05,\,0.98) = \mathbf{1.06}$ against $|a|_{\max} = 1.0$ — a **6 % margin**, *which is why every one-flag intervention moves it* |
| ⛔ *"sep/width is a reparameterisation, not a cause"* | **`sep` ALONE separates 11/11 Stage-0 cells** (min PASS **0.849**, max FAIL **0.795**) where `sep/w` separates only $d\ge4$ — because $w(d)$ happens to be flat there (0.30–0.36) and drops to 0.215–0.29 at $d\le3$ while the `sep` boundary does not move. Forcing the ratio to 4.90 destroys retrieval *because it does so by shrinking $s$* ($a_U$ 1.06 → 0.63, $D$ 0.91 → 0.46): **predicted strict 0.625, measured 0.5859** |
| ⭐ *"and the write already knows"* (new, and it runs **for** the learned arm) | $\mathrm{corr}(s_{\rm fit}, |a_i|) = \mathbf{+0.821}$ at width 0.30 (+0.630 at 0.15): **far-payload wells are 1.31× wider** (0.353 vs 0.270). It also digs **shallower** when atoms are narrow, $D \propto s^{1.46}$ ⇒ **`atom_init_width` moves the ceiling through two channels.** A constant-width designed surrogate reproduces only **5/11** cells ⇒ **the write's per-item width adaptation is load-bearing** |

⛔ **The boundary.** **(U) is single-well** — no neighbour term — and a pure reach account classifies only **8/11** cells, missing exactly the three crowded high-$K$ FAILs (d4K32, d5K64, d6K64), which are a **multi-well interference** failure it does not model; the complete law needs $\mathrm{strict} \approx \min(1, a_U/|a|_{\max}) \times P_{\rm addr}(\mathrm{sep}, s, \sigma_q)$ and **only the first factor is derived** — *that is the theorist's own open question, stated as one.* **One seed per learned cell** (the 31/32 has no error bar). (U) was derived **after** seeing the toy's 1.36 × offset, so its evidence is the **out-of-sample** 31/32 on the trained `V`, not the toy agreement. **No read noise anywhere** in this pass. ⚠ **Two knobs are re-classified: `learned_confine` and the ball radius are REACH PARAMETERS, not free knobs** (they enter only through the product $2\alpha|c_i|$; halving $\alpha$ moves the threshold **+9.1 %**, moving a site from $|c|=0.9$ to 0.3 moves it **+9.5 %**) — an independent causal test on `learned_confine` is commissioned.

**→ CLU, said plainly.** **The capacity ceiling is a bifurcation, and bifurcations do not respond to effort.** Depth is a logarithm, so the store cannot dig its way to a bigger memory; what it *can* do is shorten the distance the reader travels (Record 6) or move the item closer to the bowl's centre. And the program's sharpest-looking empirical regularity — a ratio that classified eleven cells perfectly — turns out to be **the numerator wearing a denominator that barely varies.** *A perfect classifier is evidence about the world; it is not automatically a mechanism.*

**Status: [derived + verified — ±2.5 % on the shipped integrator across 7 variants; 31/32 items on the trained shipped `V` with ZERO free parameters] · [1 seed per learned cell; single-well, no neighbour term; the three crowded high-K cells are outside its model] · [supersedes w25's `s ≳ |a|max/κ`, κ ≈ 3 — that wording is retired] · [no performance claim of any kind].** **Reading pointers:** §11.15 Record 3 (the picture it re-derives), Record 6 above (the intervention it explains), §11.6 (the capacity law), `readout-channel-theory` §1–§2 + `PREREG.md`, registry **N120** and the w26 block on **N100**.

### Record 8 — **The launder that deletes the physics** (the most uncomfortable measurement in the file, and the one to publish first)

> **The launder that deletes the physics.** Every previous "trivial baseline beat us" result had an escape hatch: *the baseline holds a different memory.* This one has none. Take the full system — the learned features, the admission gate, the placement, the eviction policy, the wells, the byte budget — and change **exactly one thing**: instead of dropping the ball and letting it roll into a valley, just **look up which valley centre is nearest** and read its answer. Same keys. Same wells. Same bytes. **The look-up wins** — a little, consistently, at every budget, on both axes. On a stream of clean classification queries the rolling is not merely unnecessary; **it costs about a percentage point.** ⭐ And the one place it does *not* lose is the clue: when the query is a **corrupted** version of something stored — where "nearest" is genuinely ambiguous — the settle **ties** the honest floor instead. *The settle costs you when the query is clean and pays when it is ambiguous* is a much smaller sentence than "a memory with dials", and it is the best-evidenced sentence the program owns.

**Every clause, with its measurement** (`matched-bytes-frontier` §1–§4; **N113–N116**; Split-MNIST Class-IL, **6 byte budgets spanning 50×**, **3 seeds**, byte accounting pinned **before** any run, launder run at matched **bytes**):

| the clause | the evidence |
|---|---|
| *"same bytes, and the accounting is against us"* | pinned, deliberately conservative: CLU store **41 floats/item** (every per-item scalar charged, even ints/bools) · kNN-φ ring buffer **34** · ER/GDumb/iCaRL **785** · DER++ **795** ⇒ **19.1×** more items for CLU at the same bytes — and **the launder is handed 1.21× more keys than CLU has wells** at every point |
| ⭐ *"against the replay field, the store wins"* | **higher ACC at 6/6** budgets — **0.903 ± 0.003 vs iCaRL 0.885 ± 0.002** at 1.2 MiB, +0.064 over iCaRL at the smallest — and **lower forgetting at 5/6**. *That reverses the matched-**items** picture* (w25: CLU 0.153 **below** iCaRL) |
| ⛔ *"and the launder is never beaten"* | matched-bytes ring buffer: **lower forgetting 6/6** ($\Delta$ = +0.032, +0.042, +0.027, +0.039, +0.018, +0.005 — narrowing but never closing) and **higher ACC 6/6**. **Fifth consecutive laundering** |
| ⭐⛔ *"delete the settle and it gets better"* (**the record's point**) | **same-keys** launder (arg-min over the store's **own** wells): forgetting **0.179 / 0.132 / 0.101 / 0.100 / 0.065 / 0.044** vs CLU **0.193 / 0.149 / 0.113 / 0.108 / 0.076 / 0.049** ⇒ **better 6/6, mean $\Delta$ 0.011**; ACC **better 6/6, mean $\Delta$ 0.013** ⇒ ***the physics costs ≈1 pp on both axes***. Corroborated at a different φ (CIFAR: same-keys 0.325 vs ring 0.350) |
| ⭐ *"the ambiguity clue"* | in the one regime with **corrupted queries and no constructible oracle**, the settle **ties** the matched-compute floor (+0.8 ± 1.6 pp, 6 seeds — §11.13, **N103**), and it beats every mechanism control there (+4.6 ± 2.2 pp over an equal-energy kick, 6/6) |
| ⭐ *"and the store fills up in a way the capacity law predicts"* | at 314 000 floats the store holds **6 085 ± 66 of 7 658** allowed wells (**fill 0.795**), admitted fraction stable at **0.57–0.62**, **`refused_full = 0` everywhere** ⇒ the binding constraint switches from **budget** to **spacing gate**: *the packing law appearing inside a benchmark*. ⚠ Registered prediction P3(b) **falsified** — forgetting did not flatten there (0.076 → 0.049) |

⛔ **The boundary.** **One dataset, one task family, 3 seeds.** The task is **clean-query classification** — the query is a *different image*, not a corrupted copy of a stored one — which is precisely the regime where "nearest centre" is unambiguous and a settle can only lose time. The dominance-over-replay half is **partly by construction** (replay stores 785 floats/item *by definition*) ⇒ **supplementary, never primary** — and the strict pre-registered rule (sd-separated **and** LA-in-band) leaves **one** dominance point, with a disclosed knife-edge (76.7 KiB fails the LA clause by **0.007**). ⚠ **Two corrections travel with any quotation of the w25 table: "24.5× fewer floats" → 19.1×**, and **iCaRL ran up to 2.5× over its item budget** at small budgets. ⚠ **LwF is 4.3 pp low as a cross-entropy-scope convention, not a tuning failure** (flat over a 200× α sweep; current-task CE gives 0.556 — *neither convention reproduces the published 23.9, and ours is the one under which EWC/SI/finetune reproduce to ≤0.5 pp*).

**→ CLU, said plainly.** **The program's own control, run properly, says the dynamics are a net negative on the benchmark where everything else about the system is held fixed.** That is not a reason to stop; it is the sharpest possible statement of where to look. **It converts a diffuse question — *"is the physics worth it?"* — into a single, testable one: *is there any read for which the settle beats arg-min over the same wells?*** Six budget points × three seeds say **no** on clean classification; the corrupted-query cell says **it ties**. ⭐ **A negative this specific is worth more than a win this vague, and it belongs in the paper in our own words rather than a referee's.**

**Status: [measured — 6 byte budgets × 3 seeds, accounting pinned in a PREREG before any run and machine-checked by a test, every number re-derived from shipped JSON] · [the pre-registered falsifier FIRED — filed SUPPLEMENTARY, and the Head's standing ruling makes win-by-construction supplementary anyway] · [one dataset; the corrupted-query contrast is 6 seeds on a different harness] · [this is a NEGATIVE record — nothing in it licenses a performance claim].** **Reading pointers:** §11.11 (the laundering control's definition and the first four firings), §11.13 (the corrupted-query tie), §11.12 (the admission gate whose saturation shows up here), `matched-bytes-frontier` §1–§5, registry **N113 / N114 / N115 / N116** and the w26 block on **N101**.

## 11.17 One picture from C1W27: **the wall that moved on the other side of the system** **[plain-language record — new C1W27, filed 2026-07-31]**

**Why this section exists.** C1's final wave produced exactly one thing that needs a picture, and it is a *mechanism*, not a number: **a change made entirely on the read-out side moved the write.** Everything else C1W27 produced is a closure (R2's bracket, R1's scope, Candidate 3's death) and lives in the registry (**N123–N132**) and the ledger's ⟲ C1W27 addendum. ⚠ **As in §11.14–§11.16, the imagery describes mechanisms, not wins.**

### Record 9 — **The wall that moved on the other side of the system** (why a read-side change can break the write)

> **The wall that moved on the other side.** Record 6 described two ways to fix a reader that cannot see far enough: **put the answers nearer the launch line** (write the value as a short address across several channels) or **lend the reader a torch and take it back** (blur only the payload direction while the address is being chosen, then sharpen). At $d=4$ both worked. C1W27 carried both up the dimension ladder — and the torch kept working (it reaches the *designed* wall at $d=6$ and $d=8$) while **the multi-channel code stopped working and then actively broke things.** Here is why, and it is not a reader story at all. **The write had been using the long payload excursion as a *ruler*.** Every item's value had to travel a long way from the launch line, and keeping items distinguishable along that long line forced the write to carve **narrow** valleys. Shorten the excursion by a factor of ten and that pressure disappears: the write, now unconstrained in the direction it used to be squeezed, **widens the valleys 2.3–2.7×**. At $d=4$ there is room. At $d=6$, twice the well width exceeds the distance between neighbouring sites, **the valleys merge, and the ADDRESS collapses** — the reader can now see everything and tell nothing apart. **A read-side change broke the write, and the laundering control could not see it, because the designed arm has no write to break.**

**The same thing in numbers** (`r2-d-sweep-close`; **N123/N124**; the interaction re-derived by the Hub from the paired seeds):

| the clause | the evidence | verdict |
|---|---|---|
| *"the torch keeps working up the ladder"* | $K_{\rm learned}(6)$: **32 → 128**, **pinned** — bracketed above by a measured **$K=256$ FAIL at 0.7358** against a 0.9 bar and a designed arm at 1.0000. $K_{\rm learned}(8)$: **32 → ≥128**. Carried by **arm (b) alone — 0 extra bytes, dims, parameters or Verlet steps** | ✅ measured, 3 seeds at the decisive cells |
| *"the designed law is exact"* | $K_{\rm designed}(d) = 4\cdot 2^d$ re-measured **exactly at $d=4,6,8$** (64 / 256 / 1024; $\log_2 K = d+2$), **zero free parameters**, one metric | ✅ measured |
| ⚠ *"…but the top of the ladder is a lower bound"* | **$d=8$: $K=1024$ passes at 0.9785 and $K=2048$ was NEVER RUN** ⇒ $K_{\rm designed}(8)$ is a **lower bound that coincides with** $4\cdot2^8$. $d=4$ and $d=6$ **are** bracketed ($K{=}256\to0.0967$; $K{=}512\to0.6504$) | ⚠ **approved wording: "bracketed at $d=4$ and $d=6$, LOWER-BOUNDED at $d=8$" — NOT RULED** |
| ⛔ *"the code is a $d=4$-only win"* | $d{=}6$, $K{=}128$ with $m{=}4$: **strict 0.0906**, basin 0.4448 | ✅ refuted at $d\ge6$ |
| ⭐ *"and here is why — on the write side"* | strip the excursion ⇒ atoms widen **2.3–2.7×**; $w_{\rm atom} = \mathbf{0.6491}$ against site separation **0.666**, so $2w_{\rm atom} > \mathrm{sep}$ ⇒ wells merge ⇒ **address collapse** | ✅ the mechanism, measured |
| ⭐ *"the two arms are substitutes, not complements"* | $I = -0.1509 / -0.1514 / -0.1359$ at $\sigma = 0/.005/.010$, paired SE **0.0083** ⇒ **18.3 SE**; arm (b) buys **+0.1507 on $m{=}1$** and **−0.0002 on $m{=}4$** ⇒ **the combination is strictly dominated by arm (b) alone** | ✅ measured; confirms `readout-channel-theory` §4.2 to the digit |
| *"and it is not budget starvation"* | 2× atoms at $d{=}6$, $K{=}128$: **0.8239 vs 0.9127** — the budget response is **negative** | ✅ N92 protocol |

⛔ **The boundary, and the thing this record was written to prevent someone claiming.** ⛔ **The wall moving is NOT a capacity-per-byte result.** With byte accounting pinned, the learned store reaches **0.0243 bits/param** at its best cell — **~47× below the designed construction (1.14)** and **~82× below a transformer's 2**. *Moving the wall 4× moved only the numerator.* ⭐ The quotable, **non-comparative** residue is *"items per learned float ×4.00 at literally zero extra bytes, at both $d=6$ and $d=8$."* ⛔ And **w21's "~1.3 vs 2" is the DESIGNED construction's number** — never the learned store's, and never "stale".

**→ CLU, said plainly.** **The store's read-out interface and its write objective are coupled through the geometry, and the coupling runs backwards from where you would look for it.** A read-side parameter that appears to cost nothing (the excursion the value has to travel) was silently supplying the pressure that kept the write's wells narrow enough to be addressable. ⭐ **The generalisable rule: before changing a read-side quantity, ask what the write was using it for.** This is the same shape as the C2W2 finding one wave later (§11.18 Record 11): **a designed quantity is only in force if some term in the objective reads it.**

**Status: [measured — 3 seeds at the decisive cells; both "killed after seed 0" cells read from stdout logs by the Hub and matching the report exactly, so no number is quoted from a file that does not exist] · [$d=8$'s designed point is a LOWER BOUND, and the wording amendment is NOT RULED] · [a law about the primitive — R2 exemption, CM-23(m): its figure is never framed as beating anything].** **Reading pointers:** §11.16 Record 6 (the two interventions at $d=4$), Record 7 (the saddle criterion, causally tested at C1W27 — **N130**), registry **N123 / N124 / N125 / N126**, ledger ⟲ C1W27.

## 11.18 The Campaign-2 physics: **trainability, superposition, and the two theorems C2 keeps** **[new C2W1 + C2W2, filed 2026-07-31]**

**Why this section exists.** Campaign 2 assembled the whole CLU, ran it, and measured its dividend at zero on every family it looked at (registry **N133–N166**; ledger ⟲ C2W1 and ⟲ C2W2). ⭐ **But the two waves also produced the first genuinely new *physics* in this file since wave-13** — a trainability theorem with two sides, a crossing taxonomy, a Liouville argument that makes a null informative, and a measured statement about what happens to designed symmetry under superposition. This section is that physics. ⚠ **Nothing here licenses a performance claim; the dividend at v0 is zero or negative on all four measured families.**

### Record 10 — **The one-way mirror** (a settled point is blind, in both directions)

> **The one-way mirror.** Think of the read as dropping a marble into a landscape and reading off where it stops. Now ask the two questions a *learning* system must ask. **"If I nudge where I dropped it, does the answer move?"** At a settled point: **no** — the marble forgets its launch entirely, which is the whole point of settling. **"If I make the marble heavier, or the air stickier, does the answer move?"** Also **no** — where a marble *stops* depends only on the shape of the ground, not on its mass or the drag. Both are features of a settled read, and both are catastrophic for a system that wants to *learn* the launch or *use* the marble's properties as part of the address: **there is nothing for gradient descent to push on.** The mirror is one-way. Watch the marble *while it rolls*, however, and both channels open at once: the path depends on where you dropped it **and** on how heavy and sticky the marble is.

**The same thing in symbols.** For an implicit settle $q^\*$ satisfying $\nabla V(q^\*) = 0$, and a read $\psi(q^\*)$:
$$\frac{\partial q^\*}{\partial q_0} = 0 \quad\text{(the fixed point has no memory of the launch)}\qquad\text{and}\qquad \nabla V \ \text{contains neither } M \ \text{nor}\ \gamma \ \Rightarrow\ \frac{\partial q^\*}{\partial M} = \frac{\partial q^\*}{\partial\gamma} = 0 \ \ \text{(Prop Q1.1)}.$$
Both are **exact**, not small. Under a trajectory read $\psi(\{q_t\}_{t})$ neither holds: $q_t$ depends on $q_0$, and $\ddot q = -M^{-1}\nabla V - \gamma\dot q$ carries $M$ and $\gamma$ explicitly.

**Every clause, with its measurement** (`trainability-spike` §3.1 + `trainability-spike-theory` Q1 → **N135**; `phi-particle-head` §2 → **N152**; both Hub-re-derived):

| the clause | the evidence | verdict |
|---|---|---|
| ⭐⭐ *"no gradient reaches the read-in"* | $\lVert\partial L/\partial\varphi\rVert$ = **exactly 0.0** (implicit) / **2.654e-9** (unrolled) / **6.421e-3** (trajectory) ⇒ ratio **2.42e6** | ✅ measured; the exact form of **N61**, and it retro-explains w19's learned-addressing death |
| ⭐⭐ *"and none reaches the particle"* | per-query **mass**: trajectory **1.744e-3 / 1.174e-2 / 1.880e-3** vs point **8.729e-9 / 3.979e-8 / 1.089e-8** (unroll) and **exactly 0.0, BITWISE** (implicit) ⇒ **1.7e5–2.9e5×**. Per-query **friction**: **2.4e-2 / 4.1e-2 / 3.3e-2** ⇒ **2.6e5–4.9e5×**. **3/3 seeds**, cross-checked vs float64 finite differences | ✅ measured — **charter §A2.2 SUPPORTED, the first measurement in 29 waves** |
| ⭐ *"the zero is structural, not lucky"* | **Prop Q1.1**: $\nabla V$ contains neither $M$ nor $\gamma$; the implicit arm's mass gradient is **bitwise zero**, not merely small | ✅ proven + verified |
| ⭐ *"friction is the stronger channel"* | friction ≈ **14×** the mass channel ⇒ *if you take one particle attribute, take friction*. ⚠ its declared band's low end is **monitor-#1 hot** ($\gamma \le 0.03$ trips on S0) | ✅ measured, with a hot edge |
| *"and the price of looking at the path"* | trajectory-$\psi$ step cost **8.5496 s vs 0.5002 s = 17.09×**; accepted for the first paper (§A4.4), and **it travels with every trajectory claim** | ✅ measured |
| ⛔ *"but opening the channel is not the same as using it"* | pillar 1's Stage-0 liveness gate: **0 qualifying cells of 588 × 3 seeds**, $\gamma_{\rm read}\in[0,0.2]$, strides 1–32 — and the trajectory *write* that was supposed to fix it is a **monotone cost** (**N136 / N148**) | ⛔ the honest other half |

⭐⭐ **The joint statement, and this is how it must be quoted** (charter §A11 task 5 frames it exactly this way): ***a settled-point read is untrainable end-to-end, IN BOTH DIRECTIONS.*** It is a **benchmark-free** structural argument — it needs no task, no dataset and no baseline — which is why it is a **B′ theorem** rather than an experimental result.

⛔ **The boundary.** This says a trajectory read is **necessary** for a learned read. It does **not** say a trajectory read is **useful**: C2W1 measured 0/1764 on liveness, and C2W2 measured the trajectory *write* as a monotone cost at `rollout_steps = 60`. **Necessary ≠ sufficient, and the file must never blur the two.**

**Status: [proven (both propositions) + measured (3 seeds, float64-cross-checked, bitwise zero on the implicit arm)] · [gradient-flow only — NO task metric; nothing here is a dividend] · [Prop F1's "mass SELECTS, never stores" (N76, 3× confirmed) is UNTOUCHED — this is a trainability result, not a storage result].** **Reading pointers:** §2.1 (inertial $M$), §2.3 (spectral $\mu$ — Def-2 discipline: this record is entirely about **inertial $M$**), §11.4 (Props 4/5, the gradient dichotomy this completes), registry **N135 / N152 / N136 / N148**.

> ### ⭐⭐ **Update (C2W3, filed 2026-07-31) — the mirror is now a THEOREM with a finite-budget law, and it retro-explains a two-year-old death with no fitted parameters.**
> **What is new is not the zeros — it is *why* they are the same zero, and what replaces them at finite compute.** Write the shipped damped-Verlet step as a map $T_\theta$. Then
> $$\mathrm{Fix}(T_\theta) \;=\; \{(q,0) \;:\; \nabla V_\theta(q) = 0\}.$$
> ⭐ **Read the right-hand side: it contains $\theta$ and nothing else.** Not $q_0$, not $p_0$, not the inertial mass $M$, not the friction $\gamma$, not $dt$, not which integrator you chose. So the read's parameters **split into two classes by the fixed-point equation itself** — *fixed-point* parameters (the store) and **transient** parameters (everything about the journey) — and every transient parameter has $\partial z^*/\partial\zeta \equiv 0$ **exactly**. **The two zeros this record reports are not two facts; they are one theorem evaluated at two arguments.**
> ⭐ **And at a finite step budget the zero becomes a rate, which is the practically useful half:**
> $$\lVert \partial z_N/\partial\zeta \rVert \;\asymp\; K_\zeta\, e^{-C}, \qquad C \;\equiv\; \sum_p N_p \ln(1/\rho_p),$$
> with $\rho_p$ the spectral radius of the exact $2\times2$ damped-Verlet propagator in phase $p$, $K_\zeta = O(1)$ for $q_0$ (a pure Jacobian product) and $K_\zeta = O(N)$ for $\{M,\gamma\}$ (injected at every step). **Measured:** slope of $\log_{10}|dq|$ against $C/\ln 10$ = **−0.9941 over 143.9 decades** (per-γ **−0.981 / −1.007**); the $\{M,\gamma\}$ law is exact to **±1 %** once the derived $N$ prefactor is divided out. For the shipped two-phase read, $\rho(0.05) = 0.974679$ and $\rho(0.02) = 0.989949$ ⇒ **$C = 18.34$, $e^{-C} = 1.084\times10^{-8}$** — which **brackets the harness's own measured $2.654\times10^{-9}$ (φ) and $8.73\times10^{-9}$ (mass).**
> ⭐⭐ **The retro-explanation, and it is the sharpest thing in the file.** Wave-19 killed learned addressing (**4.2 %, 1/24**; the damped arm's loss **frozen at 7.9035443 to seven significant digits across 4000 GD steps**). Every mode there is underdamped, so $\rho = \sqrt{1-\gamma}$ **exactly and landscape-independently** — the prediction has **no free parameter but the $\gamma=0$ anchor**. Against C1's γ-scan: measured $\lVert\nabla_{\rm address}\rVert$ **3.3e-1 / 9.1e-2 / 1.9e-2 / 1.7e-3 / 1.6e-5** vs predicted $3.3\times10^{-1}(1-\gamma)^{600}$ = **3.3e-1 / 9.9e-2 / 1.6e-2 / 7.9e-4 / 1.8e-6** ⇒ ratios **1.00 / 0.92 / 1.17 / 2.14 / 8.9** — ⭐ **five decades reproduced to within 0.92–8.9×, by a law written years after the measurement**, and the two remaining γ points are **non-monotone** (5.0e-8 → 1.2e-7), i.e. that instrument's own floor. ⭐ At C1's 3000-step probe, $C = 76.9 \Rightarrow |\partial q_N/\partial q_0| \sim 10^{-33.4}$ — **26 orders below float32 $\varepsilon$. Gradient descent was optimising round-off.**
> ⛔ **The consequence you must carry, because it reframes four waves of failures:** ***every read of record has been a settled-point read, so $\varphi$ has never received a gradient larger than $e^{-C}$. The "weak encoder" failures were not encoder-capacity failures — they were $C \approx 18$ failures.*** And ⛔ **the retrieval/learnability trade is DEFINITIONAL, not tunable**: retrieval robustness *is* $\partial(\text{final})/\partial q_0 \to 0$, and $C$ is the single knob setting both. **The only escape is to stop reading a fixed point** — a trajectory read has $\rho = 1$ on its $\gamma = 0$ leg, and $C$ is never spent.
> ⚠ **Two honest riders.** **(i)** $q^*(q_0)$ is *piecewise constant* — the gradient is 0 **a.e.**, and on the null set (the separatrices) it is a distribution, not a descent direction; this is C1's measured "staircase with a 1.7e7 cliff ratio", derived. **(ii)** ⚠ **The $\{M,\gamma\}$ prefactor is DECLARED NOT DERIVED**: the harness's point-arm mass gradient sits at $\approx e^{-C}$, i.e. $N \approx 1200\times$ *below* the toy's $Ne^{-C}$ law, while $\lVert\partial L/\partial\varphi\rVert$ matches the $O(1)$ law. **The structural claim is unaffected; the prefactor is open** (OQ-1, a ten-minute one-flag check). ⚠ **12 of 25 $\{M,\gamma\}$ cells sat on a float64 roundoff floor at ~1e-16 and were excluded before fitting — reporting them as physics would have refuted a correct law.**
> **Status: [PROVEN (the fixed-point split) + measured (−1.00 ± 0.01 over 143.9 decades; $C = 18.34$ brackets both harness gradients) + RETRODICTIVE (five decades, zero fitted parameters)] · [the $\{M,\gamma\}$ prefactor NOT DERIVED] · [benchmark-free — it licenses no performance claim].** **Sources:** `bprime-theory` T3 / T3.1 / T3.2 / §9; registry **N179**, **N181**.

### Record 11 — **The rim that isn't there** (superposition destroys a designed degeneracy — and the objective never notices)

> **The rim that isn't there.** §4.5 tilts a Mexican hat: one rim, one flat direction, a small tilt that gives it a small mass. Now build a *store*. A written site is not one hat — it is **about thirty-two hats piled on top of each other at learned centres**, and the flat direction you designed is flat only if **every** hat is flat along it. Measure how far from flat the pile actually is at a written site, and compare against a control that is *deliberately arbitrary*: pick the tilt direction at random. **The written site scores at or worse than the random direction.** The rim you designed is not the rim the store built — so tilting "the rim" tilts something that is not a symmetry direction at all, and it makes the site *less* stable rather than more. ⭐ **And then the quieter half.** The shell's radius was a designed parameter, learnable, correctly initialised. After 300 steps of writing it had moved from **0.500 to 0.501**. It was not erased. It was not used. **No term in the write objective reads it** — so it simply sat there. w20 taught us that free learning **erases** design. This is worse and quieter: **design the objective cannot SEE is neither erased nor used.**

**The same thing in symbols.** With per-group atoms $j$ of weight $w_j$ and unit shell normals $\hat n_j$ at a written site, a tilt direction $\hat u$ is a common vacuum iff $\hat u \perp \hat n_j\ \forall j$. Define
$$\texttt{tilt\_vacuum\_residual} \;\equiv\; \sum_j w_j\,(\hat u\cdot\hat n_j)^2, \qquad \text{ideal } 0, \qquad \text{random-orientation baseline } \tfrac{1}{\mathrm{dim}}.$$

**Measured** (`ssb-shell-atoms` → **N149 / N150 / N151**; two independent implementations, every family):

| the clause | the evidence | verdict |
|---|---|---|
| ⛔⛔ *"the tilt has the wrong sign"* | $\lambda_{\min}$: **+0.0994 → −8.28** (envelope), **+0.3291 → −1.198** (depth), **monotone**, two implementations, every family | ⛔ **charter §A4.2 REFUTED IN SIGN** |
| ⭐ *"and here is why — the vacuum isn't common"* | residual **0.140** (envelope) / **0.343** (depth), 0.197–0.339 across race arms, vs ideal **0** and random baseline $1/\mathrm{dim} = \mathbf{0.167}$; atom-centre spread **1.19–1.95×** the designed shell radius | ✅ **the mechanism, measured — at or WORSE than random** |
| ⭐ *"the geometry itself is fine"* | $\lambda = \varepsilon$ **unit-tested to 5 %** in the single-atom geometry it was specified in | ✅ **the ruling survives as geometry** |
| ⛔ *"and $\varepsilon$ is not the lifetime dial"* | $\tau = 2/\Gamma = 5.0$ **CONFIRMED (4.23–6.58)**, above-knee slope **0** confirmed — but $\lambda_{\rm soft}$ is floored at **$2\alpha$, 2.5× above the knee** ⇒ $\tau_{\max} = \Gamma/2\alpha = \mathbf{4.0}$ | ⛔ **$\alpha$ is the ceiling; lowering $\alpha$ breaks the write** |
| ⭐ *"and the floor is derived, then measured, from two independent sides"* | $\lambda_{\rm tan} = 2\alpha\lVert c\rVert/\rho \approx 2\alpha = 0.100$ **predicted before running, measured 0.0994 (0.6 %)** — reproducing `memory-gym-v0`'s $\lambda_{\min}\approx 2\alpha$ at **14/18** sites from the *basis* side | ✅ **two-sided confirmation** |
| ⚠ *"the write is blind to the design"* | learned shell radius $r$: **0.500 → 0.501** in 300 steps | ⚠ **a third failure mode, new to the taxonomy** |
| ✅ *"and the reduction gate is exact"* | $r=0$: bit-identical $V$, $\nabla V$, $\mathrm{Hess}\,V$ in **f32 and f64**, including at $q = c$; **12/12** system cells bit-identical on both `full` and `settle_deleted_launder` | ✅ the regression gate that makes the refutation trustworthy |
| ⚠ *"and a richer basis costs bytes"* | shell surcharge **52.00 → 58.40×** at $\mathrm{dim}=6$ = **+12.5 %** on the atom term, exactly $1/(\mathrm{dim}+2)$ | ⚠ **a basis change RAISES the architectural byte floor** |

⭐ **The failure-mode taxonomy, now three-deep — this is the part to carry:** **(1) w20 — free learning ERASES design** (the designed structure is a saddle point of the objective and the optimiser walks off it). **(2) C2W2 — superposition DESTROYS a designed degeneracy** (the structure survives per-atom and dies in the sum; measurable as a vacuum residual against a random-orientation baseline). **(3) C2W2 — the objective is BLIND to design it does not read** (the parameter is trainable, reachable and simply never moves). ⭐ **Mode (3) has a cheap standing test and it should be doctrine: before claiming a designed structure is in force on a learned store, measure whether some term in the loss reads it. A parameter the loss does not read is decoration.**

**Status: [refuted in sign — two independent implementations, every family, with a measured cause] · [the geometry survives, unit-tested to 5 % in the single-atom case] · [$\tau$ floor CONFIRMED; the $1/\varepsilon$ branch UNREACHABLE at $\tau_{\max}=\Gamma/2\alpha$] · [PREREG scoreboard: 14 confirmed (5 exact) · 6 partial · 8 refuted — every confirmation on a quantity derived from shipped constants, every refutation on something the charter hoped *learning* would do].** **Reading pointers:** §4.5 (with its C2W2 update block), §4.3 (SSB), §11.9 (the write operator), registry **N149 / N150 / N151 / N159**, charter §A4.2 (struck in place) and §A9.10.

### Record 12 — **Two ways a memory dies** (the crossing taxonomy, and why $\gamma=0$ makes a null informative)

> **Two ways a memory dies.** Push two wells together and they merge; let one well fade and it vanishes. Both are "the item is gone", and they are **different bifurcations with different fingerprints** — and the fingerprint is an *exponent* you can measure. When two **symmetric** wells merge, the barrier between them flattens as the **first** power of the distance to the critical point: a **pitchfork**. When a single well **loses** to its surroundings — the payload fades, or the spurious minimum on the read-out ray annihilates against the saddle — the gap closes as the **square root**: a **saddle-node**. ⭐ **So the exponent tells you which death you are watching**, and that is more useful than the prediction it replaced (which was 0.5 for both, and wrong for the merger). **And there is a third fact that decides whether a *null* means anything.** Set the friction to exactly zero and the dynamics become symplectic: phase-space volume is exactly preserved, and **Liouville forbids any attractor at all.** There is then **no settled point** — the trajectory is *all there is*. So if a trajectory read carries information a point read cannot, $\gamma=0$ is the regime where it must show. **It did not move.** That is why C2W1's 0-of-1764 is a *finding* and not merely a failed sweep.

**The same thing in symbols** (`trainability-spike-theory` Q1.1b / Q1.4; **N136**, **N129**, **N144**):

$$\det\!\big(\partial T/\partial z\big) = (1-\gamma)^d \ \ \text{exactly} \quad\Longrightarrow\quad \gamma = 0:\ \det = 1,\ \text{symplectic},\ \textbf{no attractor exists.}$$

| collapse mode | bifurcation | predicted scaling | **measured exponent** |
|---|---|---|---|
| symmetric two-well **merger** (permitted basin interaction) | **pitchfork** | $\propto (d - d_{\rm crit})^1$ | **1.03** (49 pts) |
| asymmetric **well loss** (decay to death; the reach saddle's annihilation) | **saddle-node** | $\propto \Delta^{1/2}$ | **0.49–0.50** (local, reach) · **0.573** (decay, grid-limited) · root-gap **0.505** |

⚠ **The theorist's own registered prediction P12 (0.5 for the merger) is FALSIFIED** — a *symmetric* merger is a pitchfork, not a saddle-node — **and the corrected classification is more useful than the prediction was.** ⭐ Two companions worth carrying: the **conditioning blow-up as an item dies** ($A_1 = 1.0 \to \lambda_{\min}\,8.244$, $\lVert H^{-1}g\rVert\,0.0093$; $0.05 \to 0.299,\ \mathbf{4.33}$ — a **465×** amplification; $A_{\rm crit}$ **measured 0.04** against the formula $2\alpha|c|s\sqrt{e} = 0.0444$, **10 % agreement**; at 0.03 the item is **gone** — the settle lands 1.53 away, in the neighbour's well, and $\lambda_{\min}$ reads the *neighbour's* 8.25); and the **$\gamma=0$ oscillatory payload channel** from C1W27, $T = 2\pi/\sqrt{\kappa(g_0+A)}$, slope **−0.5264** (**N129**) — *the same "no attractor at $\gamma=0$" fact, seen on the payload axis.*

**⭐ And the resolution that makes the singular read legal at all — project-and-transport.** A coset read has a genuinely singular implicit system (the Hessian is degenerate along the orbit). The **Ward identity** $\langle \partial_\theta \nabla V, \xi\rangle = 0$ — **verified to 1.35e-16** — says the singular system is nonetheless **consistent**, so the pseudo-inverse $H^{+}$ returns the correct *transverse* answer with *transverse* conditioning; the orbit coordinate is then **transported** rather than solved, by the exact law $L_n = (1-\gamma)^n L_0$ (**6.1e-14**, exact relativistically too), giving $\theta_\infty = \theta_0 + dt\,L_0/(m r_*^2\gamma)$ to **0.02–0.75 %**. ⭐ **The consequence is a design statement: manifold-valued content rides $M^{-1}p_0$, and `V_θ` reaches it only through the orbit radius $r_*$.** ⭐ It also **explains N5 as a corollary** rather than as an accident.

⛔ **The boundary, and it is the honest half.** The gradient machinery met **every** registered bar (implicit vs re-settled FD **5.11e-10**; truncation $=\rho^k$ ±5 % at $k=180/270/449$; ridge bias **4.134 %** at the predicted 0.35424) — **and two of the theory's own recipes broke in production**: **`k* = 269` governs an ENDPOINT loss, not a trajectory loss** (for $L(\psi(\mathrm{traj}))$ the error at $k=270$ is **0.680** and *flat in $k$*), and **the theory's truncation recipe makes $\varphi$ untrainable** ($\lVert\partial L/\partial\varphi\rVert$ **exactly 0.0** at $k = 0\ldots270$) — *truncation **direction** is load-bearing and the theory did not name it.* ⚠ **C2W2 sharpens this into a rule: truncated BPTT is PARAMETER-SELECTIVE** — $k^*$ governs $\partial q_N/\partial\theta$ **only where fixed-point sensitivity dominates the transient** (ratio 64× ⇒ holds; **27 396× ⇒ error 0.448, flat in $k$**) — and **in a $K$-item store the far-well parameters are exactly the interference gradients.** ⛔ **Never quote $k^*$ without that qualifier.**

**Status: [proven (Liouville, the Ward identity, the codim-1 crossing argument) + measured (exponents, the 465× conditioning ramp, 1.35e-16 and 6.1e-14 verifications)] · [P12 self-falsified and corrected — recorded as a finding] · [two theory recipes broke in production (R-1, R-2) and both are scoped, not retracted].** **Reading pointers:** §1.4–§1.5 (volume and symplecticity — the Liouville argument's home), §3.4 (expanding modes), §4.9 (modulus vs Goldstone — the orbit coordinate this transports), §11.4, registry **N129 / N136 / N144**.

### Record 13 — **The floor you cannot argue with** (the byte-floor theorem, and why it is a theorem and not a caveat)

> **The floor you cannot argue with.** Every dividend claim in this program is a comparison at *matched bytes* — the store against a plain table holding the same number of bytes. For four waves the mismatch was written down as a caveat: *"the store currently uses more bytes; we will close that."* C2W1 did the arithmetic and found that **it cannot be closed** — not by tuning, not by a bigger run, not by a better optimiser. Each item owns a **private group of atoms**, and private parameters **cannot superpose**: two items can never share a coefficient. Count the floats that implies and the ratio comes out as a **formula with no room in it** — and, tellingly, **it does not depend on how many items you store at all.** ⭐ And the same masking that forces this floor is exactly the property that buys the store its clean write locality. **You are not paying for a mistake; you are paying for the thing that works.** ⚠ Then C2W2 made it worse in a way worth knowing: switching to a **richer atom basis** — shells instead of blobs — **raises** the floor by a fixed, predictable amount. *A representational upgrade costs architectural bytes.*

**The same thing in symbols** (`memory-gym-v0` §3.1 → **N134**; `ssb-shell-atoms` → **N159**):
$$\mathrm{ratio} \;=\; \frac{\texttt{atoms\_per\_item}\,(\mathrm{dim}+2)}{\mathrm{dim}} \;+\; \frac{d}{\mathrm{dim}} \;=\; \mathbf{1.4\,A + 0.8}, \qquad \textbf{independent of } K,$$
verified to **1e-9 in all 28 cells**, with `matched = False` on every cell and **0 violations** ⇒ $\mathrm{ratio} \ge \mathbf{2.20\times}$, **measured minimum 2.28×**. A shell basis adds exactly $\tfrac{1}{\mathrm{dim}+2}$: **52.00 → 58.40× at $\mathrm{dim}=6$, +12.5 %**. **C2W2's minimum ratio anywhere is 17.11×.** At 20 B/row a byte-matched table holds **≥11× the CLU's items at every reachable config**.

> ### ⚠⚠ **Update (C2W3, filed 2026-07-31) — the closed form above is WRONG on 4 of 28 cells. The theorem is not.**
> ⛔ **The sentence *"verified to 1e-9 in all 28 cells"* and the form $1.4A + 0.8$ are superseded and must not be quoted.** The published law reproduces the measured ledger in **24 of 28** cells. The four `manifold` cells — the only ones with a **spectator dimension** ($n_{\rm spec} = 1$) — measure **52.00×** against a published **43.33×** (**+8.6667, +20 %**), and the printed floor there (**2.00×**) is wrong: the true floor **RISES to 2.40×**.
> ⭐ **The corrected law is exact in ALL 28 cells in rational arithmetic, 0 ulp:**
> $$\mathrm{ratio} \;=\; \frac{A\,(D+2) \;+\; d}{d+m}, \qquad A \equiv \frac{N_{\rm at}}{K}, \quad D = d + m + n_{\rm spec}.$$
> **The bug, in one line.** `byte_ratio_law` divides by the **store** dimension $D$ where the launder's row is $(d+m)$ floats — so it is **invisible whenever $n_{\rm spec} = 0$**, which is the only geometry the unit test covers. ⭐ *A formula that is right on every case you test is not a verified formula; it is an untested one.* **That is the transferable lesson of this erratum, and it is worth more than the correction.**
> ⭐⭐ **Why the record survives, and in which direction.** The agreement was never a fit — it is an **accounting identity over the parameter leaves** — so the falsifier fired on the **published wording**, not on the theorem. **And the error is CONSERVATIVE: the store costs *more* relative to the table than we published, so no claim of ours was inflated.** The $n_{\rm spec} = 0$ floor **2.20×** and the measured minimum **2.28×** are **unchanged**; shell floors are **2.40 / 2.60×**; the shell surcharge $+1/(D+2) = +12.5\%$ is **exactly** reproduced by the corrected law. ⇒ **`PREREG-Bprime.md` §7's reuse licence STANDS, and `bprime-rivals` does NOT re-measure.**
> ⭐ **And the theorem gained a companion worth reading with it (N177): `2.20×` is exactly the byte price of ONE privately-deletable atom per item**, matched bytes needs **$S^* = (D+2)A_{\rm tot}/m$** items per shared atom (**7** at $A_{\rm tot}=1$, **2387** at the shipped anchor), and at matched bytes at most **0.042 %** of an item's parameter mass can stay byte-exactly deletable. ⇒ ***compression and byte-exact deletion are the same trade, with that exchange rate.***
> **Source:** `bprime-theory` T1.1/T1.2/T1.3/T1.4 (exact integer/rational arithmetic, 28 C2W1 cells); Hub re-derivation in the `[C2W3]` §10 entry. **Registry N167 / N177**, and **N134**'s correction block. **Correction of record:** `.claude/outputs/track2-admissibility/ERRATA-Bprime.md` §E1 — ⛔ **the prereg itself is deliberately NOT edited.** ⚠ The **code** fix is `harness-debt`'s (C2W4): **their landed numbers win over this block if the two ever differ.**

> ### ✅ **Update (C2W4, 2026-08-01) — the correction is now IN THE CODE, and the law decided an architecture question the same wave**
> **The fix landed and the landed numbers agree with the block above, cell for cell** (`harness-debt` §1/§1.2, commit `8e122bf`): the corrected law is **exact in 28/28** cells in rational arithmetic (0 ulp), the shipped one in **24/28**, the 24 `n_{\rm spec} = 0` cells are **bitwise unchanged** (`float.hex()` equality), the four `manifold` cells move by **exactly +8.6666666667** (43.3333 → 52.0000), the printed floors go **{2.00, 2.20} → {2.20, 2.40}**, and the **measured minimum 2.2824× is unchanged.** ⭐ **The `floor_note` needed no edit at all — it is *derived* by calling the law, so it corrected itself.**
> ⭐⭐ **The lesson that outlives the formula, and it is a physicist's lesson about instruments:** ***a closed form that is right on every case you test is untested, not verified.*** The bug was invisible whenever $n_{\rm spec} = 0$ — **every geometry any test instantiated** — and it survived three waves of a fully green suite. **The fix is one `@pytest.mark.parametrize` plus adding the one family that has a spectator dimension** (**N202**).
> ⭐ **And the law's first ARCHITECTURAL consequence was measured this wave, inside a real block (N198).** At the store's own state bytes, with $A = 128, D = 3, d = 2, m = 1$: $[A(D{+}2)+d]/(d{+}m) = (128\cdot 5 + 2)/3 = \mathbf{214.00\times}$ per item ⇒ **a plain table gets 1 714 rows where the store gets 8 items.** (Pre-registered pilot-geometry value **299.33×**, 9 578 rows vs 32 items.) ⚠ **We sit 89× above the law's own floor because $A = 128$** — *the floor is a theorem about the architecture; 214× is what our configuration actually pays.*
> ⚠ **One live residue:** the C2W1 artifact **on disk still prints the old ratios and floors** for those four cells and was **deliberately not rewritten** (*re-writing a published artifact is the silent re-score the fix exists to forbid*) ⇒ **any consumer applies the corrected law.** **Registry:** N167's discharge block, **N198**, **N202**.

⛔ **Consequences, all of them binding.** **(1)** ⛔ **No gym / C2W2 / C2W3 cell may be quoted as a byte-matched dividend** — a `matched = False` cell may be *reported and labelled*, never quoted. **(2)** ⛔ **The Hub's own prescribed route — *"load the store beyond the launder's table"* — is RETIRED**; it cannot happen (Hub accepted: *"the wording was mine"*). **(3)** ⛔ **Pillar (a), beyond-capacity compression, is structurally excluded** at this architecture (§A2.3) — and, independently, **has no admissible public venue at this weight class** (**N161**). **(4)** ⭐ **The only thing that could dissolve it is a shared/factored substrate**, which is **deferred, not closed**, and any revival inherits **§A9.9**: **deletion is then measured as a CURVE** — exactness preserved on the private-atom fraction, measured degradation on the shared fraction — because **byte-exact deletion is never spent silently.** *That is the trade in one sentence: the property that makes deletion exact is the property that makes compression impossible.*

**→ CLU, said plainly.** **State it as a theorem, in our own words, before a referee states it as a hole.** *"Under a masked write with per-item atom groups, matched bytes is unreachable by construction at ≥2.20×; the exclusion is a consequence of the C3 masking that buys write locality, not a tuning failure."* ⭐ It is the strongest structural result Campaign 2 produced, it is one of **B′**'s theorems (§A11 task 5), and — unlike almost everything else in this file — **it needs no seeds.**

**Status: [proven (closed form) + verified to 1e-9 in all 28 cells, 0 violations, `matched=False` everywhere] · [a theorem about the masked write with per-item atom groups — a shared/factored substrate is the only thing that could dissolve it] · [the shell surcharge is measured and predicted, exactly $1/(\mathrm{dim}+2)$].** **Reading pointers:** §11.9 (the write operator and C3 locality — the property this floor is the price of), §11.15 Record 4 (the parking rule / deletion exactness this trades against), registry **N134 / N159 / N161**, charter §A2.3 and §A9.9.

## 11.19 Three pictures from C2W3: **the ruler that measures the task**, **six numbers that were the whole trajectory**, and **the stranger's pull** **[plain-language records — new C2W3, filed 2026-07-31]**

**Why this section exists.** C2W3 asked two questions the program had been circling for a year — *is our measuring instrument measuring anything?* and *is the trajectory a storage medium?* — and answered both, one narrowly yes and one no. ⭐ **The physics worth keeping is not either verdict; it is the third result, which says exactly what a lookup table can never do and then shows that our own admission gate makes it invisible.** ⚠ **Nothing here licenses a performance claim.** ⛔⛔ **And read this first: C2W3 closed REVIEWED-BUT-INCOMPLETE — the audit paper's rival arms were released and never built.** Registry **N167–N190**; ledger ⟲ C2W3.

### Record 14 — **The ruler that measures the task** (when a benchmark grades your construction back at you)

> **The ruler that measures the task.** You build four little memory tasks to test a memory. To make sure the test is fair you also run a *cheat*: a reader that gets **the same stored bytes** but is allowed to do something stupidly simple with them — return the nearest row, echo the question back, read the rows in the order they were written — and costs **four extra bytes, total**. If the cheat scores the maximum the metric allows, then the task never had room for a memory to be *good* at it; ⭐ **you have not measured your memory, you have measured your own construction.** So: run it. On **three of four** families something costing **≤4 B sits at the metric's exact maximum** — and **it is never the CLU**. On the hardest family the CLU itself reads **0.9722** while *three different* readers of a table costing **1/478th of its bytes** read **1.0000**. ⭐ **But "everything is at ceiling" is FALSE**, and the one survivor is the interesting part: it survives for a reason you can *build to*. Its answer is a **blend** of stored values, and the construction **throws the blend away if it happens to land on a stored value**. So the answer is *provably not in the table.* ⭐⭐ **That property — "the answer is provably not in the table" — is the only thing that has survived a +0 B audit in two waves.**

**The same thing in symbols** (`bprime-fb4-gate` D0; the rule and its constants were **pre-registered and frozen in code before the first measured run**). With a family's metric maximum $M$, a blank-store control, the best $+0\,$B substitute and a full-attention table reader,
$$S(f) \;\equiv\; \frac{\mathrm{sub}(f) - \mathrm{blank}(f)}{M - \mathrm{blank}(f)}, \qquad f \ \text{SATURATED} \iff S(f) \ge 0.95 \ \wedge\ \mathrm{sub} \ge \mathrm{attn} - 2\,\mathrm{SE}.$$

| family (anchor) | metric | blank | **sub** (which) | attn | **$S(f)$** | SE | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `overload` (478.2×) | decode | 0.1667 | **1.0000** (`settle_deleted`) | 1.0000 | **1.0000** | 0.0000 | ⛔ SATURATED |
| **`aggregate`** (54.56×) | neg-MAE | −0.4221 | **−0.2081** (2-NN mean) | −0.2493 | **0.5068** | 0.0171 | ✅ **SURVIVES** |
| `recency` (54.56×) | acc | 0.5463 | **1.0000** (row-order reader) | 0.4755 | **1.0000** | 0.0221 | ⛔ SATURATED |
| `manifold` (52.0×) | $R^2$ | −0.0001 | **1.0000** (echo) | 0.0000 | **1.0000** | 0.0000 | ⛔ SATURATED |

**→ CLU, said plainly.** ⭐ **The gate did not fire, so the protocol is not invalid and the audit paper is licensed** — on a thin base, honestly labelled. **Four design rules follow, and they are now a standing construction standard:** the answer must **not** be recoverable from the table's row order (this kills `recency`); must **not** be the query or a function of it alone (kills `manifold`); the store's operating point must **not** be one where the arg-min table is already at the metric's maximum (kills `overload`'s anchor); and ⭐ **the target should be constructed to be absent from the table**, which subsumes the other three.

⚠ **Two things must travel with this record, always.** **(i) The engineer's unresolved objection:** ⛔ **the rule cannot distinguish *"the family is substitutable"* from *"the family's ANCHOR is substitutable."*** `overload` saturates only at the anchor chosen *because the base budget is unusable* — and the rule would return the same verdict even if the family were fine. **(ii) The ancestry, conceded before a referee finds it:** this is the **partial-input-baseline** tradition (Poliak et al. \*SEM 2018; Feng, Wallace & Boyd-Graber ACL 2019), applied to a store's own bytes at a state-byte convention. ⛔ **And Feng et al.'s converse caveat is binding on us: a substitute audit that a memory PASSES does not show the memory is doing real work.** ⭐ *A conceded ancestor is worth more than a contested monopoly.*

⭐ One clean sub-result worth remembering: **a global softmax with one temperature is not uniformly the strongest table reader.** It ties the arg-min on `overload`, is **beaten by a hard 2-NN mean** on `aggregate` (−0.2493 vs −0.2081), and is **structurally incapable** on `recency` (no time column — a positive scalar cannot reorder two logits) and `manifold` (the spectator column is written zero, so any convex combination is **exactly 0.0000**). ⚠ **This attention arm is a TABLE READER at +4 B; it is NOT `AttentionPsi`** and inherits none of that quarantine.

**Status: [pre-registered, frozen in code, hit exactly — 10 confirmed (3 sharper than predicted) · 2 partial · 0 wrong-direction; $S$(aggregate) predicted to three decimals] · [3 seeds, 12 cells, bit-fidelity to the on-disk C2W1 artefact verified per seed] · [⛔ NO cell here is a byte-matched dividend — minimum ratio measured anywhere is 17.11×] · [⛔ the `recency` family is STRUCK as protocol-invalid; neither its pre- nor post-fix number is a dividend null].** **Reading pointers:** §11.13 (test-time compute and its three controls), §11.11 (the φ-laundering precedent this generalises), registry **N170 / N171 / N172 / N188**, charter §A3.7 and §A14.2/§A14.8.

### Record 15 — **Six numbers that were the whole trajectory** (a memory that works perfectly is a memory a table can copy)

> **Six numbers that were the whole trajectory.** The hope was simple and it was good: don't read the marble only where it *stops* — read it at six points **along the way**, and store six different things in one memory. Measure whether those waypoints carry the store, and they do: at the **very first integrator step** the waypoint already knows more about the answer than a store-deleted control does. So far so good. ⭐ **Then run the kill-condition, which was deliberately built first: for each waypoint, write down each item's *average* value there. That is six numbers per waypoint — a lookup table 478× cheaper than the store. Does it reproduce the read?** It reproduces **37 of 38** waypoints, **all 18** of the ones that passed the unlock bar, and the trajectory beats it by a meaningful margin at **exactly zero** of them. ⭐⭐ **And the reason is not that the dynamics are broken — it is that they are working.** The flow squeezes every noisy launch of the same item together (a **4.6×** contraction) while pushing *different* items apart (**+15 %**). A map that contracts within an item and separates across items sends **every query of an item to one value per waypoint** — and *one value per waypoint per item* is precisely what six rows are. **The better the memory got at its job, the more exactly a table could copy it.**

**The same thing in symbols** (`route3-stage1-plus-2x2` D1–D3; $D = |\text{Spearman }\rho|$, zero fitted parameters, identical on all arms; 3 seeds; `overload` at the 478× anchor). Let $\delta$ be a launch perturbation within one item's query cloud and $s_t$ the slot content:
$$\underbrace{\frac{\lVert \Delta s_t\rVert}{\lVert\delta\rVert} \;\to\; 0.218}_{\text{contractive WITHIN an item}} \qquad\wedge\qquad \underbrace{\frac{\text{between-item spread}}{\text{launch spread}} \;\to\; 1.153}_{\text{separated ACROSS items}} \qquad\Longrightarrow\qquad \mathrm{Fisher} \;=\; \frac{\text{between}}{\text{within}}\;:\; 6.3 \;\longrightarrow\; \mathbf{1.47\times10^{7}}.$$

| the clause | the evidence | verdict |
|---|---|---|
| ✅ *"the store acts from the first step"* | at $t = 0.05$ — **one** integrator step — $D = \mathbf{0.559}$ vs a store-deleted launder **0.268** and a launch-noise floor **0.154** | ✅ **charter §A8.1's core CONFIRMED, and now measurable at one step** |
| ⭐ *"and the unlock bar clears"* | **18** clearing family×channel×slot rows, both channels, 3/3 seeds; smallest margin **+0.1338 ± 0.0650** (lower-2SE **+0.0039**), largest **+0.6544 ± 0.1237** | ⭐ `unlock = true` |
| ⛔⛔ *"and six time-indexed numbers reproduce it"* | **K = 6** rows/slot, nearest-key, leave-one-query-out, **478× cheaper**: reproduces **19/19 q-slots**, **18/19 p-slots** = **37/38**, **18/18 clearing slots**; **read beats table by >0.10 at ZERO slots**; best margin **+0.0141 / +0.0529**, worst **−0.0235 / −0.2044** | ⛔⛔ **§A9.5 FIRES AND OVERRIDES ⇒ no stage 2** |
| ⛔ *"and the q/p story was wrong"* | at the first slots q and p are **rank-identical to four decimals** (0.5590 both) — because the read launches at $p_0 = 0$ with $q_0$'s payload channels zeroed, so Verlet gives $q_1 - q_0 = dt\,p_1/m$, a **positive multiple** | ⛔ **§A8.1's split REFUTED in the answer channel, for an exact reason** |
| ⭐ *"but it is exactly right about the ADDRESS block"* | position's address block: **1.000 full vs 1.000 launder ⇒ dividend exactly 0.000 at EVERY slot** (pure query at *all* $t$, stronger than claimed); momentum's: **0.319–0.556 vs 1.000** — the store **destroys** query identity because $p \propto (c_i - q)$ collapses every query of an item onto one vector | ⚠ ✅ **declared POST-HOC secondary diagnostic — the label travels** |
| ⛔ *"and more slots cannot rescue it"* | the slot vector is the image of a $(3d{+}1)$-dim launch map; measured rank **13 = 3d+1** at $d=4$ for **every** $S \ge 2$ (slot dims 16 → 160), **8** at $S=1$, **4 = d** under the shipped read | ⛔ **a per-slot table can hold strictly MORE per-item content than the CLU can generate** |

**→ CLU, said plainly.** ⭐⭐ **State the mechanism, never just the verdict: *the kill is a consequence of the medium being in the productive regime, not of it being broken.*** A contractive, item-separated flow map is **exactly** a map whose per-slot output is a function of item identity — and $K$ rows express a function of item identity exactly. ⛔⛔ **And state the SCOPE in the same breath, or you close a direction the Head kept open: the kill is for INFERENCE-READ claims only. Route 3's *training-time* machinery — slotted/trajectory write objectives, the `allocate` verb, flow-map shaping — stays LIVE as tier-ii organizer tooling, because reduction to a table AFTER training is a computational win, not a defeat.** ⭐ **And the shape of any survivor is now fixed by arithmetic: per-item is closed, so a Route-3 claim can only ever be CROSS-ITEM.**

⭐⭐ **The process lesson is the wave's best return on judgement, and it is now program doctrine: BUILD THE KILL-CONDITION BEFORE THE THING IT CAN KILL.** §A9.5 was a stage-2 obligation; a Hub scoping decision moved it into stage 1; **it fired there, for hours of measurement instead of a stage-2 build.** ⚠ **Its counter-example is in the same wave — the escape the kill demands (Record 16) was *also* measurable on the existing rig, and was not measured.** ⇒ *build the kill and its escape hatch together.*

⛔ **The honest cost.** **Two of three families cannot be written admissibly at all** — `aggregate` and `manifold` are **0/3** after their one bounded escalation (300 → **900** write steps moved endpoint loss by **≤0.005**; losses **0.24–0.38** against a **0.05** tolerance) — independently reproducing C2W2's finding that **the plateau is the atom budget's expressivity floor, not an optimisation artefact.** ⭐ **Every verdict in this record rests on ONE family.**

**Status: [pre-registered; 8 substantive predictions → 4 survive · 3 fail · 1 partial, and P5 (the kill) survived and is the decisive result] · [3 seeds everywhere, no exceptions; both bit-identity gates PASS, incl. r=0 re-run with the path term ON] · [⛔ the kill is scoped to inference-read claims (charter §A14.1)] · [⚠ the address-block result is post-hoc and did NOT enter the bar] · [⚠ the unlock's small-$t$ end has lower-2SE bounds of +0.004…+0.099 at n=3 — the robust clears are just "the read works"].** **Reading pointers:** §11.4 (the gradient dichotomy), §11.6 (capacity regimes), Record 10 and its C2W3 update ($e^{-C}$), Record 16 (the one escape), registry **N173 / N174 / N175 / N186 / N189**, charter §A8.1/§A8.2/§A9.4/§A9.5/§A14.1.

### Record 16 — **The stranger's pull** (the one thing a lookup table can never do — and the gate that hides it)

> **The stranger's pull.** A lookup table answers by **choosing a row** and reading it. So ask it a question, then quietly **delete a row it did not choose**. Its answer changes by **exactly zero** — not approximately, not usually: exactly, by construction, because it never looked. Now ask the CLU. Its marble rolls in a landscape whose slope is the **sum of every well in the store**, including the ones the query never selected, so deleting a stranger's well **moves the path**. ⭐⭐ **That is the whole difference between a table and a field, stated in one experiment, and it is the only inter-slot coupling anyone has proved a per-slot table cannot express.** ⛔ **And then the deflating half. How big is the stranger's pull? It falls off like a Gaussian in the distance between wells — and our admission gate is designed to keep wells *far apart*, precisely so items do not interfere. At the gate we designed, the pull is 7 parts in ten thousand: below every noise floor the harness has. At the rig we have actually been running — which quietly overrode that gate — it is 0.69 to 0.80: enormous.** A **1089× span**, set by a single configuration flag. ⭐ **We have been sitting on the measurement that could satisfy our own kill-condition, at zero build cost, on the rig already running, for an entire wave.**

**The same thing in symbols** (`bprime-theory` T5.4/T5.5, `t5b_exchange.py`; well width $s$, inter-well distance $d$). For a per-slot table with query-dependent row selection $i(x)$:
$$\frac{\partial \hat y}{\partial(\text{any non-selected row})} \;=\; 0 \quad\textbf{exactly}, \qquad\text{while}\qquad \Delta q(t) \;\approx\; \frac{t^2}{2M}\,\lVert \nabla V_j(q_t)\rVert \;\neq\; 0,$$
because $\nabla V$ sums over **all** wells. The magnitude is Gaussian-suppressed:
$$\frac{\lVert\nabla V_j\rVert}{\lVert\nabla V_{\rm own}\rVert} \;\sim\; \exp\!\Big(-\tfrac12 (d/s)^2\Big).$$

| $d/s$ | **1.9–2.0** | 2.5 | **2.9** | 3.0 | 3.5 | 4.0 | **4.4** | 5.0 | 6.0 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| coupling / own gradient | **0.80 / 0.69** | 0.281 | **0.111** | 0.085 | 0.020 | 3.4e-3 | **7.0e-4** | 4.8e-5 | 2.3e-7 |
| | ⭐ **the rig we actually run** (`d_safe_override = 0.58`) — also the merger band | | **soft-certificate region** | | | | **the DESIGNED gate** ($d_{\rm safe} = 4.4\,s$) | | |

**→ CLU, said plainly.** ⭐ **Two design-deciding sentences.** **(1)** Under the **designed** admission gate the only coupling a per-slot table cannot express sits at the $7\times10^{-4}$ level ⇒ ⛔ **§A9.5's kill-condition is satisfiable only inside the soft certificate.** *That is a narrowing, not a kill* — and it was far cheaper to learn from a derivation than from a build. **(2)** ⛔ **Any future design that RESTORES the designed gate to buy conditioning is buying away its own headline claim, and must say so.** The span from the designed gate to the soft-certificate region is **157× (2.20 decades)** of coupling, at a measured price of $\lambda_{\min} \div 2.2\text{–}6.0$.

⭐ **Where this lands in the program.** It is filed as **B′ protocol evidence**, not as a revival: *"here is what a table structurally cannot do, measured"* is an **audit-paper column**. The probe itself is cheap — delete a **non-selected** item, read $\Delta$ per slot, plot vs $t$ and vs $d/s$ — and it doubles as the **entry gate for any cross-item successor.**

⚠ **The caveats are load-bearing and the theorist published them himself.** ⛔ **The prefactor was REFUTED as registered**: $|\Delta q(t)|$ matches the ballistic prediction only to **0.61–0.73× at $t=10$** and **0.17–0.34× at $t=20$** (the residual is free-fall toward the item's own well) — ⭐ **the *scaling* holds (2.60 decades over $r/s = 2\to4$, inside the registered 2–4), the prefactor does not.** ⚠ It is a **two-well toy at $p_0 = 0$**; with a live particle head a learned $p_0$ could **steer** the path toward strangers and beat the suppression — **untested, and it is the single pre-registered revival trigger for inference-read claims.** ⛔ **And the caveat that gates everything: naming $s$ for a *learned* multi-atom well is an UNSOLVED modelling question.** The figure $d/s \approx 1.9\text{–}2.0$ uses `atom_init_width` as a proxy for the fitted well width — ⭐ **it is a bracket, not a measurement** — and **none** of $s/\mathrm{sep}$, $d/s$, $s_{\max}/\sigma_q$ has ever been measured on the shipped learned $V_\theta$. ⛔ **Finally: the coupling list is NOT proven exhaustive.** We proved one coupling is inexpressible and that richer per-item slot content is *not* a route; we did **not** prove nothing else qualifies.

**Status: [proven (the row-selection argument: a table's derivative w.r.t. a non-selected row is exactly 0) + measured on a toy (the $\exp(-\frac12(d/s)^2)$ curve, 1089× span, 2.60-decade falloff)] · [⛔ prefactor REFUTED as registered — scaling confirmed, free-fall correction missing] · [⚠ two-well toy at $p_0=0$; $s$ for a learned well is UNSOLVED and gates every transfer; the coupling list is NOT exhaustive] · [⭐ measurable TODAY on the existing rig at zero build cost — tasked as `bprime-c6`].** **Nomenclature (Def-2):** $M$ here is **inertial mass** throughout (it appears only in $t^2/2M$); **spectral $\mu$ does not enter this record.** **Reading pointers:** §1.3 (the learned potential as a sum over wells — the reason $\nabla V$ is non-local), §11.3 (Prop 2, hard read isolation — ⭐ *note the tension worth savouring: isolation is the property we sell, and this coupling is the property we would need; the admission gate is the dial between them*), Record 15 (the kill this escapes), registry **N180 / N181**, charter §A9.5/§A14.1.

> ### ⚠⚠ **Update (C2W4, 2026-08-01) — IT WAS MEASURED, THE LAW HELD, AND TWO OF THE NUMBERS ABOVE ARE WRONG**
> ⛔⛔ **Superseded, and this is the one to remember: "0.69–0.80 at the rig we actually run" and "a 1089× span."** Both came from reading `d_safe_override = 0.58` as the spacing the store *achieves*. **It is not a spacing — it is an admission gate, the radius at which a write is REFUSED.** Measured on the shipped cell (`bprime-c6` §1, 3 seeds): the **achieved separation is `sep = 1.3459`**, so the rig sits at
> $$d/s \;=\; \mathbf{4.34}\ \text{(atom-width ruler)}\quad/\quad 3.72\ \text{(fitted-width ruler)},\qquad \text{coupling}\;=\;\mathbf{1.53\times10^{-2}}\;\pm\,0.006 .$$
> ⭐⭐ **We have been INSIDE the designed-gate regime all along.** ⛔ **The span retires with it:** across the whole admissibly-writable sweep ($d/s = 1.77 \to 5.32$) the measured coupling spans $10^{2.72} = \mathbf{525\times}$, and from where we run to the designed gate is **≈10×**. ⚠⚠ **Direction, stated so it is not misread: this makes Record 15's kill and its inference-read-only scope STRONGER, not weaker.**
> ⭐⭐ **What HELD — and it handed back a quantity this primer has been calling unmeasurable.** On the learned multi-atom store, $\ln\kappa - \ln(d/\sigma_q)$ is linear in $d^2$ with slope $-1/2s^2$: **static ∇V ratio → $s = 0.3979$ ($R^2 = 0.9953$)**, **dynamical slot coupling at one step → $s = 0.3981$**, and **`CluSystem.well_fits()` independently → $s = 0.4006$** — ⭐ **three estimates inside 0.7 %.** ⇒ **the exponent transfers exactly; the prefactor is 0.379 and is UNEXPLAINED; and `atom_init_width = 0.30` is a 1.33×-low proxy that overstates the law's decades by 1.74× because the exponent is $d^2/2s^2$.** ⛔ **Every $d/s$ statement must NAME ITS RULER** — and ⚠ **the fitted $s$ is not even a constant of the architecture: it drifts 0.482 → 0.362 across the sweep, so $d/s$ is a ruler that moves under the thing it measures.**
> ⚖ **The falsifier's verdict is SPLIT, and both halves are the record.** Against a **matched blank-store delete control** the coupling **is** measurable at the shipped rig (**8/12 q-slots, 11/12 p-slots** clear by 2 SE on 3 seeds; **12/12 both channels** at $d/s = 1.77$). Against the **launch-noise floor** it is **not** (**0/12**, two orders below). ⇒ ⭐ **the sentence of record: at the weight class we run, the escape a table cannot express EXISTS, is REAL, is the STORE'S — and is two orders below the query's own launch noise, i.e. unusable as a read-out.** ⛔ **The table's own Δ is `0.0` at every slot × every dropped row × every cell — float equality, not a tolerance.**
> ⛔ **And the cost, where it hurts:** the coupling is $O(1)$ **only where the store cannot be reliably written** — at $d/s = 1.77$ the write is admissible in **3 of 6 seeds** ($\lambda_{\min} < 0$: merged wells) — ⭐ ***the regime in which a per-slot table is structurally inadequate is the regime in which our own admission machinery says the store is unsafe.*** **That is a design identity, not a defect of this implementation.**
